# spec-to-code map

> Maps spec clauses to the code coordinates that ground them, so the next
> session opens a coordinate instead of re-searching — locating the code is the
> expensive half, and this file exists so it is paid once. Written and checked
> by machines (`evidence-check`); committed so it follows worktrees and other
> machines.
>
> This is SpecSeal's ledger for SpecSeal, and it is the gathered half of one.
> **A work item writes `seal/ledger/<work-item-id>.md` instead of appending
> here** — two branches never queue at one file, because no two work items
> share an id. The checker reads both addresses. At each release,
> `.github/scripts/fold_ledger.py` moves every fragment into this file under a
> `## X.Y.Z — <date>` heading, one `###` section per work item marked with
> `<!-- specs/<work-item-id> -->` (the marker's text names the work item by its
> sub-directory and did not change when the root did), and removes the
> fragment. So this file grows once per release, by the rows that release's
> work items wrote, and the areas above those sections are the rows from
> before the fragments existed.

## Coordinates

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Coordinate notation | `path#anchor@hash` from the repo root |
| Trust exceptions | none |

A coordinate names **content, never a position**. The anchor is a symbol name
where the language has one — `.py` is read with Python's own parser — and a
distinctive line of text otherwise; the hash covers the region under it. A row
carries no line number and no commit SHA, so nothing in it goes stale for a
reason unrelated to the claim, and the check calls git for nothing — the one
exception is `--migrate`, which consults the old stamp's commit before it
trusts a line number it is rewriting.

The **Checked** column carries the date somebody read the code. Re-verifying a
row is re-reading it and running `evidence-check --reverify`, which recomputes
the hash and names what it changed.

**A row whose anchor a change removes is removed with it.** Its claim went
with the code; the new claim is a new row, in the work item's own fragment.
So a claim leaves this file when the code it was about does, and the fold at
the next release brings the new claim back in with the rest of its fragment.

## Scope decisions

Judgments that don't follow from code or documents alone.

| Decision | Content | Grounds |
|---|---|---|
| The ledger opens at one commit | Every row below is stamped at this tree's first commit rather than left to the header alone | A row with no stamp of its own measures from the header, and a header nobody re-reads re-dates every claim at once. Stamping them all at the baseline costs nothing today and makes the first genuine re-verification visible |

| Only a segment whose command word is `git` with the `commit` subcommand is counted, so a commit sitting inside a string literal is not | `hooks/commit-review-gate.py#_hides_a_commit@2eab9355`, assembled in `commit_invocations` at `hooks/commit-review-gate.py#commit_invocations@bd3f5b98` | **Executed**, round 1's probe. It is NOT "the fixtures are shell command strings, so the reader finds a commit in them", and it is NOT "the documents are what trip it": issue #34's eight-line PARTIAL patch of a fixture trips as well. | A segment the reader cannot expand counts as a commit, so a body with no `git` in it at all can stop the session | `hooks/commit-review-gate.py#_hides_a_commit@2eab9355`, resolved by `_eval_hides_a_commit` at `hooks/commit-review-gate.py#_eval_hides_a_commit@11406469`, against `EXPANDS` at `` hooks/cmdline.py#"EXPANDS = "$`*?[]{}""@6d56a043 `` | **Executed**, four bodies: `eval "$CMD"` TRIPS · `eval $(cat f)` TRIPS · `eval "echo hello"` clean (no expansion character) · `print('hello')` clean | 2026-08-31 | Fail-closed by design — the docstring says nothing can tell *reduces to a commit* from *reduces to something else* without running the shell. Removing the apostrophe restored `clean`. Round 2 confirmed the parity at the same coordinate: 0 → clean · 1 → TRIPS · 2 → clean · 3 → TRIPS | 2026-08-31 | **What is parity-sensitive is the apostrophes ADDED above the waiver row, not the file's absolute count** — measured after round 2's edit, `agents/warden.md` holds 11 apostrophes above that row, an odd number, and the file is still clean. Backticks and double quotes interact, so only the whole-file verdict answers this; a raw count does not. Measure above the waiver row, never below, because quote state flows forward. Pinned by `tests/test_edits_go_through_the_edit_tool.py::test_the_warden_file_does_not_itself_trip_the_commit_gate`. `agents/smith.md` is deliberately not pinned — it has tripped at its own waiver example since before this work |

Review round 1 established three more parser facts (executed there, read here): a result row never creates a turn even when its uuid differs from its call's; an unresolved tool_use joins the turn count but not the call count and drags the ratio down — 0.5 on a synthetic, negligible at a 65-call scale; non-monotonic timestamps split one turn into two gap groups and overstate model time, which an append-only transcript does not produce. **Re-read 2026-09-05 in work item 1788613827's round 1 fix pass**, which put the type check under the three-step fallback: an id that is not a string is skipped for the next candidate, where it used to go straight into a `set` and raise `TypeError: unhashable type`. ## 0.4.0 — 2026-09-02

<!-- specs/1788229400-every-branch-appends-to-the-same-two-files -->
### 1788229400-every-branch-appends-to-the-same-two-files

Rows for the work item that closed #46 and #52.

A coordinate here names content, never a position: a symbol name where the
language has one, a distinctive line of text otherwise, and a hash of the
region under it. No row carries a line number or a commit, so nothing in one
goes stale for a reason unrelated to the claim it makes.

#### A coordinate names content, not a position

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|

### 1788277657-one-bar-misreads-two-of-the-three-segment-kinds

Rows for the work item that wrote the per-segment bars and the resume rule
(issue #51, observations 1 and 2).

The resume rule's row is no longer here. #265 moved the section it was
anchored on, §*Orchestrator: a fix pass resumes the implementer*, out of the
review skill and into `skills/code-review/orchestration.md`, so the anchor
was removed rather than re-pointed and the claim was re-stated in that work
item's own fragment. The old path is deliberately not named beside the
heading here: a reference pairing the two is what the split had to correct in
five live files, and a note about the move should not read as a sixth.

#### One bar no longer judges all three segment kinds

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The bars are written beside the meter they interpret, one per segment kind, and they refuse nothing — a reviewing segment at tools per turn ≥ 1.8, an implementing segment on `repeats = 0` and calls per deliverable, a verifying segment exempt | `docs/review-handoff-protocol.md#"### After the run — the per-segment bars"@6b8c8a9e` | **Executed** — pinned by `tests/test_the_handoff_before_round_one.py::test_the_protocol_names_a_bar_per_segment_kind` and the draft-number pin (`::test_the_title_and_the_status_section_agree_on_the_draft`), the latter planted first and seen red against the 0.7 Status line before round 1's 🔴 1 fix landed; the cross-instrument pin (`::test_the_advisory_and_the_tying_paragraph_name_one_value`) reads the threshold out of `session_cost.py` and the tying sentence out of the protocol, so either moving alone turns it red | 2026-09-02 | The small-round nuance is in the section: a 23-call round read 1.64 with few independent batches to rise on, so the bar reads rounds of ordinary size. The advisory (1.2, in `session_cost.py`) and the bar (1.8) are tied by the section's closing paragraph — the script cannot tell segment kinds apart, the orchestrator can. **Re-read 2026-09-05 in work item 1788613827's phase 4**, after phase 3 added the run-level table's two paragraphs to this section. The three bars, their thresholds and the tying paragraph are untouched, and so is the `1.2` in `session_cost.py` that `test_the_advisory_and_the_tying_paragraph_name_one_value` reads out by regex — the new paragraphs say what the table judges and point at its owner rather than restating a bar. **Re-read 2026-09-05 in the same work item's round 1 fix pass**, which took the five glossed row names out of that paragraph after round 1's finding 8. The three bars, their thresholds, the small-round nuance and the tying paragraph are untouched, and the case that reads the `1.2` out of `session_cost.py` is green |

### 1788302682-the-release-check-never-watched-bin

Rows for the work item that put `bin/` under the release check's eye and
pinned the list of roots it watches (issue #10).

#### The release check watches every root that ships

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |

| Only a segment the reader understands binds a name, a prefix assignment does not — `A=1 true` is that command's environment, not the shell's — and an array assignment `A=(x)` empties the name rather than binding `(x)` | `hooks/cmdline.py#_bind@71c43e63`, `tests/test_a_path_the_command_wrote_out.py#test_an_assignment_in_front_of_a_command_is_that_commands_environment@debc83c6`, `tests/test_a_path_the_command_wrote_out.py#test_an_array_assignment_empties_the_name@573b1c1c` | **Executed** 2026-09-02; bash 3.2.57 prints an empty line for `A=1 true; echo "$A"`; round 1 measured `SB=(/three); git -C "$SB"` composing `(/three)` onto the session directory before the array rule | 2026-09-02 | |

| A statement inside a compound command's body is forgotten rather than bound; the body is an entry on a stack, closed only by its own closer in command position, and `)` closes only a `(` | `hooks/cmdline.py#_nesting@0472ff22`, `hooks/cmdline.py#_leads@aebb2626`, `hooks/cmdline.py#CLOSES@1c9663a2`, `hooks/cmdline.py#walk_directories>"env = _bind(env, tokens) if not stack else _forget(env, tokens)"@bbd066e1`, `tests/test_a_path_the_command_wrote_out.py#test_a_bodys_later_statement_is_not_a_top_level_one@938252f5`, `tests/test_a_path_the_command_wrote_out.py#test_the_body_ends_at_its_closer@b3f334ba`, `tests/test_a_path_the_command_wrote_out.py#test_what_opens_a_body_and_what_closes_one@90c2c062` | **Executed** 2026-09-02: round 0's differential of the wide reset against the aimed one over 1,790 inputs found 82 fail-opens; round 1 found two more the integer count let through (a multi-line `case` arm pattern `a )`, the glued `f(){`); after the stack, 2,109 inputs against bash 3.2.57 — 0 fail-open, 0 resolutions lost. 7 mutations of the count and 4 of the stack each red | 2026-09-02 | After the stack two shapes still answer where bash disagrees: a quoted closer written ALONE as a command inside a body that does not run (`if false; then "fi"; SB=/three; fi`), and a subshell whose first statement is a subshell (`( ( echo hi ); SB=/three; true )`) — round 3 found the second and the repository owner recorded it as issue #72 rather than fixing it here. | The pipeline branch forgets, and `lastpipe` is why: which stage runs in the current shell depends on a shell option this process cannot see | `hooks/cmdline.py#walk_directories>"elif known:"@040ed27a`, `tests/test_a_path_the_command_wrote_out.py#test_the_pipeline_branch_forgets_and_lastpipe_is_why@29011d13` | **Executed** 2026-09-02; turning the branch into `pass` turns the test red, where round 2 of the original item found nothing reaching it | 2026-09-02 | |
| Whatever the reader answers about a name, bash answers the same — a prompt is exempt, an answer is not | `tests/test_the_reader_agrees_with_bash.py#test_whatever_the_reader_answers_bash_answers_too@7a3421a8`, `tests/test_the_reader_agrees_with_bash.py#WRAPPERS@021beee1` | **Executed** 2026-09-02 under bash 3.2.57, one bash process for the whole corpus; skipped where `usable_bash()` is false | 2026-09-02 | Round 1 🟡 13: the round-0 differential lived in a scratchpad and every wrapper in it was one line, which is how 🔴 2 and 🔴 3 escaped it |

The catch is still `Exception` and the mark is still written before the group decides, so the claim holds. **That stamp names no commit any more** — #239 moved every rider to `Verified <date> against <anchor>@<hash>`, which is why this row's hash moved again without its claim moving; the sentence above is what the 2026-09-05 re-read found, kept because it is the incident #239 removes |

<!-- specs/1788360817-the-pull-request-language-is-fixed-inside-a-skill -->
### 1788360817-the-pull-request-language-is-fixed-inside-a-skill

<!-- This work item's ledger rows: one file per work item so that two branches
never queue at seal/ledger.md, folded into it at the release.

Every row here is over PROSE, and that is the work item rather than a gap in
it. Nothing in `hooks/` reads `seal/config.md`: judging what language a
commit message is in means being wrong about names, identifiers and quoted
English, and a wrong stop blocks a correct commit (`questions.md` Q3). So
each row's code grounds are the document that carries the rule and the case
that pins it, and "Executed" means the case was run, not that a session was
observed obeying the rule. The one thing no case here can reach is named in
`overview.md` §Not verified. -->

###### The row, and where a session reads it from

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 · the skill names both the file and the row, so a session knows where to look and what to look for | `skills/commit-pr-convention/SKILL.md#"## The language is the repository's, and it says so in a file"@a76da269`, `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py#test_the_skill_names_the_file_and_the_row@cbca742e` | **Executed**: 24 passed in the new file at `56ec644`. Both halves are asserted separately — a path with no row name and a row name with no path each leave a session unable to act | 2026-09-03 | The row is the only thing in `config.md` today; the table is open for more items later |
| S2 / 🟡 3 · every way of NOT naming a language lands on English, and there are FOUR: no file, no such row, an empty value, and a file that cannot be read or does not parse as that table | `skills/commit-pr-convention/SKILL.md#"## The language is the repository's, and it says so in a file"@a76da269`, `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py#configured_language@4e389a05`, `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py#test_every_way_of_not_naming_a_language_lands_on_english@b00fbf02`, `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py#test_an_unreadable_config_lands_on_english_too@ea7e7742`, `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py#test_the_skill_names_every_way_of_not_naming_one@73ae726b`, `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py#test_the_default_is_stated_where_the_path_is@8fc71b49` | **Executed**: the rule is now RUN, not only asserted in prose — a reader built to the skill's sentence answers `English` for every shape, the unreadable one built as a `config.md` that is a directory (`IsADirectoryError`, an `OSError`, the class chosen so a permission error lands the same way). 46 passed in the file | 2026-09-03 | Round 1 🟡 3: the first draft named two absences and stopped, so a session meeting an empty value could stop, ask, or guess. Round 2 ❓ 8 settled the COUNT at four — "cannot be read or does not parse" is one way with two spellings — and `UNNAMED` keeps five ids for it, which its comment now says. The old row cited `test_absence_means_english_in_both_of_its_spellings`, which round 1 split |
| S3 · the root is resolved `<repo>/seal/` then `<git-common-dir>/seal/`, never spelled as the first alone | `skills/commit-pr-convention/SKILL.md#"## The language is the repository's, and it says so in a file"@a76da269`, `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py#test_the_root_is_resolved_rather_than_spelled@6181a8fe` | **Executed**: both `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` and `<repo>/seal/` are asserted present, so neither place can be dropped alone. **Read**: `hooks/optin.py#home_at` is the code that does this for the hooks; no hook reads `config.md`, so the skill's sentence is the whole mechanism here | 2026-09-03 | Spelling `<repo>/seal/` and stopping is what left a local-mode ledger unread at every commit in #80 — the same mistake, one release earlier |

### 1788398967-local-modes-records-never-leave-the-clone

<!-- This work item's ledger rows: one file per work item so that two branches
never queue at seal/ledger.md, folded into it at the release.

Every "Executed" here names a run whose output was read. Two rows say
"Executed" about a claim that was FALSE when it was written and is corrected
in place — the `extractall` pair — because the measurement is the row's whole
value: a later session reading only the corrected sentence would not know the
claim had ever been the other way, and would be tempted to widen it back. -->

<!-- Four rows OUTSIDE this fragment drifted on this branch and were
re-verified rather than re-pointed, because every one of their claims still
holds. Three cite `hooks/optin.py#home_at` — two in `seal/ledger.md` and one
in #80's fragment — which this work rewrote without changing what it answers.
Two cite the whole-document anchor on `templates/seal-README.md`, which this
work added a paragraph to.

The second group is #80's own 🟡 4 — *a whole-document anchor makes any edit
to that document another work item's drift, which a fragment-scoped check
cannot see* — and it has now demonstrated itself. Nothing here acts on it; it
is recorded so the next reader knows that row describes something observed
twice rather than once. -->

#### The root's two places

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S16 · both places `seal/` is read from have one address, so a command that must NAME the mode it is not in does not spell either path | `hooks/optin.py#home_paths@5e10ddea`, `tests/test_optin_home.py#test_home_paths_names_the_two_places_shared_first@539620e0` | **Executed**: 42 passed in `tests/test_optin_home.py` at `f2fdcbe`, the existing 38 unchanged. Seen red first: the same call against `git show HEAD:hooks/optin.py` raised `AttributeError` | 2026-09-03 | `home_at` answers *where the root is*; `--into shared\|local` asks *where would it be*, which no existing function could answer. Spelling `<repo>/seal/` and stopping is what left a local-mode ledger unread at every commit in #80 |
| S16 · the pair costs no extra `git` call, so three gates importing this module pay nothing for it | `hooks/optin.py#home_at@1381e6ef`, `tests/test_optin_home.py#test_home_paths_costs_no_second_git_call_when_common_is_passed@28834cfa` | **Executed**: the case replaces `optin.subprocess.run` with a raiser and passes the common directory in. **Read**: the rider on `repo_root` counts five `git` calls per gated command, three of them `rev-parse --show-toplevel` | 2026-09-08 | **Re-verified 2026-09-08 in work item 1788817289's round 2 fix pass**, and widened by it: `home_at` now takes the same `common` parameter as `home_paths`, so the property this row states holds one level up as well. Measured with a logging `git` on `PATH` — a Bash call in a linked worktree with a local root fell from four `git` processes to two. The old fast path looked like it skipped `git_common_dir` when the shared root existed and did not: the throwaway opt-out needed the common directory anyway. Hoisting it is what let the two places be produced in one place |

#### What leaves the clone, and what never does

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S2 · no session state beside the root reaches the zip, and the exclusion is structural rather than a maintained list | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#root_files@f77dd55b`, `tests/test_the_records_can_be_carried_out_and_in.py#test_no_session_state_beside_the_root_is_in_the_zip@7762309b` | **Executed**: the case builds all six markers plus the worktree-choice directory and asserts none appears in the namelist | 2026-09-03 | The list in the case is a fixture, not the mechanism. The mechanism is that the export walks the root — which is why `docs/one-root-by-lifetime.md` requires the root to be its own directory |
| S3 · a symbolic link inside the root is skipped and named, which is the one way out of that structure | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#root_files@f77dd55b`, `tests/test_the_records_can_be_carried_out_and_in.py#test_a_link_out_of_the_root_is_skipped_and_named@4aa9f96b` | **Executed**: a link to `specseal-implementer` is planted, the export reports the skip, and no member of the zip holds the target's bytes | 2026-09-03 | Silently dropping a file from a copy is how a copy is found incomplete on the machine that has nothing else, so the skip is reported |
| S4 · one walk feeds both the zip's members and the digests the manifest records | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#root_files@f77dd55b`, `tests/test_the_records_can_be_carried_out_and_in.py#test_the_zips_members_are_exactly_what_the_walk_yields@8cb48441`, `tests/test_the_records_can_be_carried_out_and_in.py#test_a_link_leaves_the_digest_alone_too@32db5a8c` | **Executed**: the namelist is compared against the walk, and a link planted inside a work item leaves its digest equal | 2026-09-03 | Two enumerations drift at the seam: the manifest would record what one saw while the reminder compares against what the other sees, and the difference reads as a work item somebody changed. Pinned from both sides |

<!-- specs/1788411058-the-mode-is-two-shell-lines-in-a-readme -->
### 1788411058-the-mode-is-two-shell-lines-in-a-readme

<!-- This work item's ledger rows: one file per work item so that two branches
never queue at seal/ledger.md, folded into it at the release.

The rows split in two. The first table is about a rule that is PROSE — the
row is declared state and nothing at runtime may read it — so its code
grounds are the module that does the resolving and the case that pins the
absence. The rest are behaviour, and "Executed" there means the case was run
against the shipped code.

Two rows rest on measurements rather than on reading: what `git rm -r
--cached` does to a staged edit, and what each of the two shipped checkers
does in a repository with no `seal/`. Both were run on 2026-09-03 and both
changed what the code does. -->

###### The row is declared state, and the folder still decides

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S-row · `seal/config.md`'s `Mode` row says where the root SHOULD be; `hooks/optin.py#home_at` says where it IS, and stays the only reader of that question | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#declared@7fa4b22e`, `hooks/optin.py#home_at@1381e6ef`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_the_report_names_the_folder_the_row_and_whether_they_agree@997faec8` | **Executed**: the report names both and the cases assert what the FOLDER holds after every switch, never the row's word for it. **Read**: that no hook opens `config.md` — `grep` over `hooks/` finds no reader, and this row is what a later one would have to overturn | 2026-09-08 | **Re-verified 2026-09-08 in work item 1788817289's round 2 fix pass**, which gave `home_at` a `common` parameter and left it the only reader of where the root IS. A gate that trusted the row would open a place with no folder, and `optin` is documented to fail toward "not opted in" rather than toward a guess |
| S2 · the row has NO default. An absent one is written from where the folder is, which is the state of every repository that has a `config.md` today | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#mode_report@94f925b8`, `templates/config.md#"## Mode"@325a82df`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_an_absent_row_is_written_from_where_the_folder_is@113b9e72` | **Executed**: eight cases, four absent shapes × both modes. Running it in one mode only would pass for a fixed default of `shared`; the mutation that writes `shared` regardless reddens four of them | 2026-09-03 | The first-setup question's default (shared, offered first) is a different thing: it applies where the folder exists at NEITHER place, and it is an answer a person gives rather than a value code falls back to |
| S5 · a value that is neither mode is a claim nobody can act on — reported, fails `--check`, refuses `--apply`, and never overwritten | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#declared@7fa4b22e`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_a_value_that_is_not_a_mode_is_a_claim_nobody_can_act_on@570b5fff` | **Executed**: the report leaves the row as it found it, which is the half that matters — treating it as "absent" would write over what a person meant | 2026-09-03 | The four ways of NOT declaring (absent, empty, unparseable, no file) all pass; this fifth shape is not one of them |

###### What the switch touches, as a class

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S15 · the guard refuses what the INDEX can lose and names an untracked file as travelling. `git rm -r --cached` drops a staged edit out of the index and prints nothing | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#indexed@1ff97a21`, `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#porcelain@a9cc6d66`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_a_change_the_index_carries_refuses@7343ce5d`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_an_untracked_file_travels_with_the_folder@beeacc70` | **Executed** 2026-09-03, four tree states against `git rm -r --cached seal`: clean, a staged modification, a worktree modification, and an untracked extra. All four exit 0 and leave every file on disk; the staged one loses its index content silently. That measurement is the guard's whole grounds, and it is also why untracked does not refuse | 2026-09-03 | Refusing over an untracked file made the ordinary first run refuse: the bare command writes an absent row, and the switch met the file it had just written |
| S10 · a pure deletion is allowed once the move has already happened, and only then — the shape a stopped run or the README's by-hand `mv` leaves | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#indexed@1ff97a21`, `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#refusals@495488d6`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_a_stopped_run_is_finished_by_a_second@7bd5d983` | **Executed**: the case reproduces the by-hand state (`os.rename`, index untouched) and the second run finishes it. `MD` and `AD` still refuse | 2026-09-03 | `hooks/root-migrate.py#dirty` makes the same exception in the same words: a resume has to see past its own earlier steps |

The five paths are enumerated in `spec.md` §"What the switch touches" rather than fixed where a finding points |

### 1788445862-a-phase-hands-the-next-one-a-record

<!-- One work item's rows. No header — `fold_ledger.py` writes the `###` at
the release and moves this file into `seal/ledger.md`.

Five rows. This work item is templates, agent instructions, skill wiring and
tests — no hook, no gate, no checker — so most of what it built is already
pinned by the two new test modules and needs no coordinate of its own. What
earns a row here is what a future session could not cheaply re-derive from
those tests alone: a naming split that reads as an inconsistency until you
open the reasoning behind it (P3), and a testing pitfall this branch caught
in itself before it shipped (P5). -->

That is why the class matters rather than the coordinate: three limits, one cause, and only the third one bites the person writing the record. The real-records case is the other half — nothing had ever run these checks against `seal/specs/*/rounds/*.md`, so the first reader of a record this repository writes was the pull request it was written for. Re-read 2026-09-04 by work item 1788501054, which changed `fix_surface` and drifted this anchor: the new arm sits inside the `says_none` branch and reads no separator, so the ordering of the three limits is unchanged. **Re-read again 2026-09-04 in that item's round 3 fix pass**, which drifted this anchor a second time: nothing executable moved — the docstring gained a paragraph about the arm's key, and the `value.split(";")` that runs before the arrow and the comma is still the first thing either row meets. |

<!-- One work item's rows. No header — `fold_ledger.py` writes the `###` at

Both cases were seen red at `b1f7340` before the guard existed, and four mutations killed — the exclusion removed, the exclusion inverted, the retry removed, and the sleep removed | 2026-09-06 | Round 1's 🔴 1 and 🟡 2. The first version of this guard read the raw open-issue list, and `main` closes the current log seconds before the ladder runs — so a `gh issue list` lagging that close answers with the closed issue, the guard reads it as the create landing, and the release ends with no rolling log open and the workflow green. This module's docstring is what the guard leaned on, and it says a lag can only ever produce a reading short a result. That was written about a reading taken after a label **add**. A reading taken after a **close** is the case it does not cover, and it produces exactly the extra issue the sentence rules out. The path is not exceptional either: whenever the milestone cannot be resolved, which is the case the ladder exists for, the first rung always fails and the guard always runs. The retry's sleep is the half a later reader drops as noise — an immediate second read hits the same lagged index, and removing it left every case here green until the case pinned it. **Re-read 2026-09-06 in work item 1788661274's phase 3**, which drifted the anchor without touching the guard: that branch's phase 1 renamed `open_issue`'s version parameter and moved the title behind `log_title`, and `landed_create` — where the exclusion, the retry and the sleep all live — did not drift at all. The hash is re-stamped by hand rather than by a blanket `--reverify --ledger seal/ledger.md`, because that run cannot narrow below a file and would also re-stamp `templates/config.md`'s pre-existing drift, which no session on that branch read |

### 1788491830-a-segments-record-says-what-it-cost

<!-- One work item's rows. No header — `fold_ledger.py` writes the `###` at
the release and moves this file into `seal/ledger.md`.

Four rows, and each is a narrow reading a later tidy-up would widen in good
faith: a branch order that looks arbitrary (R1), a regex whose whitespace
looks like slack (R2), two needles in one parametrised list that look
inconsistent with each other (R3), and two templates saying one rule at two
lengths where the shorter one reads better and is wrong (R4).

R2 and R3 came out of the mutation loop rather than out of reading the code,
and both were SURVIVORS — the case existed, looked like it pinned the thing,
and stayed green against the mutation of the thing it named. That is the class
this work item produced twice, and the rows are here so the next reader meets
it before the loop does. -->

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 · `runner_problem` tries the `unknown` branch **before** the `on` split, and that order settles what a cell is read AS and never whether it is accepted | `skills/code-review/scripts/chain_check.py#runner_problem@44124ee0`, `tests/test_a_record_says_what_ran_it.py#test_the_arm_order_changes_the_reading_and_never_the_verdict@e8b243f6` | **Executed** 2026-09-04 at `6bd730c`: the cited case now BUILDS the other arm order from the module's constants and compares the two on the verdict across 756 constructed cells, zero disagreements. Verified three ways — green unmutated, green with the real arms reversed (which is the claim, not a hole in it), and red naming the cell when an arm's separator set is changed so the orders come apart | 2026-09-04 | Round 1 wrote this row after disproving the load-bearing claim, and cited a case that did not run it — round 2's 🟡 6. That case asserted three values each asserted verbatim elsewhere plus `ON_RE.search("unknown") is None`, which cannot fail, since `unknown` holds no `on` substring at all. Reversing the real arms left it green. **A ledger row pointing at a case that does not execute its claim is the mistake this row's own Notes named one sentence later**, which is why the row is worth keeping rather than deleting: the claim is right, the grounds were a story, and the gap between those two is what a reader has to be able to see. What the order buys is the READING a message quotes back — `unknown — the model was not recorded on this run` is an unknown with a reason here and a pair whose model is `this run` under the other order, and both pass. A later reader may reverse it without breaking a verdict; what they would break is the sentence the refusal prints |
| R2 · `ON_RE` requires whitespace on **both** sides, and the word that pins it has to split into two NON-empty halves — `monitor`, not `carbon` | `skills/code-review/scripts/chain_check.py#ON_RE@17c6f7c5`, `tests/test_a_record_says_what_ran_it.py#test_the_on_must_stand_alone@a76f5757` | **Executed** 2026-09-04 at `2a36737`: the pattern mutated to a bare `on` and the case exits 1 on `monitor`. Against `carbon` alone the same mutation left every case green, which is how the gap was found | 2026-09-04 | The refusal has two clauses in sequence — *not two halves* and *a half is empty* — and `carbon` trips the second, so a case carrying only that word is satisfied by a clause other than the one it names. Stripping the `\s+` from the pattern is the obvious simplification (the row already says `on`, and the whitespace reads as slack), and it silently makes the `on` inside a name a separator. The general form is worth more than the instance: **a case whose input, needle or fixture is satisfied by something other than the thing it means to pin is green against its own mutation.** This work item has now produced six, and the shape moved each time — an input tripping an earlier clause (this row), a needle satisfied by prose around the row (R3), an exit code satisfied by an unreachable commit (round 1's 🟡 5), a fixture with one record where the claim needed two (round 2's 🟡 7), an assertion that cannot fail at all (round 2's 🟡 6), and a needle satisfied by the same phrase in a neighbouring subsection (found in the mutation loop for round 2's own fix, three times in one run). None was found by reading; every one was found by mutating |
| R3 · `INSTRUCTORS` pins the three row-carrying files on the ROW and the two skills on the NAME, and the asymmetry is deliberate | `tests/test_a_record_says_what_ran_it.py#INSTRUCTORS@3330c08b`, `tests/test_a_record_says_what_ran_it.py#test_every_instructing_file_names_the_row@a2fe73e9` | **Executed** 2026-09-04 at `bf2eddc`: `docs/review-handoff-protocol.md`'s `| Ran by |` renamed to `| Run by |` and the case exits 1. Against the bare-name needle the same mutation left it green | 2026-09-04 | Making the five needles uniform is the tidy-up a reader reaches for, and it is what the case looked like before the mutation caught it. `docs/review-handoff-protocol.md` names `Ran by` in its own `####` section and in the drafts log as well as in the table, so the bare name survives the row being renamed out of existence — the document still says the word while the row a session copies is gone. The two skills carry no table at all, so the row spelling is not available to pin there, which is why the split is asymmetric rather than an oversight |
| R4 · `templates/sdd-phase.md` separates whose the value is from whose keystrokes fill it, and the round template's sentence copied across unchanged makes the row unfillable by anyone present | `` `templates/sdd-phase.md#"# <work-item-id> — phase <N>"@9146ea0e` ``, `tests/test_a_phase_hands_the_next_one_a_record.py#test_the_template_resolves_who_types_a_phase_records_row@460d1dbc`, `tests/test_a_record_says_what_ran_it.py#test_verify_says_how_a_phase_record_comes_to_carry_the_row@ebdd6741` | **Executed** 2026-09-04 at `473765d`: three mutations killed — the spawn-prompt half removed, the transcribe/decide distinction removed, and the sentence naming who writes a phase record removed | 2026-09-04 | The rule is one act for `rounds/round-N.md`, because the orchestrator knows what ran the round and runs `round_record.py new`, which writes that file from the `--ran-by` value it is handed. A phase record is written by the segment that just ran, so *the spawning session fills it, never the segment itself* — true of the round record and copied here verbatim in the first draft — tells the only party holding the pen not to write the row. The correction is not a softening: the value is still the orchestrator's to SOURCE, and what stays refused is the segment sourcing it from its own idea of what it is. A later editor tightening the two templates back into one sentence for symmetry re-opens exactly this, and the shorter sentence is the one that reads better. **Re-read 2026-09-05 in work item 1788597030's round 1 fix pass**, which moved the template's one sentence about the round record: the orchestrator runs `round_record.py new`, which writes that file from `--ran-by`. The distinction this row is about — whose the value is against whose keystrokes fill it — is untouched, and the sentence at the head of these Notes is corrected the same way (round 1's ⬜ 9 of that item) |
| R5 · a phase record's field table carries `Phase`, `Commit` and `Ran by` — three rows, not two — and its three sections still ship outside HTML comments as placeholders rather than filled claims | `templates/sdd-phase.md#"\| Field \| Value \|"@d59fb237`, `tests/test_a_phase_hands_the_next_one_a_record.py#test_the_field_table_is_phase_commit_and_what_ran_it@67075610`, `tests/test_a_phase_hands_the_next_one_a_record.py#test_all_three_sections_exist_outside_comments@f2f6b8d9` | **Executed** 2026-09-04 at `a8d899d`: the case asserts the exact list and goes red on any other, and the sections case is unchanged and green | 2026-09-04 | **Replaces P1 in `seal/ledger.md`, which this branch removed rather than re-pointed.** P1 claimed the table carries ONLY `Phase`/`Commit`, and adding `Ran by` made that false rather than stale — the claim went with the code, which is the case `CLAUDE.md` says is a removal and not a re-anchor. Its second anchor was BROKEN as well, because the case it named was renamed to say what the table now holds. The pin is an exact-list equality rather than a membership test, so it goes red whichever direction the template and the list move apart — worth knowing before a fourth row is added, because the equivalent round-record pin is a membership test and only goes red in one |

| R1 · `added_on_branch` reads the LATEST add, and `--diff-filter=A` is what keeps *added* from collapsing into *touched* — the two together, because each is undefended by the cases the other one needs | `skills/code-review/scripts/chain_check.py#added_on_branch@5461cc08`, `tests/test_a_record_precedes_the_fixes_it_commissions.py#test_a_record_added_before_the_base_and_updated_on_the_branch_passes@53889300`, `tests/test_a_record_precedes_the_fixes_it_commissions.py#test_a_record_deleted_and_re_added_after_the_fix_is_judged_on_the_later_add@4dee6e90` | **Executed** 2026-09-04 after round 1: `found[0]` → `found[-1]` turns the delete-and-re-add case red and nothing else; dropping `--diff-filter=A` turns TWO cases red, the base-moved one and the updated-in-place one. Nineteen mutations across both checkers, one survivor and it is C12 below | 2026-09-04 | **The index was `found[-1]` until round 1's 🟡 8 and nothing held it** — mutating it left all twenty cases green, because `--diff-filter=A` returns one commit for a file that was added and then only modified, which every case then in the file built. The earliest add is the permissive direction: a stub committed on time, deleted, and the real record written after the fixes passes on the strength of a commit that no longer holds any of its content. Note what changed when the index flipped: under `found[-1]` dropping the flag was separable only by a base that MOVES under a long branch, and under `found[0]` it also refuses every correctly updated record, because the newest commit that TOUCHED a record is its verdict update. **A row written for the old index would now be false**, which is why this row names the pair rather than the flag alone |
| R2 · a fix commit that is an ancestor of the record's own `Target SHA` is dropped, and without that the check fails the SECOND round of every run | `skills/code-review/scripts/chain_check.py#written_late@1e82498f`, `tests/test_a_record_precedes_the_fixes_it_commissions.py#test_a_fix_this_round_did_not_commission_passes@d61a2df0` | **Executed** 2026-09-04 at `55ae839`: the filter replaced by `commissioned = True` and the cited case exits 1; every other case in the file stays green | 2026-09-04 | It reads as belt-and-braces beside the ancestry test that follows it, and it is the opposite: round N+1 reviews round N's fixes, so its verdict table answers round N's findings and its cells legitimately name commits that already existed. Round N+1's record is committed after those commits BY CONSTRUCTION. Without the filter the refusal is not a rare false positive, it is red on the second record of every well-run chain — including this repository's own |
| R3 · two names are one ledger when they reach one **inode**, so the skipped set folds by `st_dev`/`st_ino` and not by any spelling of the path | `skills/evidence-check/scripts/evidence_check.py#file_identity@e8a4f000`, `skills/evidence-check/scripts/evidence_check.py#skipped_by_narrowing@91696b61`, `tests/test_a_narrowed_ledger_read_says_what_it_skipped.py#test_an_inode_of_zero_does_not_fold_two_files_into_one@65e2fc82`, `tests/test_a_narrowed_ledger_read_says_what_it_skipped.py#test_a_hard_link_to_the_ledger_is_not_reported_as_skipped@b1ef0ac4`, `tests/test_a_narrowed_ledger_read_says_what_it_skipped.py#test_a_second_spelling_of_the_same_file_is_not_reported_as_skipped@2ac929ab`, `tests/test_a_narrowed_ledger_read_says_what_it_skipped.py#test_two_devices_that_share_an_inode_number_are_two_ledgers@52ab1e1d` | **Executed** 2026-09-04 after round 1: the fold replaced by `normcase(realpath(...))` and two cases exit 1 — the hard link on every platform, the `SEAL/ledger.md` spelling where the filesystem folds case. Measured on the build machine: `normcase('SEAL/ledger.md')` is the identity, `realpath` keeps the case, the filesystem folds it, and a hard link has one inode under two realpaths | 2026-09-04 | **It was `normcase(realpath(...))` until round 1's 🟡 9, and the platform was inside it**: `os.path.normcase` folds case on Windows alone and `realpath` canonicalises case nowhere, so on a case-insensitive filesystem the run read the ledger and then listed it as unread. That is `agent-contract` §13 exactly — a defence resting on the platform where nobody removed the guarantee — and the three obvious cases all pass under either spelling, because `--ledger seal/ledger.md` and the resolver's answer are string-equal. Three spellings break the old rule and one is platform-free: a leading `./`, a case variant, and a hard link. The inode fold answers all three plus symlinks with one rule. **Two ways out of the fold, and round 2's 🟡 8 is that only one of them existed**: `os.stat` raising reaches the fallback, and a `st_ino` of 0 did not — Python's contract makes a zero no identity at all, and taken at face value it gave every unstattable file ONE identity, so a ledger that was read swallowed every ledger that was not. Silence, which is the reverse of what this notice is for. Both ways out now key by path. **Re-read 2026-09-04 in round 3's fix pass, which found two things and moved the anchor** (🟡 6 and the enumeration around it). The `st_ino` test was undefended — swapping it for `st_dev` left the whole suite green, because zeroing BOTH fields is the only state any case built and a zero device falls back exactly as a zero inode does; a real device with a zero inode separates them and now runs in the same case. And the fallback's own claim was backwards: it was written as over-reporting, and it is a WEAKER identity than the inode — `abspath` folds `./seal/ledger.md` into `seal/ledger.md`. **Re-read 2026-09-05 in round 4's fix pass, which found the repair of that sentence false in the same place it had just been fixed** (round 4's 🟡 5). It read *`normcase` folds case where the platform does, so two spellings of one unstattable file read as one wherever the platform says they are one*, and `normcase` folds on WINDOWS alone rather than wherever the filesystem does — which is round 1's 🟡 9 restated one function over, arriving inside its own correction. Executed on a case-insensitive macOS volume: one file, one inode, TWO fallback identities. So the fallback OVER-reports off Windows, which is the declared direction, and the same holds for the two spellings the platform keeps apart anywhere. The paragraph declaring `normcase` unreachable was false too: it tied reachability to the zeroed inode, and `OSError` reaches the fallback on every platform — what is Windows-only is `normcase` differing from the identity, so it is unkillable off Windows and load-bearing on it. Nothing goes unread either way, and the conclusion is unchanged; only both reasons moved |

| R1 · `bin/test` pays for its environment once. A warm call decides by the filesystem alone — an interpreter and a `pytest*` script inside `.venv`, and the version `pyvenv.cfg` records — and never reaches the builder, not even to ask a tool whether the environment is current; a cold call reaches it exactly once; and a build that reports success while leaving no pytest behind is a sentence rather than a call that rebuilds forever | `.github/scripts/run_tests.py#has_pytest@2548b29f`, `.github/scripts/run_tests.py#ensure@2c6edb92`, `.github/scripts/run_tests.py#main@323adce0`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_a_built_environment_is_reused_and_never_rebuilt@d77ca17a`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_a_missing_environment_is_built_once@0d903e72`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_a_build_that_leaves_no_pytest_is_a_sentence@5fb2baeb` | **Executed** 2026-09-06 in phase 1 (cold 5.24 s, warm 0.60 s and 0.56 s over one 25-case module) and re-measured 2026-09-06 in phase 3 at `a192d47`: two consecutive warm calls of `./bin/test tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py -q` from the worktree, **1.01 s and 0.66 s** wall for 37 cases, the interpreter line naming the reused `.venv` both times. The reuse case fails the run if `build` is reached at all, the cold case asserts it is reached once, and the half-built case exits with `still not in it` rather than looping | 2026-09-06 | **The tidy-up this row exists against is making `has_pytest` sure.** Its check is `venv_python(venv).exists() and any(scripts.glob("pytest*"))` — filesystem only, no subprocess — and the obvious repair is to run `python -m pytest --version` and read the answer. That pays an interpreter start on every warm call, which is the cost the whole work item removes; the docstring says so where the repair would be typed. **The claim is not that the first call is cheap** — it is that it is the only one. Phase 1's cold figure was taken where uv's wheel cache and a 3.13 interpreter were already present, so on a genuinely cold machine the first call pays what #133 measured and every call after it still pays 0.6 s |
| R2 · the virtualenv is invisible to git on **every exit of `ensure`**, which is every path that can leave one behind, because `main` reaches a virtualenv through that function and through no other. The runner writes the ignore rather than the tool that built it, and writes it from a `finally` rather than from a list of remembered paths — so it covers the build that succeeded, the build that failed partway, the `.venv` merely adopted, the adopted `.venv` the FLOOR **refuses**, and the directory an earlier run left on a machine where neither builder can now finish. `build` keeps a `finally` of its own for the directory it makes. It does nothing when there is no directory, and leaves an existing ignore alone. **Invisible insofar as the write lands**: the guarantee is owed on every exit and delivered on every exit whose `.venv` this process can write to, and a directory the operator has made read-only is theirs — it stays visible to git, and the runner says which file it could not write and what that costs rather than raising over the refusal it has already printed (#177) | `.github/scripts/run_tests.py#hide_from_git@6182d405`, `.github/scripts/run_tests.py#build@ee6d6dd0`, `.github/scripts/run_tests.py#ensure@2c6edb92`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_the_environment_hides_itself_from_git@70a8f5e5`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_an_existing_ignore_is_left_alone@64998dce`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_a_failed_build_still_leaves_no_trace_in_git@f8e6601a`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_an_adopted_environment_is_hidden_too@f4dbc041`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_a_refused_environment_is_hidden_too@a4263cef`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_a_directory_no_builder_can_finish_is_hidden_too@2d872b61` | **Executed** 2026-09-06 in phase 3, both tools measured rather than inherited from prose: `uv venv --python ">=3.12"` (uv 0.10.4) writes `.venv/.gitignore` containing `*`, and `python3.12 -m venv` (3.12.7) writes **no `.gitignore` at all**. And the fallback branch end to end, with `uv` stripped from PATH: `build()` announced `python -m venv`, returned `None`, `has_pytest` came back `True`, and the ignore read `'*\n'`. **Executed** 2026-09-06 in round 1's fix pass, the two paths the claim used to skip: a build whose install step exits non-zero after the directory exists, and an adopted `.venv` with no ignore — `git status --porcelain` is empty in a real repository for both, where before the fix each printed `?? .venv/`. **Executed** 2026-09-06 in round 2's fix pass, the two exits the list ALSO skipped, in a real `git init` repository at `2b7eea4`: a `.venv` at `version = 3.11.9` refused by the floor, and a `.venv` with no pytest on a machine faked to have neither `uv` nor a 3.12 — `?? .venv/` for both before the fix, empty for both after. Both cases seen red against `2b7eea4` before they were planted, and both killed by mutating `ensure`'s `finally` to write nothing. **Re-read 2026-09-06 in #177's phase 2** at `d210bac`, after phase 1 guarded the write and drifted this anchor: the mechanism the claim is about is untouched — `ensure`'s `finally` still calls the function on every exit, `build` keeps its own, and the six cases above are unchanged and green. What the anchor gained is the guard and its sentence, whose own evidence is R1 of `seal/ledger/1788691941-an-unwritable-venv-turns-the-refusal-into-a-traceback.md` rather than this row | 2026-09-06 | **The tidy-up is deleting `hide_from_git` because uv already writes that file.** True for one of the two strategies and false for the other, and the one it is false for is the fallback — the path taken on exactly the machines least likely to notice a `.venv` in `git status`. The ignore goes inside the directory rather than into the repository's own `.gitignore` so that removing the virtualenv removes the rule with it. **The second tidy-up is moving the call back to the end of a successful build**, where it stood until round 1 found it: the run that ends in a sentence is exactly the run that leaves a half-built directory, so success is the one path that did not need the write. Round 1 reproduced both misses in a clone before the fix. The `is_dir` guard is what makes the failed-build call safe — the first step can fail before any directory exists, and `test_a_failing_build_step_is_a_sentence` goes red without it. **The third tidy-up is turning the `finally` back into calls on the paths that need one**, which reads tidier and is how this row got written twice. The list was tried and went short twice — round 1 added two paths to it, and round 2 found a third with a fourth beside it, one of them created by round 1's own fix. A list is short whenever somebody adds a `return`; an exit-level guarantee is not, and it is why the claim above names a function rather than a count. **The fourth is reading #177 as this row's falsification and removing it.** A row is REMOVED when a change takes away the code it cites, and #177 took nothing away: the function, the two `finally` blocks and all six cases stand, and the claim's whole content — the guarantee is owed to a function's exits rather than to a list of paths — is what the guard was written to keep. What was wrong was never the mechanism but an unstated precondition. The outcome half had ALWAYS depended on the write landing, and on the one path where it does not the row was false before #177 as well, in the worse way: the write raised through the `finally` and replaced the refusal with a traceback. So the caveat states a condition that was always there and is now visible to the reader, which is an under-specified row repaired in place — removing it would delete a true claim and leave the three tidy-ups above unguarded |

### 1788661274-the-roll-names-the-next-version-by-guessing

<!-- One work item's rows. No header — `fold_ledger.py` writes the `###` at
the release and moves this file into `seal/ledger.md`.

Six rows over a branch that is one script and two documents. What earns a row
here is a judgment whose repair looks obvious from the outside, and this
change has an unusual number of them because every one of its decisions is a
choice between two things that both look correct in the file: a guard that
reads as a special case (G1), a parser that reads as unhelpfully strict (G2),
two `print` calls on a path that exits 0 either way (G3), a constant used once
at each end rather than a literal written once (G4), a format stated in the
tracker document rather than in the skill beside it (G5), and a `gh` argument
that looks like plumbing and is text a person reads (G6).

Four of the six are about the same failure seen from four sides: a roll
that silently does nothing, with the workflow green. That is the class of the
bug being fixed, one step over, and it is what `plan.md` names to design
against.

Nothing from phase 1 or phase 2 reached the ledger, so G1 through G5 are
written in phase 3 against code those phases wrote. The units and cases were
opened at `4695eae` before each row was written, and the two modules were
re-run at the same commit.

**G6, and G2's third instance, are round 1's — written in the fix pass at
`253ac8c`.** G2 said its class was closed at two members and it had three:
the row enumerated what follows the marker and never what precedes it. It now
states the method behind the enumeration — a title is three segments, and a
version comes out of one of them — rather than a list, because a list is what
was complete last time.

Every hash here was stamped by `evidence-check --reverify --ledger '<this
fragment>'`, the scoped WRITE form, and never by an unscoped run; the unscoped
READ form ran afterwards, its output in `phases/phase-3.md`. The scoped form
cannot narrow below a file, so a blanket run would re-stamp `seal/ledger.md`'s
one pre-existing drift on behalf of a session that never read it — and it
would re-stamp F5, whose anchor this branch moved and whose row is edited by
hand for exactly that reason. -->

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| G1 · the roll fires only where a new version has shipped since the open log opened, and it decides that by **equality** — what the open log's title says it rolled from, against the version in the tree — never by comparing two versions in order and never by asking whether this push was a release | `.github/scripts/roll_flow_measurement_issue.py#roll_is_due@a1bd8c19`, `.github/scripts/roll_flow_measurement_issue.py#main@aa71fddc`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_a_push_that_shipped_no_new_version_rolls_nothing@a6a6c945`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_a_new_version_since_the_log_opened_rolls_it@6d9c74d3` | Read 2026-09-06 in phase 3 at `4695eae`, the guard and both cases opened together; **executed** 2026-09-06 in phase 1, where both cases were seen red before the condition existed, and re-run 2026-09-06 in phase 3 at the same commit: 28 cases in that module, exit 0 | 2026-09-06 | **The tidy-up is deleting the guard**, because a release always rolls and the branch reads as a special case. That is the code as it stood before #155, and it is the 0.8.1 incident: a patch release closed a log opened for a version that had not shipped, and opened a second one under the identical title. **The second tidy-up is comparing the two versions in order** — `rolled_from(title) < shipped` reads as the same question and is not: it makes the answer depend on version arithmetic at the one moment `docs/branch-and-release.md` says the next number is not knowable, and it is what turns an old-convention title naming `0.9.0` into a log that is never due once `0.9.0` ships. Equality asks only what the title already states. **The third is asking whether this push is a release.** The workflow fires on every push to the default branch, so that fact is not available here; what is available is whether the open log already names the version in the tree |
| G2 · a title this script cannot read as its own is **due**, never silent, and *its own* is the whole of what `log_title` writes, required from the first character. A title is three segments — what precedes the marker, the marker, and what follows it — and a version may be read out of the third only when the first two are exactly what the writer wrote. `rolled_from` answers `None` otherwise, and `None` is a different fact from a version rather than a softer one: not the whole title, not the empty string, and **not a version lifted out of a title with a different prefix** | `.github/scripts/roll_flow_measurement_issue.py#rolled_from@4ff7e2f2`, `.github/scripts/roll_flow_measurement_issue.py#TITLE_PREFIX@daf589d5`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_rolled_from_reads_only_the_title_the_roll_itself_writes@365dd11b`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_a_title_the_roll_did_not_write_names_no_version@db48506b`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_a_title_the_roll_cannot_read_is_due_rather_than_silent@6a6080bd`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_a_title_written_before_this_change_is_always_due@d3813203` | **Executed** 2026-09-06 in phase 1: two mutations survived the first loop and both are killed by name — `rolled_from` answering the whole title where the marker is absent, and answering `""` where the marker is there with nothing after it. **Executed** 2026-09-06 in round 1's fix pass: the third instance, found by round 1 and pinned by `test_a_title_the_roll_did_not_write_names_no_version`, plus two more mutations killed at the same time — the `startswith` guard removed, and `.strip()` removed. Read 2026-09-06 at `253ac8c`, the function and its five cases opened together | 2026-09-06 | **The tidy-up is making `rolled_from` helpful.** Returning the title it was given, or `""`, reads as the forgiving answer and passes every case that goes through `main`, because a wrong answer that is also unequal to the shipped version still comes out as due. The gap opens at a title that happens to BE a version string: read as the log for that version, it is not due, and the log stalls with the workflow green. That is why the pure function is pinned directly rather than through its caller. **The second tidy-up is reading an unreadable title as not due** — do not touch what you do not understand. The two directions are not symmetric: *not due* stops the log forever and nothing goes red, *due* costs at most one roll that was not owed. **The third tidy-up is the one this row missed for a phase and a round — matching the marker anywhere instead of the prefix from the start.** `partition` reads as the same question and is not: it accepts a title with any prefix at all, so `docs: explain flow measurement — after 0.8.2` answered `0.8.2` and the log for 0.8.2 was never due. The row enumerated the segment after the marker, both instances of it, and never looked at the segment before it — which is why the class had a third member the row did not name. One rule then covers four things — an old-convention title, a title somebody tidied by hand, a title this script never wrote at all, and the migration off the old convention, which retires itself at the first release after this change |
| G3 · both outcomes exit 0, so the two printed lines are the only thing that tells a release that rolled from a mechanism that has stopped. `nothing due:` names the log and the version; `rolled:` names the issue closed and the title opened | `.github/scripts/roll_flow_measurement_issue.py#main@aa71fddc`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_the_nothing_due_line_names_the_log_and_the_version@f7fefa42`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_the_rolled_line_names_the_issue_closed_and_the_title_opened@2bbf0752` | Read 2026-09-06 in phase 3 at `4695eae`, both cases opened against the two `print` calls they read; **executed** 2026-09-06 in phase 1, both seen red before the lines carried what they now carry, and re-run 2026-09-06 in phase 3, exit 0 | 2026-09-06 | **The tidy-up is deleting a `print` as noise**, or collapsing the two into one line that says the job ran. What a workflow log is for is the state a person came looking for, and this script has two states that both leave the job green — the whole point of `spec.md`'s fourth acceptance scenario is that a reader tells them apart without opening GitHub. **The second tidy-up is making *nothing due* a non-zero exit**, on the reasoning that a release should always roll. It should not: a re-run of the job, and a push to `main` that shipped nothing, are both ordinary, and failing them turns the normal case into a red release. Contract §14 is what puts the two lines under cases at all — they are text a person reads and acts on |
| G4 · `TITLE_PREFIX` is the one string the title is written through and read back through, in full at both ends, so the writer and the reader cannot drift apart in width any more than in wording. `log_title` writes it and `rolled_from` requires it; `TITLE_MARKER` is the substring it is built from and exists because `docs/issues-and-milestones.md` is read against the marker by name. No other place in the module spells the title out | `.github/scripts/roll_flow_measurement_issue.py#TITLE_PREFIX@daf589d5`, `.github/scripts/roll_flow_measurement_issue.py#TITLE_MARKER@55d0d25a`, `.github/scripts/roll_flow_measurement_issue.py#log_title@2f833e6b`, `.github/scripts/roll_flow_measurement_issue.py#rolled_from@4ff7e2f2`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_the_title_the_roll_writes_is_the_title_the_next_roll_reads@66101365` | Read 2026-09-06 in phase 3 at `4695eae`: the constant, both functions and the round-trip case opened together, and the module read for any other spelling of the title — `open_issue` calls `log_title`, `main`'s recovery message calls `log_title`, `close_issue`'s comment calls `log_title`, and no f-string writes the words. **Executed** 2026-09-06 in phase 1, where the case reads the writer's own output back through the reader rather than comparing either against a literal | 2026-09-06 | **The tidy-up is writing the title where it is used**, which is what the code did before this change: `title = f"chore: flow measurement — {version}"` inside `open_issue`, one literal at the one place a title is created. It reads simpler and it is how the two ends come apart — a wording change to the writer alone leaves `rolled_from` reading for a marker nobody writes, every title answers `None`, every title is therefore due, and the roll fires at every release forever. That is the invariant broken from the other side, and nothing goes red: the failure looks exactly like the old behaviour. `after` is the load-bearing word in the constant, because what follows it is the version the log rolled FROM |
| G5 · the two documents a person reads carry the convention, and the **title format lives in `docs/issues-and-milestones.md` rather than in the shipped skill**. The skill states the rolling log's two ends — it opens at a release and is discarded by the release that ships the next version — and names no title | `` `skills/verify/SKILL.md#"## Measure the segment, and feed the flow log"@b1d57f7c` ``, `` `docs/issues-and-milestones.md#"## A label answers *what it is about*, and survives the move"@993881ca` ``, `tests/test_a_segment_feeds_the_flow_log.py#test_the_rolling_logs_life_is_bounded_by_two_releases@1d551564`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_the_tracker_doc_states_the_condition_the_roll_carries@becb243a`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_the_tracker_doc_states_the_title_the_roll_writes@969baa01`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_the_tracker_doc_says_what_a_title_written_before_this_means@20349ded` | Read 2026-09-06 in phase 3 at `4695eae`, both sections opened against the four cases that read them; **executed** 2026-09-06 in phase 2, each of the four seen red against `deca998` before either document was edited, and both modules re-run 2026-09-06 in phase 3, exit 0. **Re-read 2026-09-08 (#206)**: the sentence in this section saying what the version check refuses was corrected from *whether it has shipped or is still ahead* to at-or-above the running one, with the below-the-running half stated. The title format this claim is about, and the fact that it lives here rather than in the shipped skill, are untouched | 2026-09-09 | **This row is the presence F5 is the absence of**, and the two are read together: F5 holds that the shipped skill names no tracker state existing in this repository alone, which is why the title format cannot go there, and this row holds that it went somewhere a reader will find it. **The tidy-up is moving the format into the skill for symmetry** — the skill is where a session is told to post a measurement, so the title belongs beside it — and nothing in the skill's own prose would tell that reader they had crossed a line. **The second tidy-up is deleting the tracker document's *not rewritten* paragraph as history nobody needs.** Both conventions are visible in the tracker forever, because closed logs are not retitled; without that paragraph the obvious tidy is to retitle the old logs into the new form, which falsifies every comment citing them. **The document's case reads the script's own constant rather than a literal**: it feeds every backticked `chore: …` example in the section through `rolled_from` and requires at least one the script can read back, because an example answering `None` is an old-convention title being held up as the current one — which is what a well-meaning edit produces, since the older form is what the tracker still shows **Re-read 2026-09-06 at the 0.8.2 release preparation.** That release's own broad gate refused three released version strings in the cited section and they were rewritten to an illustrative one; #179 carries the class. Both halves hold: the title format is still in that section, and the shipped skill still names no title. Stamped by hand from a copy, because a `--reverify` over `seal/ledger.md` also re-stamps S8, whose claim nobody here has read. **Re-read 2026-09-07 in work item 1788735085's phase 1** (#179), which renamed `test_no_loaded_file_hardcodes_the_running_version` inside this section and widened the sentence around it from "a released version" to a real version at or above the running one. The title format the claim is about — `chore: flow measurement — after 1.2.3` — and where it lives are untouched; the four cases this row names were re-run, 175 passing. The blanket `--reverify` that moved this hash rewrote only the three anchors this branch edited, so S8 was not re-stamped by it. **Re-read 2026-09-09 for #300**, same section rewrite. The title format is still in `docs/issues-and-milestones.md` and still absent from the shipped skill, and the rolling log's two ends are stated as before. |
| G6 · the comment posted on the log being closed states the convention **in force**, and it names the successor by calling `log_title` rather than by describing it. It used to promise a log *"for the version this release ships next"*, which is the prediction this work item removed — `close_issue` takes the shipped version for no other reason | `.github/scripts/roll_flow_measurement_issue.py#close_issue@34c368fd`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_the_close_comment_names_the_version_this_release_shipped@dd97c886` | **Executed** 2026-09-06 in round 1's fix pass: the case was seen red with the old wording restored under the new signature, and the whole module re-run green after. A generic sentence is exactly what drifts back: nothing in it is derived from the title the roll actually writes, so the next edit can restate the removed convention and no case notices. Naming the successor through `log_title` is what makes the comment fail when the convention moves. **The second tidy-up is leaving it alone as a workflow detail** — this is a comment posted on a GitHub issue people go on reading after it closes, which is contract §14's own subject, and round 1 found it precisely because `issue_body` was fixed and its class-mate was not |

<!-- One work item's rows. No header — `fold_ledger.py` writes the `###` at

Five rows over a branch that is one new function and its cases. What earns a

change has five because the guard is built almost entirely out of refusals
to do the direct thing: it does not list the sections it protects (F1), it

A numeric consumer that carries no operator is none of those, and the one this module has, `round`, is where the 🔴 was.
