Re-executed: the exempt set in the loaded tree is exactly five files, the two dated records plus the three exact paths, and the loaded set still reports no offender. **Re-read 2026-09-07 in review round 2's fix pass, findings 4 and 5, and the token rule moved.** `(?!\w)` is gone. It was added for `v1.2.30`, `\d+`'s greed already covers that — measured, with and without, identical — and its only remaining effect was to hide a LETTER-suffixed prerelease of the unshipped version: `0.9.0rc1`, `0.9.0b1`, `0.9.0_final` and `v0.9.0rc1` all answered no offender while `0.9.0-rc1` was refused and the substring check this branch replaced caught every one. **A prerelease of a version that has not shipped is a timer in both spellings**, which is the owner's decision after the same lookahead produced a finding in three consecutive passes. The comment above the constant had stated the disproven `v1.2.30` grounds while the module said the opposite thirty lines down; both now say the same thing. Re-executed over 18 trailing contexts: the removal widens exactly the five prerelease shapes and changes nothing else — `0.9.0.1`, `2.0.1.5`, `v1.2.30`, `0.9.05` and `x0.9.0` all answer as before — and the loaded set still reports no offender. **The leading lookbehind had no case either**, found by mutating this fix: `2.0.1.5`'s tail is below the running version, so the case guarding it answered `[]` with or without the lookbehind. `1.9.9.9`, whose tail is above it, and `x0.9.0` now pin it. **Re-read 2026-09-07, finding 6.** The other-product route could not be taken on the token the refusal prints: the message reports `match.group(0)` — `v9.9.9` — and the lookup read `match.group(1)`, so an author declaring exactly what was printed got no change. Executed both ways round. Either spelling is now accepted. The live entry pins neither arm, because `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py` writes `4.4.17` with no `v` and the two spellings are the same string there; dropping either arm left the tree green until a case declared a `v`-prefixed token both ways. **Re-read 2026-09-07, finding 8.** Round 1's fix had replaced `any()` with a loop that RETURNS on the first `/` entry whose prefix matches, which made `RECORDS_OF_A_MOMENT` order-dependent: an entry written below the prefix that contains it was unreachable, and that list is precisely the one a later author appends to. Back to `any()`, so every entry is considered. Executed: with an exact entry written after `docs/experiments/`, the loop answered `False` where `any()` answers `True`. No instance existed in the tree; the three exact entries sit above the one prefix. **Corrected 2026-09-08 (#205): the reviewer's second example, a narrower prefix appended after a wider one, is NOT the same mechanism and never changed an answer in either implementation.** Every `/` entry takes the same `DATED_RECORD.match(basename)` check whether it is wide or narrow, so where it sits in the list cannot decide it; only an exact entry, which skips that check through `rel == entry`, can. Re-executed 2026-09-08 over the four arrangements in both directions — the `any()` implementation from the tree, the early-return one reconstructed from the docstring that describes it, since the branch carrying it was reverted and its intermediate commits are not in this clone. The exact-path arrangement is the only row where the two implementations differ (`True` against `False`) and the only one whose answer moves when the entries are written the other way round. The one assertion pins the one mechanism there is, and **no assertion was added for the other**: asserting an arrangement that changes no answer is the class this correction belongs to. **Re-read 2026-09-07, finding 9.** `DATED_RECORD`'s shape was unpinned: dropping its trailing `-` and loosening its component widths each left every case green, and the first silently widened the exemption — `docs/experiments/2026-09-03.md` would have become exempt where it is scanned. The pattern itself is right, being the name `docs/experiments/README.md` prescribes (`<date>-<what-was-asked>.md`), so this was case strength rather than a wrong pattern. Two assertions pin it, and all three mutations of the pattern now turn the case red. The exempt set in the loaded tree is unchanged at five files, and the enumeration after every fix in this pass reports no offender |
| R3 · the refusal names a route the author can actually take for **every** kind of token the check reads as a version, including one that is not a release at all | `tests/test_release_hygiene.py#what_to_write_instead@401a8ae9`, `tests/test_release_hygiene.py#refusal@2035da87`, `tests/test_release_hygiene.py#test_the_message_has_a_route_for_every_token_the_check_refuses@8c805612`, `tests/test_release_hygiene.py#test_no_loaded_file_names_a_version_at_or_above_the_running_one@08730484` | **Executed** 2026-09-07 in review round 1's fix pass, finding 3. Four shapes put through `timers_in` at `e25ff8b`: `2026.09.03` is refused as `(2026, 9, 3)` and `3.13.9` as a CPython patch version, while `2026-09-03` is not read at all. The case was seen red on exactly one of its five assertions — the date — with the other four green, which is what settled that only one route was missing. Mutations: deleting the date route and deleting the another-product route each turn the case red. ~~**Re-executed 2026-09-08 (#203)**: `refusal`'s seven elements deleted one at a time, `tests/__pycache__` cleared between and the file restored from bytes kept before the first write — every one caught, none survived~~ (**the seven were real and every one was caught; the word that was not measured is *every*, and review round 1 corrected it below**). **Re-derived 2026-09-08 in that round's fix pass**: `ast.parse` of the returned expression gives four operands whose first is one `JoinedStr` of three parts — six leaves — and thirteen mutations over them leave two survivors, named in the note | 2026-09-08 | **Only the date needed a new sentence, and `3.13.9` did not.** The round left finding 3 open as "three routes and none fits", and the orchestrator asked which of the two shapes was actually unrouted. A version belonging to another product is exactly what `VERSIONS_OF_ANOTHER_PRODUCT` is for, and bash's `4.4.17` already sits in it as the worked precedent — the route existed and the message already named the constant. **A date has no route and gets no exemption**, because it is not a release of anything and an exemption would be a lie about what it is; the message tells the author to write it in a form that is not version-shaped, and names the dashed spelling this repository already uses. **The regex was deliberately NOT narrowed to exclude dates** — that is finding 1's mistake wearing this one's clothes, and #179's *Done when* makes the message the deliverable. **Corrected 2026-09-07 by review round 2, finding 7.** This row recorded, as a measured fact, that *"no assertion can pin which expression an `assert` uses as its message without reading the file's own source, so the floor is one obvious line rather than another checker"*. The sentence about the final line is true and it was the wrong grounds: it was used to justify leaving the message inline in the `assert`, where an edit detaching the routes from the printed text left all 28 cases green. Extracting the whole refusal into `refusal(running, offenders)` — the same move `what_to_write_instead` already had — closes that with an ordinary assertion and no source reading. **Re-executed**: the exact mutation this row called unpinnable, replacing the builder's `what_to_write_instead()` with a literal, now turns the routes case red where it was green at `27c36fe`. What is left unpinned is only the one line `assert not offenders, refusal(running, offenders)`, and bypassing THAT still leaves every case green — a residual of one obvious line rather than of the whole message. **Corrected 2026-09-08 (#203): that last sentence was written as a measured fact and it was not one, which is the second time this row has done it.** Three mutations of `refusal` disprove it, each executed on its own at `7349afc` and each leaving the module at 30 passed: deleting `+ "\n  ".join(offenders)`, deleting the `{running}` interpolation, and deleting the whole paragraph that says why such a line is a timer. What the sentence could truthfully have claimed is the survivor of the mutations that were actually run, which is a smaller claim than *what is left unpinned*. **A third class is outside that set by construction**: nothing pins that nothing was ADDED. A sentence inserted at the end of the timer paragraph, or a line inserted before the routes, leaves the module at 32 passed (round 2, re-measured round 3). The pin is one assertion over the concatenation of the pieces the case already reads — measured green unmutated and red on either insertion in round 3 — and it is declined because that concatenation is `refusal` rebuilt in the test, which `plan.md` rejected. ~~**Re-executed 2026-09-08**: `refusal` is now enumerated by construction as four pieces and three separators — seven elements, the fifth being the `"\n  "` that CLOSES the first literal and so reads as part of the paragraph rather than as an operand, which is the one three consecutive attempts on the original branch each missed. `test_the_refusal_prints_every_piece_it_builds` pins each on its own; all seven deletions were run one at a time with `tests/__pycache__` cleared between, and every one turned that case red.~~ **Corrected 2026-09-08 by review round 1 (#203), and this is the THIRD false completeness claim this row has carried.** *"Four pieces and three separators"* is a reading of the source, not the source: `ast.parse` of the returned expression flattens the `+` chain to four operands whose first is one `JoinedStr` of three parts, which is **six leaves**. Seven was reached by splitting that third part into a paragraph and the separator closing it, and by promoting the `"\n  "` the join is called ON — the receiver, `func.value`, where `args` holds `offenders` alone — to an element of its own. A bare `ast.walk` does return that string; the flattening rule stated before the count is what puts it inside the join rather than beside it (review round 3). What it cost is that the paragraph itself was read only at its two ends: deleting `it goes red on the day that version ships, on the release's own `, or just `preparation commit, `, each left the module at 32 passed, with 86 characters between the two assertions read by nothing. Every leaf is read whole now, the paragraph by two contiguous halves, and both deletions turn the case red. **Two mutations survive that set and neither is a limit.** The check's own `assert not offenders, refusal(running, offenders)` handed a literal leaves the module at 32 passed — and pinning it does NOT mean reading this file's own source, which is what the sentence it replaced claimed: `tracked` and `timers_in` are module globals, and swapping them raises the check with a message comparing equal to `refusal(running, offenders)`, measured in the round. The other is a reordering that carries every separator with the piece it precedes; the naive swap is caught. **What is absent from a measured list is unmeasured** — that sentence was right each time and each time the list beside it was described as something more than a measurement |
| R2 · `-z` is what makes `git ls-files` safe to split, and `core.quotePath=false` is not the argument that turns the escaping of non-ASCII paths off — **either one does that by itself** | `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py#shipped_templates@12c49cdb`, `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py#test_the_templates_check_reads_prose_only_and_descends@e4cc13db` | **Executed** 2026-09-07 on git 2.50.1 (Apple Git-155), in a scratch repository holding `templates/spaced name.md`, `templates/한글.md` and a name carrying a newline. Four variants and a fifth reading for the control character. No arguments: `"templates/new\nline.md"` and `"templates/\355\225\234\352\270\200.md"`, both quoted and octal-escaped. `-z` alone: every name raw, NUL-separated. `core.quotePath=false` alone: the Korean name raw, and the newline name STILL `"templates/new\nline.md"`. Both together: byte-identical to `-z` alone | 2026-09-07 | **The instruction the comment gives was right and its grounds were wrong**, which is why nothing in the call changed. `core.quotePath=false` contributes nothing while `-z` is there — the two listings are identical byte for byte — so it is still the one to prune if either goes. What `-z` does that the config does not is two things: it turns off the escaping of CONTROL characters as well, and it separates on NUL, the one byte a filename cannot hold. The split below the call is on NUL, so dropping `-z` returns the whole listing as a single entry rather than as one entry per file. **This is comment-and-record only and the cases pass either way**, which is exactly how the false sentence sat unnoticed through a round record, a ledger row and a comment. Nothing in the suite could have caught it, and nothing added here would: what a comment claims about a tool is outside what any assertion in this repository reads |

Four more rows arrived with review round 1's fixes. R5 is the receiving
guard's signal, which the round measured as the wrong one; R6 is the two
machines the refusal has to tell apart, and the precedence between them when
both go silent; R7 is the exporting machine getting the reason it is the only
one that can act on. R8 is not this work item's defect at all — it is four
escape cases that a clock disarmed, found by re-enumerating this pass's own
diff, and it is in this fragment because this is the branch that measured and
fixed it.

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 · `git_asked` is the one place that can say WHY git did not answer, and `git()` is that function with the distinction thrown away — so a caller reading `""` as a fact about the repository asks through the first and a caller with nothing to do with `why` keeps the second unchanged. Its `answered` parameter carries the one measured exception: `git config --get` exits 1 when the key is not set, so for that command alone a non-zero code is an answer | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#git_asked@1a908746`, `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#git@f605675e`, `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#remote_url@b381e445`, `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#head_sha@6cefdf40` | **Executed** 2026-09-07. Four mutations of these units, all killed: an `OSError`/timeout answering `""` instead of `None`, a non-zero return code counting as an answer, `remote_url` dropping `answered=(0, 1)` so an unset remote reads as unanswerable, and `head_sha` asking a different question. The exit codes themselves were measured against git 2.50.1 before the shape was chosen — unset gives `(1, '', '')`, a `.git/config` git cannot parse gives `(128, '', 'fatal: bad config line 9 …')`, and `rev-parse HEAD` on an unborn branch gives `(128, 'HEAD', …)` | 2026-09-07 | **The shape is promoted, not invented.** `porcelain`, `tracked` and `gitlinks_under_root` had each grown the same *(value, why)* body for themselves, and the last of them states the rule this work item cites: the unanswerable question refuses. A fourth body would have been one idea in four places — which is how `head` got the return-code check in #81 and the `remote` beside it did not. **`--default ""` was measured and rejected**: it collapses the unset case into exit 0 and removes the special case entirely, and it arrived in git 2.18, which nothing else this plugin runs needs. Leaning on it would turn an old git into a refusal on a path that works today, which is a compatibility break bought for one line of tidiness |
| R2 · `manifest_of` LEAVES OUT a field git could not answer rather than writing it empty, so `remote` carries three states — a URL, `""` for a repository with no `origin`, and absent — and `head` carries two, present meaning a SHA was read. The format number does not move | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#manifest_of@7dd09c09`, `tests/test_the_records_can_be_carried_out_and_in.py#test_the_export_omits_a_remote_it_could_not_read@41f2519d`, `tests/test_the_records_can_be_carried_out_and_in.py#test_the_export_records_an_empty_remote_it_could_read@aabb24f1`, `tests/test_the_records_can_be_carried_out_and_in.py#test_a_repository_with_no_commit_records_no_head@0f3c65a3` | **Executed** 2026-09-07. Three mutations of `manifest_of`, all killed: an empty remote left out as well, an unreadable remote written as `""`, and an unreadable head written as `""`. Each of the three cases was seen red before the fix — the two absence cases against the old code directly, and the empty-remote case by the `remote_url` mutation above, which is the only way a case pinning existing behaviour can be shown to bite | 2026-09-07 | **`""` had to keep meaning *no remote*, and that decided the direction of the whole change.** Twenty-two hand-built manifests in the export/import module write `"remote": ""`; making `""` the unreadable state would have edited all twenty-two, and each edit is a chance to change what a case was pinning. The third state is absent, which no existing fixture is in. **The format number stays 1** because no field was renamed or repurposed and format 1's only reader of these two already went through `manifest.get` — moving it would refuse every zip an older build wrote, for a change older builds tolerate |

Four mutations of the block, all killed: the refusal never firing, the flag ceasing to be an escape, a manifest recording `""` counting as unreadable (nine cases red, including six that predate this work item), and the second `git()` restored inside the message. Five of the seven cases were red against the old code before the fix; the two that pin existing behaviour — a clone with no remote, and a zip recording `""` — were seen red by the `remote_url` and manifest mutations | 2026-09-07 | **Two flags because there are two facts.** Typing `--allow-other-repo` is saying *I have read both URLs and they are one repository under two spellings*; someone whose git could not answer has read neither. Routing both past one flag merges the facts again at the only place a user acts on the distinction, which is what the ticket asked to separate. **The refusal names git's own words** because *run it again* and *this machine will never answer* are the two things a person does next, and only git's text tells them apart. **The unreadable case cannot be built out of a repository on disk**: a `.git/config` broken enough to fail `config --get` also fails the `rev-parse --show-toplevel` that resolves the root, so the command stops one screen earlier — measured, both exit 128 — and a duplicated `url =` line answers with the last value at exit 0. The enumeration is `grep -n 'git(' skills/implement/scripts/seal.py`, which names every call by construction: nine hits at the base commit, of which three are prose inside a comment or docstring and one is the definition, leaving five call sites. Re-run after the fix: one call site, `other_worktrees`. `grep -nE '=\s*git\s*$\|=\s*git\s*[,)]'` is the cross-check for a call reached through a reference and returns nothing, so no call escapes the first grep. **Re-verified 2026-09-08** by work item 1788817289, which drifted this anchor from outside: its round 1 fix pass planted a `# RIDER:` inside `other_worktrees` and its round 2 fix pass re-stamped that comment. The enumeration was re-run — `grep -n 'git(' skills/implement/scripts/seal.py` still leaves one call site, `other_worktrees`, and the rider adds no call of any kind — so the claim is unchanged and the row is re-stamped rather than removed: nothing this row cites was taken away | 2026-09-08 | **The ticket said four were left and there were five.** It did not count the second `git()` inside the refusal message, which asked git again for the URL it had just read — this ticket's own failure appearing inside the message reporting it. Four of the five needed a behaviour change and one needed a sentence, so *four* is also the count of sites that changed, but it is a different four and the partition is what matters. **The fix pass added no new member.** `remote_url`, `head_sha` and `git_asked` all return `(value, why)`, so a caller of theirs cannot read a value as a fact without also being handed the reason it is not one — the class is structurally empty for them rather than empty by inspection |

Review round 1 measured five values — `null`, `42`, `[]`, `{}`, `true` — all importing at exit 0 with both guards silent, and I reproduced all five red at `5cf81b3` before the fix. One mutation of the fixed guard, back to key presence, kills five cases | 2026-09-08 | **Presence was never the signal.** The absent case and the `null` case are the same fact stated two ways, and the check that separates them is `isinstance`, which covers both with one test. `normalise_remote`'s own `isinstance` guard stays load-bearing on the one path a non-string still reaches it — behind `--allow-unreadable-remote` — which is where `test_a_manifest_field_of_the_wrong_type_does_not_raise`'s two `remote` rows now run |

A flat list of reasons could not carry which field went unread, so the first cut printed *the other machine takes in without a flag* for an export that had read the remote and only missed the HEAD SHA — a fix promised for a refusal that was never coming. The dict is what makes the caller able to ask |
| R8 · a case that recomputes a path the code derives from a clock must use the SAME clock. Round 1 ran the same module green nine hours earlier. One mutation of the helper, back to the local clock, kills the same four | 2026-09-08 | **This predates the branch and is not this work item's defect.** It is recorded here because this is where it was measured. East of UTC the two clocks disagree from local midnight to UTC midnight — nine hours a day in this timezone — and in that window the link lands where the export never writes, so four cases pinning round 3's 🔴 assert a refusal against nothing and report green. A case armed or disarmed by the hour it runs at reports nothing either way, which is what makes this worse than a case that is simply absent. What is left is the UTC midnight instant between the helper's call and the export's own: microseconds a day where it used to be hours |

<!-- specs/1788789330-the-update-notice-names-the-expensive-move -->
### 1788789330-the-update-notice-names-the-expensive-move

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 · S2 · S3 · the session-start notice names `/reload-plugins` before the restart, scopes the reload's measured reach to the copy already in force, and names all three unmeasured axes | `hooks/version-check.py#notice@6bb51f3b`, `tests/test_version_check.py#test_the_warning_names_the_cheap_move_before_the_expensive_one@d2062fee`, `tests/test_version_check.py#test_the_notice_agrees_with_the_docstring_about_what_a_reload_re_reads@a4a373f5` | **Executed** 2026-09-07. Seen red before the hook was touched, twice: at the build (`1 failed, 16 passed`, exit 1, the failure printing the old message verbatim) and again at round 1's fix pass, where the added scope and third-axis assertions failed against the shipped notice (`2 failed, 16 passed`, exit 1). Green after (`18 passed`, exit 0). Round 1's fix pass ran five mutations and killed five — the reload unnamed, the restart named first, the reload's claim replaced with *a reload installs the new version into this session*, the scope qualifier alone dropped, the third axis deleted, and the gap flipped to a positive claim. **Round 2 then ran two the fix pass had not thought of, and both were green**, so that count was true of the five chosen and not of the class. Round 2's fix pass re-ran five against `db5b9cd` and killed five, and **that count was wrong about the class for the second time** — the orchestrator's re-run then found M9 (`unmeasured, yet it is picked up`) alive at `e678c47`. Against the exact pin at `760ac3e`: baseline green on both cases, then six mutations and six killed — M6 (the gap hands the third axis back after `but`), M7 (the reload's claim gains *and out of the one you just installed*), M8 (M6 with `yet` for `but`), M9, and the two controls. `hooks/version-check.py` restored byte-identical after each | 2026-09-08 | **A substring test over a multi-sentence message pins the message's vocabulary, not any of its claims, and splitting on sentences does not fix that by itself.** The build's draft asserted `"measured" in msg` and the gap sentence satisfied it for the reload. The sentence-split draft that replaced it was still green against *a reload was measured to install the new version into this session*, which carries every word it looked for — round 1 found that by running the assertion body against five mutations. What closes it is pinning the claim's **subject** (`skill bodies`) and its **scope** (`already on` / `in force`), and every axis the sentence is supposed to disclose. **And that is still not enough, because a required phrase cannot see an ADDED clause.** Round 2 ran the sentence-pinned body against two mutations carrying every word it looks for that still tell a user the reload picks up the new install, and both passed. **Four predicates over this prose were tried and each was blind one word over, so the predicate was abandoned rather than extended.** They were: the words appear anywhere in the message; the words appear in the sentence making the claim; the clause from `install` to the next comma carries the negation; and no adversative from a four-word list follows. `unmeasured, yet it is picked up` walks through all four — executed against the third and fourth at `e678c47`, `18 passed`, exit 0, with the notice then telling a user the reload picks up the new install. English has more ways to hand an axis back than a list can hold. So `notice()`'s whole output is pinned as an exact string at `760ac3e`. **What that pin holds is the text as it stands, not any future text.** Measured against six mutations that leave the golden alone, it kills six. Measured again with the golden updated in the same edit — which is what an author rewording the notice does — an added overclaim and `unmeasured, yet it is picked up` both pass, and so do the restart named first, the `measured` label dropped, the `skill bodies` subject dropped, and two of the three axes dropped: `18 passed`, exit 0, each time. The second case holds exactly one property across a rewording, the scope qualifier, and the rest is carried by the assertion message, which a person reads rather than a checker. **The cost, accepted deliberately: a legitimate rewording fails the case.** The wording is the contract here — it is what a user reads about what was measured |
| S4 · S5 · S7 · the update procedure gives both moves with their evidence labels, names the run that would settle the open row, and says it will type neither | `skills/update/SKILL.md#"## Procedure"@8141a934`, `skills/update/SKILL.md#"## What this does not do"@8f48c0f7`, `skills/update/SKILL.md#"## Output"@4720ea3d` | Read, not run. Nothing pins this file's wording | 2026-09-07 | **The scope statement had to widen with the change or the change backfires.** *"It does not restart anything"* was about one move; a procedure that names `/reload-plugins` and never says it will not type it is offering the act the ticket's own *Not this* rules out. The reason is durable rather than incidental — a built-in CLI command is not a skill, so no procedure can invoke one  **Re-read 2026-09-10 by work item 1788993115 (#292)**: the diff command in *Procedure* runs against `templates/claude-md-block.md` instead of the marketplace clone's `CLAUDE.md`, and a sentence says which of the two is the source (phase 3). The three-part fact is untouched |
| S6 · both READMEs carry the same three-part fact under *Updating*, English and Korean, with the scope qualifier inside the claim's own sentence in both | `README.md#"### Updating"@2a2d9e55`, `README.ko.md#"### 업데이트"@c479d680` | Read, not run. `tests/test_docs_line_wrap.py` holds both editions to 88 display columns and passed — re-run 2026-09-08 after the direction fix, `48 passed`, exit 0. That test skips fenced code, so nothing pins the two by-hand comments; a reader is what caught them | 2026-09-08 | **Same sentence, not the next one.** The first Korean draft carried the qualifier in a following clause opening `다만`, where the English had it inside the claim. A reader who stops at the first sentence gets the unscoped version, which is the defect one clause over — found by re-running the property over the corpus after round 1's fix landed, not by reading the diff. **The same fix pass then left both by-hand comments pointing the wrong way**: it replaced them with a pointer at the load paragraph and wrote *see below* / *아래 참고* when that paragraph is above them. The paragraph opens *Then load it* in English and *적용하는 방법은 두 가지이고* in Korean, and both fenced by-hand blocks sit after it in their own edition. Corrected at `db5b9cd`. The class was re-derived over every line the branch ADDS rather than over every direction word in the two files, because the branch only inserts and an insertion flips a direction word only where the inserted text is the new referent  **Re-read 2026-09-10 by work item 1788993115 (#292)**: both editions' *Updating* gained the sentence that `templates/claude-md-block.md` is the block's one source and the repository's `CLAUDE.md` a generated copy (phase 3), together, which is what moved both hashes. The three-part fact is untouched |
| The measurement's boundary is one axis wider than the experiment's own *What it did not establish* section says | `docs/experiments/2026-09-03-skill-preload-and-the-copy-in-force.md#"## Results"@b1d499df`, `docs/experiments/2026-09-03-skill-preload-and-the-copy-in-force.md#"## Method"@5e8739e2`, `docs/experiments/2026-09-03-skill-preload-and-the-copy-in-force.md#"## What it established"@57003901` | Read, not run. §*Method* installs no second version — it edits a skill in place, spawns, reads the answer, restores from a backup. §*What it established* item 2 names the version cache as the copy that loads, with runs 1–4 failing **because** they edited the marketplace clone. §*Results* run 6 reads `version cache, flag on` | 2026-09-07 | **Inferred during implementation.** The experiment names one gap (whether the `/` menu omits a flagged skill) and this is a second it never claims to have covered: a re-read of the copy in force is not evidence about resolving to a *new* copy. It is the axis the ticket assumed when it wrote *"a full restart is not required"*. Round 1 asked for the two load-bearing anchors beside the results table, because the inference rests on the method rather than on run 6's row. The `0.5.0` in the path is not the experiment's — it is at `seal/specs/1788433011-every-spawn-prompt-is-retyped-from-memory/questions.md` Q1 |
| The corpus this class was enumerated over is 37 distinct lines at `86e140f`, 15 in class and 22 out | `seal/specs/1788789330-the-update-notice-names-the-expensive-move/spec.md#"## The class, enumerated by construction"@5589a37f` | **Executed** 2026-09-07. `git grep` over tracked files at `86e140f`: 33 `restart` · 3 `재시작` · 2 `reload` after `grep -vi preload`, less the one `docs/flow.md` tracker row that carries both `restart` and `reload` | 2026-09-07 | **An enumeration has to name the tree it counted.** The build measured `grep -r` over a working tree partway through the branch and got 38; the third `reload` hit was this work item's own `routing.md`, so the count included a line the branch had just written. Round 1 proposed an untracked `.venv` as the cause and that is not it — `grep -rnI 'reload' .venv` returns 0. Both numbers were right for their own tree and neither said which tree, which is the whole of the defect |

<!-- specs/1788789985-round-record-dies-on-python-3-9 -->
### 1788789985-round-record-dies-on-python-3-9

<!-- specs/1788789985-round-record-dies-on-python-3-9 -->

<!-- One work item's rows. No header — `fold_ledger.py` writes the `###` at
the release and moves this file into `seal/ledger.md`.

Three rows for one ticket, split at the lines where the claims are actually
different things. R1 is the guard and the moment it fires, which is the whole
of #226. R2 is where the floor number lives, which is a decision the ticket
did not make and the handoff asked to be argued rather than assumed. R3 is the
class, which is what §12 of the agent contract owes beyond the coordinate the
ticket named. -->

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 · `round_record.py` refuses an interpreter below the floor **at entry** — before `argparse` reads anything and before `chain = load(CHAIN, ...)` reads and executes the sibling checker — with a sentence naming the floor, the version found and the interpreter it was found at, saying nothing was read and nothing was written, and exit 2 | `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#below_floor@d1565fbf`, `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#BELOW_FLOOR@d3f8a865`, `tests/test_a_script_says_which_interpreter_it_needs.py#test_an_interpreter_below_the_floor_gets_a_sentence@e554443d`, `tests/test_a_script_says_which_interpreter_it_needs.py#test_a_floor_above_this_interpreter_refuses_before_anything_is_read@c8c4b481`, `tests/test_a_script_says_which_interpreter_it_needs.py#test_the_script_refuses_at_entry_on_a_below_floor_interpreter@937d3718`, `tests/test_a_script_says_which_interpreter_it_needs.py#test_the_guard_precedes_every_other_module_level_act@8b737070`, `tests/test_a_script_says_which_interpreter_it_needs.py#test_the_refusal_is_ascii_because_it_is_written_before_stderr_is_set_up@1e429de5` | **Executed** 2026-09-07. The real script under `/usr/bin/python3` (3.9.6) on macOS, before and after: before, the same command exited 2 saying `--item no/such/work-item is not a directory inside a git repository`, having got through argument parsing and path resolution — which is the ticket's complaint, that the failure reads as a bug in the input; after, it exits 2 naming the floor, `3.9.6`, and the interpreter's path, and `--help` no longer succeeds either. Seven mutations, all red on the case that should catch them: the boundary `>=`→`>`, the version dropped from the sentence, exit 2→0, the write removed, the *nothing was written* clause removed, the floor lowered to 3.9, and — the decisive one — the guard **moved into `main()`**, which is where a guard is naturally written. That last mutation left every end-to-end case green; only the AST case caught it, which is why that case is not decoration. **Re-verified 2026-09-08 after review round 1**, with the sentence changed: `PYTHONIOENCODING=ascii /usr/bin/python3 …` now prints the whole refusal in ASCII and still exits 2, where the `§` had reached the operator as `\xa7` — the guard writes at module level, before `__main__` reconfigures stderr. An eighth mutation, an act (`os.path.abspath`) inserted above `FLOOR`, is now red on the AST case and was green against its earlier spelling | 2026-09-08 | **The four `strict=True` sites are untouched, on grounds and not on taste.** `CONTRIBUTING.md` §*Running the checks* names 3.12 as the supported floor, so the ticket's other suggestion buys a few more lines before the next 3.10+ construct at the price of the invariant the comment above the first of them states. What is NOT claimed here: any behaviour on an interpreter older than 3.9.6, which is the oldest one this machine had. The block deliberately uses no walrus and no f-string so that it parses much further back, and that is a design intent rather than a measured fact |
| R2 · the floor is one number with six carriers and the guard does not import it — `FLOOR` in `round_record.py` is pinned by the suite to `.github/scripts/run_tests.py`'s `FLOOR` and to `ruff.toml`'s `target-version`, rather than read from either at runtime | `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#FLOOR@856e0b4d`, `tests/test_a_script_says_which_interpreter_it_needs.py#test_the_floor_is_the_number_the_runner_and_the_linter_hold@b448c042` | **Executed** 2026-09-07: the case passes, and the mutation lowering `FLOOR` to `(3, 9)` turns it red along with three others. **Read**: the plugin cache at `0.9.0` does ship `.github/scripts/`, so the import this row refuses would work today — the refusal is not about reachability. `round_record.py#load` already imports a sibling script by file path, so the machinery was there | 2026-09-07 | **The argument, because a copied literal needs one.** A read that can fail gives the guard a second way to die on the one machine that has no other way of being told what is wrong; and a fallback-safe read still has to name a floor in its `except` branch, so the second spelling survives the import anyway — which is what settles it, since there is no version of the import that removes the second number. `seal/specs/1788789985-round-record-dies-on-python-3-9/spec.md` §*Where the floor number lives* carries it in full. **What this row also closes as a side effect**: the repository's two floor authorities were never compared to each other — `test_release_hygiene.py` reads `ruff.toml`, `test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py` reads the runner, and nothing read both |
| R3 · every shipped `.py` this plugin ships is re-enumerated by the suite for constructs newer than the floor, and a file carrying one either says at entry what it needs or is classified with where it was deferred to | `tests/test_a_script_says_which_interpreter_it_needs.py#ABOVE_THE_FLOOR@a089d49d`, `tests/test_a_script_says_which_interpreter_it_needs.py#CLASSIFIED@165bdc52`, `tests/test_a_script_says_which_interpreter_it_needs.py#shipped_python@171bc017`, `tests/test_a_script_says_which_interpreter_it_needs.py#test_no_shipped_script_needs_more_than_the_floor_without_saying_so@a8fe2955` | **Executed** 2026-09-07. Three constructions, none of them a walk through the tree looking for problems: `grep -rln '__name__ == "__main__"'` over `git ls-files '*.py'` for the entry points (25); `hooks/hooks.json` and the documented `python3 ` commands for which interpreter each one gets; and `/usr/bin/python3 -m py_compile` at 3.9.6 over every shipped `.py` for what actually breaks — one failure, in a test file, so **no shipped script carries 3.10+ syntax** and a guard placed after the imports is always reached. **Six** files carry a runtime construct above the floor; one is fixed here and five are in `seal/follow-up.md`. That count was five and four until review round 1 re-derived the class from the AST rather than from this row, and the sixth — `skills/verify/scripts/session_cost.py`, executed on 3.9.6 and exiting 1 with a bare `AttributeError` traceback — is the limit stated in this row's Notes firing on a construct the row had already thought of, spelled differently | 2026-09-08 | **The enumeration's spelling has been wrong twice, and that is the part worth keeping.** Both times an instance the pattern could not reach, both times the class one member larger than the count. `zip\([^)]*strict=` hid the site in `round_record.py#inherited_rows`, where an inner `verdict_words(reader, rows)` closes a parenthesis before the keyword is reached; `datetime\.UTC` hid `session_cost.py`, which spells the module `import datetime as dt`. A class enumerated with a pattern that cannot match every instance is a class enumerated by reading with a command's clothes on. **What this check is worth, stated rather than implied, and now measured**: it matches two constructs by text, so a 3.10+ construct nobody has thought of is invisible to it, and the two spellings it still cannot see are a `zip(` whose `strict=` is on a later line and `from datetime import UTC` used bare — neither in the tree, checked rather than assumed. A third text widening would move the blind spot a third time; the AST walk is what closes it, and it is a change to a gate rather than a fix. The `python3 -m py_compile` sweep that covers syntax was run once by hand rather than wired in. **The balanced-paren rewrite offered for the zip half was declined, measured**: it loses `zip(xs, f(g(y)), strict=True)`, which the shipped spelling catches |

<!-- specs/1788817289-local-mode-from-first-setup-to-the-gate -->
### 1788817289-local-mode-from-first-setup-to-the-gate

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1–S4 · a work item under the common git directory resolves to a repository, and the tree it resolves to is the CALLER's where the caller is one of the clone's worktrees — asked of `git worktree list`, never derived from the path — and where that list names something that is not a work tree, the caller's own tree wins if it belongs to the same clone, and a path no later `git -C` can use is refused rather than named | `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#worktrees_of@780acd56`, `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#repo_of@f1694538`, `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#common_dir_of@b603480b`, `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#shares_the_clone@5617a3e9`, `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#where@189d10bd`, `tests/test_local_mode_reaches_the_review_chain.py#test_a_separated_git_directory_does_not_displace_the_callers_tree@eb376528`, `tests/test_local_mode_reaches_the_review_chain.py#test_the_clone_is_identified_from_the_directory_it_was_asked_about@a0035eca`, `tests/test_local_mode_reaches_the_review_chain.py#test_a_root_that_is_not_a_work_tree_is_refused@27db2232`, `tests/test_local_mode_reaches_the_review_chain.py#test_a_local_mode_item_resolves_to_its_repository@ea44fa69`, `tests/test_local_mode_reaches_the_review_chain.py#test_a_linked_worktree_resolves_to_itself@04a503bf`, `tests/test_local_mode_reaches_the_review_chain.py#test_a_shared_item_in_a_linked_worktree_resolves_to_that_worktree@e5b4b108` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. The refusal was reproduced first against a scratch local-mode repository — `round-record: --item …/.git/seal/specs/<id> is not a directory inside a git repository`, exit 2 — and the cause measured: `git -C <path inside .git> rev-parse --show-toplevel` exits 128 with `fatal: this operation must be run in a work tree`, while `--git-common-dir` and `worktree list --porcelain` both exit 0 from the same path, in a main tree and in a linked worktree. Six cases, four seen red first. Five mutations of the two new units, all killed | 2026-09-10 | **`--show-toplevel` is not failing to recognise the path; it is declining the question.** Nothing done to the argument gets an answer out of it, which is why the fix asks git a different question rather than normalising harder. **Which tree, when the root is shared by all of them.** One local root serves every worktree of the clone, so the item cannot say which tree a run is about; a round record names a HEAD and a branch, so the caller's tree is the answer and the main tree is the fallback for a caller outside the clone entirely. **The shared-mode fast path is behaviour, not an optimisation.** A mutation dropping it left every case green and moved a shared item in a linked worktree to the MAIN tree, because the fallback takes the first entry git prints; the case that pins it was written from that survival. **Re-verified 2026-09-08 in round 1's fix pass**, which built the case the claim had not been tried against: a repository made with `--separate-git-dir`. `git worktree list --porcelain` prints the GIT DIRECTORY as the worktree path there, with no `bare` line to tell it by, so the caller's real tree was not in the list it belongs to and was discarded in favour of a path `git -C` refuses — measured end to end as *…/sepgit is not in a git repository — nothing was compared*, exit 2, with the record already written. A bare clone took the same path. The clone is compared by common git directory now, three cases added and all three seen red, three mutations of the added units all killed — one survived the first pass, `common_dir_of` dropping the resolution against the directory it asked, because `--git-common-dir` answers absolutely from a separated repository and `.git` from an ordinary one, so only the second shape reaches it **Re-read 2026-09-10 in work item 1789002694 (#30), phase 4.** `where`'s round guard became `getattr(args, "round", 1)`, because the new `seal` subcommand finds the last record instead of naming a round and so carries no `--round`. Nothing on the resolution path moved: the claim is about which tree a work item resolves to, and that question is asked of `git worktree list` exactly as before. |
| S2 · the refusal that remains is two sentences, and each says which of the two things failed | `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#where@189d10bd`, `tests/test_local_mode_reaches_the_review_chain.py#test_an_item_under_no_repository_is_still_refused@2469a201`, `tests/test_local_mode_reaches_the_review_chain.py#test_a_path_that_is_not_a_directory_says_so_on_its_own@d2b9a1ba` | **Executed** 2026-09-08, both cases red against the joint sentence first. The mutation restoring the old wording is killed | 2026-09-10 | **The old sentence was TRUE of a correctly placed work item.** `--item … is not a directory inside a git repository` covered a typo and a local-mode root at once, and the second reading is the one nobody could act on: the path was right and the message said it was not. Splitting is what makes the local-mode arm nameable at all, and it is why the second half says which two places were tried **Re-read 2026-09-10 in work item 1789002694 (#30), phase 4.** The same one-line guard change moved this anchor. Both refusal sentences are untouched. |
| S5, S6 · `chain_check` names the root it searched: a local-mode repository is told where its root is and that nothing under it is committed, a shared-mode one is still told to write the file, and the sentence names the prefix and the branch that were searched for | `skills/code-review/scripts/chain_check.py#local_root@4863a31b`, `skills/code-review/scripts/chain_check.py#nothing_declared@03324ece`, `tests/test_local_mode_reaches_the_review_chain.py#test_local_mode_is_told_it_is_local_mode@94aba745`, `tests/test_local_mode_reaches_the_review_chain.py#test_local_mode_still_examines_nothing@146bd428`, `tests/test_local_mode_reaches_the_review_chain.py#test_the_shared_sentence_says_what_it_searched_and_for_what@3212efca` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. The false sentence was reproduced first: a local-mode repository holding a declaration for the current branch printed *Add seal/specs/<work-item>/routing.md to declare*, exit 0. Five mutations of the two new units, all killed — one survived the first pass, the shared sentence's new half, and the case pinning it was written from that survival | 2026-09-08 | **The verdict does not move, and that is the argument.** Reading the untracked declaration is what `tracked_declarations` already refuses in its own docstring — the local run would become more permissive than the place the check actually runs — and local mode's documented trade is that CI checks nothing (`templates/hygiene.yml`'s header, `implement` §Bootstrap). So the remaining friction is correct: what was wrong was a sentence that could not be told apart from a genuinely undeclared work item. **`routing.optin`, not a second load.** `hooks/routing.py` imports that module by plain name, so it is in `sys.modules`; a load of our own would leave two module objects answering the one question the module is named for having one of. **The alternative is recorded rather than dropped** — `questions.md` Q3 and `seal/follow-up.md` put `--worktree` reading declarations from the working tree to the repository owner. **Re-verified 2026-09-08 in round 2's fix pass**, which resolved the common git directory once for both readers here — the same pair of calls that cost the mode gate a `git` process on every Bash call. The sentence this row is about is untouched |
| S7–S10 · a root whose `config.md` carries no `Mode` row is named by a gate: one deny, then one `ask`, then silence for the rest of the session — two prompts per session per repository — and nothing at all once the row exists. The repository is the CLONE in local mode, where one root serves every work tree, and a `config.md` that exists and cannot be opened is silence rather than a deny | `hooks/mode-gate.py#undeclared@1a042be4`, `hooks/mode-gate.py#unreadable@934fbbe9`, `hooks/mode-gate.py#already_asked@168b4cec`, `hooks/mode-gate.py#git_dir_of@9b23be68`, `hooks/mode-gate.py#marker_dir@79bac62f`, `hooks/mode-gate.py#main@d9c63327`, `tests/test_the_mode_question_is_asked_once.py#test_a_root_with_no_recorded_mode_is_named@4bdb51cb`, `tests/test_the_mode_question_is_asked_once.py#test_a_session_in_a_subdirectory_is_still_asked@6a7ca212`, `tests/test_the_mode_question_is_asked_once.py#test_the_second_attempt_in_a_session_only_asks@7ba3b6f7`, `tests/test_the_mode_question_is_asked_once.py#test_the_third_command_of_a_session_is_silent@cd899bb7`, `tests/test_the_mode_question_is_asked_once.py#test_a_config_nobody_can_open_is_silence_not_a_deny@dd8dbab3`, `tests/test_the_mode_question_is_asked_once.py#test_one_local_root_is_one_question_for_the_whole_clone@033002ae`, `tests/test_the_mode_question_is_asked_once.py#test_a_shared_root_is_still_a_question_per_work_tree@f361c4b5` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. Every case written before the gate existed and seen red — six as failures, eight as collection errors. Nine mutations across the gate and the moved reader; two survived the first pass and both were real gaps, not equivalent mutants: taking `cwd` for the repository root left the gate silent for every session sitting in a subdirectory, and the table parser's header gate had no case anywhere in the tree. **Re-verified 2026-09-08 in round 1's fix pass**, which changed what this claim says: the budget was re-derived by RUNNING the gate — twenty ordinary Bash calls per session, two sessions, three repositories — giving 1 deny + 1 ask + 18 silent where a root has no row, and 20 silent in the other two. Six further cases, five seen red against `9d2f440`; nine mutations of the units this pass added or changed, all killed | 2026-09-08 | **The budget the claim stated was not the budget the code had.** It read *one deny then `ask`*, and the `ask` stood for the rest of the session: on a gate that fires per Bash call rather than per commit that is a prompt on every command — one deny and nine asks over ten ordinary calls, where the sibling gate was silent on nine of the same ten because it returns early for a command that is not a commit. The way out, `seal mode`, is itself a Bash call, so an unattended run could not reach it. Both prompts are spent once now. **A gate is not a writer.** `hooks/config.py#declared_mode` folds a file that will not open into *declared nothing*, which is right for `seal mode` — it goes on to write the row either way — and wrong for a gate, which denied a repository whose answer might already be there. **The marker follows the ROOT, not the tree.** A local root is one folder every worktree of the clone shares, so keying it per tree asked twice about one folder; shared roots stay per tree because each work tree carries its own. **The subject is the session's workspace, not a commit's target.** Its sibling gate resolves where a commit LANDS because a verdict about a change has to be about the repository that change reaches; this is a fact about the workspace, and `git -C <elsewhere>` does not move the session. **It fires on the first Bash call rather than at the commit, and the budget is what decided it.** The count is the same either way, because the budget is per session; where it lands is not — at the commit the question arrives beside the review arm's, on a session that may have nobody at the keyboard, which is what `skills/implement/SKILL.md` §1 costs a question by. **No waiver token.** The two arms of the commit gate each carry one because a change can honestly be exempt from a check; setup cannot, the way on is one command, and a token here would be the standing exemption `docs/review-chain-spec.md` refuses. `ask` is the escape hatch, and an unattended run can take it. **Re-verified 2026-09-08 in round 2's fix pass**, which changed what the gate COSTS and nothing about what it says: the common git directory is resolved once in `main` and handed to `undeclared` and `marker_dir`, where three units had each asked git for it. Measured with a logging `git` on `PATH`, one `ls` payload — a local root falls from 2 `git` processes to 1 in a main work tree and from 4 to 2 in a linked one, a shared root in a linked worktree from 4 to 3, and a repository with no root is unchanged at 1 and 2. The `deny → ask → silent` sequence and all 279 cases across the seven gate modules are unchanged |
| Q1 of the mode row · the `Mode` reader is one parser, in `hooks/config.py`, and `seal.py` re-exports it | `hooks/config.py#declared_mode@a2f91a20`, `hooks/config.py#config_rows@2e0e32f1`, `tests/test_the_mode_question_is_asked_once.py#test_the_command_and_the_gate_read_one_parser@b8850700`, `tests/test_the_mode_question_is_asked_once.py#test_rows_above_the_header_are_not_rows_of_this_table@62caca65` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. The identity of the implementation is asserted by `__code__.co_filename`, because a test that loads a hook module under a name of its own holds a different module object with the same code. The header-gate case was written from a surviving mutation and confirmed uncovered elsewhere: the same mutation left `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py` at 117 passed | 2026-09-08 | **A `PreToolUse` hook must not import a two-thousand-line command to read one row**, and two parsers of one table is how a file passes the writer and fails the reader. The reader moved and the writer stayed, with aliases rather than a copy. **Not into `hooks/optin.py`.** That module's docstring says there is no config key and everything in it fails toward *not opted in*; the row records an answer and decides nothing about opting in, so putting it there would read as `docs/one-root-by-lifetime.md` §*The opt-in signal is the root itself* being softened. **A third copy of the loop exists** in `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py#items` and is out of scope here; it is named in the handover |

<!-- specs/1788817290-the-derivation-misreads-and-the-record-refuses-the-id -->
### 1788817290-the-derivation-misreads-and-the-record-refuses-the-id

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 · a finding id is a bare integer behind an optional severity marker, read through one pattern that both the verdict table and the fix table share; anything else is refused naming the format, quoting the cell and quoting the whole row, and a genuine duplicate quotes both rows | `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#finding_number@816e3c3b`, `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#FINDING_ID_RE@2795fca6`, `tests/test_a_finding_id_is_a_bare_integer.py#test_the_committed_records_only_lose_a_miscount@112c666b` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. Every case seen red first against the unfixed generator, where the round-prefixed ids produced `two rows for finding 2` and no message named a format. Six mutations of `finding_number`, one at a time with `tests/__pycache__` cleared between: the anchored pattern, the marker character class, the quoted first row, the named format, the quoted row, and the table label were all caught. A seventh — dropping the emphasis strip — SURVIVED and put `test_an_emphasised_id_is_the_same_id` behind it, four spellings; it is caught now. **Round 1's finding 3 is that the refusal did not arrive at all on one shape it refuses:** a `#` cell of punctuation ending in a non-digit sent the pattern exponential — 11.4 s at 28 characters, doubling per character — so `close` and `new` produced nothing and never returned. Seen red both ways before the repair, by the timed assertion at 28 characters and by the case itself being killed at 90 s. The repetition now takes one marker character rather than a run of them, which is the same language: verified over 6245 `#` cells in the committed records and 4368 constructed shapes with zero disagreements, and 100 000 characters now refuse in 0.003 s | 2026-09-08 | **The corpus is what chose the refusal over accepting a round prefix.** All 130 committed records that parse were run through both rules: 82 pass under either, 46 already refuse today, 2 pass today only by miscounting. Those two are `1788420761-…/rounds/round-4.md`, whose `r3 🟡 2` keys as finding **3** out of the `3` in `r3`, and `1788433011-…/rounds/round-3.md`, whose `🟢 round 2's finding (🟡 4)` keys as **2** where the cell names 4. The case pins the INVARIANT rather than that count — a record the old rule reads correctly is never refused by the new one — because the count moves as records land, and it asserts the rule refuses something in the corpus so it cannot pass with no teeth |
| R2 · a unit pytest itself reaches — a `test_*` def in a file `python_files` collects, a fixture under `tests/` or in a `conftest.py` pytest loads, a `pytest_*` def in a `conftest.py` pytest loads — has no call site in the tree by design, so its reach is `pytest only` and not `no call site found` | `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#runner_reached@20cc5b88`, `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#conftest_is_loaded@6ea63f32`, `tests/test_a_runner_reached_unit_reads_pytest_only.py#test_a_conftest_nothing_is_collected_under_is_not_loaded@e67369f0`, `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#decorated_as@466c4b58`, `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#call_sites@61f9b386`, `tests/test_a_runner_reached_unit_reads_pytest_only.py#test_a_fixture_reads_pytest_only@03ed0a0f` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. The class was enumerated by RUNNING `call_sites` over every top-level def under `tests/` at `ba22b28`, not by reading: 1892 of 1947 `test_*` defs, 8 of 42 fixtures, 1 of 483 other helpers and 0 of 2 classes read `no call site found`. Cases seen red first — all three built kinds read `no call site found` alike against the unfixed generator. Eight mutations, one at a time: the `under_tests` guard, each of the three arms separately, a blanket *everything under tests/*, the attribute tail in `decorated_as`, and `call_sites` never asking were all caught. A ninth — the hook arm ignoring the file name — SURVIVED and put `test_a_hook_outside_a_conftest_is_a_recorded_limit` behind it. **Round 1's findings 1 and 2 are that this predicate drew the boundary one step short of that clause in both directions**, and both were seen red first: a `test_*` def in `tests/helpers.py` read `pytest only` because the arm asked only `python_functions` and not `python_files`, and a root `conftest.py`'s fixture and hook read `no call site found` because `under_tests` gated every arm. Re-enumerated after the repair by running the predicate over all 3052 top-level defs in the 114 tracked `.py` files: not one verdict in this repository moves, and the four that move are built placements this tree holds none of. Six more mutations of the added `collected` unit: five were caught and **one SURVIVED** — dropping the `_test.py` half of `python_files`, which nothing was holding because the built repository had no suffix-named module. `test_the_second_python_files_pattern_collects_too` is that half, and all six are caught now. **Round 2's finding 1 is that the conftest arm then said `pytest only` about every conftest pytest does not load**, which is round 1's finding 1 arriving from the other side inside its own fix — executed over seven built placements, `src/`, `a/b/`, a vendored tree, an examples directory and `tests/vendor/` among them. The arm now asks `conftest_is_loaded`, which is whether a file `python_files` collects sits at or below the conftest's own directory, and it replaces the `tests/` gate for a conftest rather than sitting beside it. Both new cases were seen red against the shipped predicate before the repair, and the re-enumeration at 3052 still moves no verdict here | 2026-09-08 | **One helper is what bounds the rule**, and round 2 found it is no longer the one round 1 named. `tests/test_the_reopening_is_one.py#floor_record` is still passed by name as a value at five sites and never called, but it stopped reading `no call site found` at `824bfca`: this work item's own `round-1.md` and `round-1-report.md` quote its `def` line in a paste-ready fix, the walk greps every tracked file rather than the Python ones, and the row now names those two records as its reach. The unit still bounding the rule is `tests/test_the_records_can_be_carried_out_and_in.py#timed_out`, which arrived with the merge of `release/v0.9.1` and did not exist when round 1 enumerated. That a committed record quoting code invents a call site is a defect of `call_sites` and is deferred, not closed here. **One limit is recorded rather than closed:** the hook arm reads `conftest.py` alone, and pytest also dispatches hooks from collected test modules — widening it would catch any helper somebody named `pytest_something`. **What letting a conftest through costs, and where the line now sits:** the grounds this cell used to give — *a fixture in a file named `conftest.py` is pytest's by construction wherever it sits* — are false of pytest, and that was round 2's finding 1. The directory decides: pytest loads a conftest for the test files collected at or below its own, so one with nothing collected under it is imported by nobody. The repository root passes that in any tree with tests, which is the placement round 1's finding 2 was raised for; `src/conftest.py` passes it in a colocated layout and fails it in a segregated one, which is what pytest does. **What is NOT repaired**, and it is the same class one gate over: a fixture in an uncollected non-conftest module under `tests/` still reads `pytest only`, because deciding it means asking whether a conftest imports the name — a third question with its own argument. This tree holds none, measured over all 3052 defs. **No reach value was added**, so `docs/review-chain-spec.md`'s five-value enumeration and the test that derives it from `call_sites`' own source both stay green |
| R3 · a unit's contract carries the set of constant literals its own `return` statements can produce, keyed by type as well as value, so a unit that gains or loses a returnable value enters `Contract changes` with its reach | `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#return_literals@12c84144`, `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#top_units@a8f7b24c`, `tests/test_a_new_returnable_value_is_a_contract_change.py#test_the_derivation_agrees_with_an_independent_one_over_the_whole_tree@25b6fd65` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. Cases seen red first, the end-to-end one reading `Contract changes \| none` — #194's exact symptom — before the fix. Cross-checked twice, and the two halves are not the same kind of check — round 1's finding 4 is that this cell used to call both of them independent. The whole-tree half compares `literals_of` unit by unit over every top-level def in every tracked `.py` file, and `literals_of` is the shipped `return_literals` loop character for character apart from the order of one assignment: two copies of one algorithm agree by construction, so what it holds is the shipped derivation against later drift, not a blind spot. The count of units actually carrying a literal is asserted beside it, so an always-empty derivation could not pass. The INDEPENDENT statement is the 21 constructed before/after pairs, whose expectation column is a hand-written judgment of what should be reported per shape rather than a reading of the code, and it is what covers the nested-scope boundaries and the bare `return`. Six mutations, one at a time: type-keying, tuple elements, the nested-scope boundary, the contract's third element, and a class carrying a literal set were all caught; a sixth, dropping the `n.value is not None` guard, survived and is an EQUIVALENT mutant — `isinstance(None, ast.Constant)` is false, so the loop adds nothing either way | 2026-09-08 | **A real instance the old two-element contract missed, found by construction rather than by reading:** across `v0.8.0..v0.8.3`, `skills/code-review/scripts/chain_check.py#read_record` began returning an explicit `None` — literals `{}` → `{None}` — with signature and arity unchanged. The old contract read it as unchanged and the new one reports it. Two other real spans (`v0.8.3..v0.9.0`, `v0.9.0..86e140f`) held no member, which is why containment on them proves nothing and is not cited as if it did |
| R4 · what the literal-set comparison does NOT catch — a changed input→value mapping — is stated in the spec as a paragraph, with the measured instance named, rather than closed | `` `docs/review-chain-spec.md#"##### The fix surface — `Contract changes` and `New units`"@68d96f3f` ``, `tests/test_a_new_returnable_value_is_a_contract_change.py#test_the_hole_is_a_hole_and_not_a_claim@7eb168de` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. The measured instance rebuilt from its two ends and asserted UNCHANGED under the shipped contract, so the case goes red the day somebody widens the check and leaves the paragraph standing. The instance is `tests/test_release_hygiene.py#is_a_record_of_a_moment`, confirmed at `seal/specs/1788735085-a-loaded-file-naming-a-real-version-is-a-timer/rounds/round-2.md` finding 11, which the smith read rather than took on the prompt's word. **Re-read 2026-09-08 in round 1's fix pass**, which added a paragraph to this same subsection saying which of the three member rows are about where a file sits and why the two answers differ. The hole paragraph and its measured instance are untouched, so the claim stands and only the region's hash moved | 2026-09-08 | **The hole is the deliverable and not a gap left over.** Documentation alone was refused as an answer before the first edit, because moving the work to a person is the more expensive design by this repository's first goal — so the check ships and the paragraph states where it stops. A `Contract changes` cell reading `none` means *no unit's parameters, arities or returnable literals moved*, never *nothing a caller depends on moved*, and the residual is the reviewer's. `docs/review-handoff-protocol.md` carries the same two limits for a conforming tool, because a protocol that promises the row without them promises more than any implementation can give |
| R5 · a rider is watched wherever this repository plants one, and its stamp may name either a commit or the content it was read against | `tests/test_a_rider_reaches_its_file.py#RIDER_ROOTS@36ad1730`, `tests/test_a_rider_reaches_its_file.py#rider_stamps@ec3486f6` | **Executed** 2026-09-08, round 2's finding 2. The roots were `hooks`, `skills`, `agents`, `templates`, and `tests` was missing — so the rider round 1's fix pass planted at `test_the_reopening_is_one.py` was seen by neither the stamp check nor the ancestry check, while the branch's own record asserted the stamp names no commit of this branch as though a check had answered it. Adding `tests` was seen red first, and it uncovered four things rather than the two the round named: this file's own assertions about the marker read as riders with no stamp, `test_the_records_can_be_carried_out_and_in.py` carried a rider with no stamp in any form, and `test_the_printed_ledger_name_is_the_file_that_was_read.py` was stamped `4581fe1`, an ancestor of neither `HEAD` nor `origin/release/v0.9.1`. The split now anchors on a comment line that OPENS with the marker, and the stamp pattern accepts `against <anchor>@<hash>` beside `at <sha>`, which is what lets a rider whose measurement commit was already squashed away carry a stamp that resolves — no commit-form stamp for that one can be written, and that is #239's defect stated as a corpus rather than as an argument. The ancestry case skips anchor stamps and asserts the corpus still holds a commit-form one, so it cannot pass over an empty list. Cross-checked by running `fix/239-a-stamp-names-content-not-a-commit`'s own `rider_check.py` over this working tree: the four riders stamped here read **4 ok · 0 drifted**, and every remaining problem is a pre-#239 `at <sha>` stamp that branch migrates on its own | 2026-09-08 | **This is the second time this list was the defect rather than the corpus**, `templates` having been the first, and it is why the repair is not the one that lands. `fix/239-a-stamp-names-content-not-a-commit` moves the walk to `.github/scripts/rider_check.py` over six roots and migrates every stamp, so whichever of the two branches merges second takes that version wholesale — the hunks here are all inside the block it replaces. What is written here is the smaller change that keeps this branch's own rider watched in the meantime, in the form the release ends in. **Two readings of the marker are in the tree at once and they had to be made to agree:** #239 opens a block at any comment line CONTAINING the marker and this file at one that opens with it, so prose about the convention is a stamp-less rider there and not here. The marker is therefore never spelled out in a comment in this file, which is what the executed cross-check above confirms. **What this check does NOT verify is the hash behind an anchor**; that is #239's checker's, and until it lands an anchor stamp here is a date and a claim rather than a recomputed one **Two of the four anchors this row cited are gone, and they were removed rather than re-pointed:** `#239` replaced this file's machinery on the release branch, so `STAMP` and `test_every_rider_stamp_names_a_commit_this_branch_can_reach` no longer exist. The claim did not go with them — the roots gap is still a fact and the two surviving anchors still carry it — and what now watches a rider wherever one is planted is `.github/scripts/rider_check.py`, evidenced in `seal/ledger/1788826000-a-stamp-names-content-not-a-commit.md`. Read and edited 2026-09-08 at the merge that removed them. |

Its fixture put the second rider directly after the first, the two merged into ONE comment run, and it passed with the rule narrowed to the first block — the exact thing it claims to pin. It now asserts its own fixture has two blocks first. What the design gives up: a change consisting only of adding or removing a comment inside the unit goes unnoticed, and an ordinary comment butted against a rider is absorbed into its block. Both lose an alarm rather than inventing one. **Re-read 2026-09-08 in round 2's fix pass**: `region_lines` had `rel` in scope and passed it to nothing, so the exclusion list it builds and the one the reader builds would have disagreed the moment `comment_blocks` learned that `#` is a heading in markdown — the reader returning no rider for a heading while the hasher still cut that line out of the region. Found by enumerating the call sites of finding 11's fix rather than by a finding; the row's claim is unchanged and the hash moved by one argument |
| S3 · a rider block opens only at a line that carries the marker **and is itself a comment** — the head of the line after stripping, `#` or `<!--`, and `#` only where `#` opens a comment at all, which in a `.md` file it does not: there it opens a HEADING, and both callers of the reader are told the path so they cannot disagree about it — and it CLOSES at the next marker as well as at the end of its comment run, so two riders written back to back are two riders. The marker appearing in prose, in a table cell or inside a string literal is not a rider. That is what let the scanned roots widen to `.github` and `tests` without a blocklist | `.github/scripts/rider_check.py#comment_blocks@f9d15bf7`, `.github/scripts/rider_check.py#RIDER_ROOTS@7a4aac80`, `tests/test_a_rider_reaches_its_file.py#test_a_markdown_heading_naming_the_marker_is_not_a_rider@faa61efd`, `tests/test_a_rider_reaches_its_file.py#test_the_marker_in_prose_or_a_string_is_not_a_rider@0e20656e`, `tests/test_a_rider_reaches_its_file.py#test_a_comment_block_runs_through_its_blank_comment_lines@5a9f4c40`, `tests/test_a_rider_reaches_its_file.py#test_a_second_rider_directly_under_the_first_is_its_own_rider@2bca64e7`, `tests/test_a_rider_reaches_its_file.py#test_a_second_rider_sharing_one_html_comment_is_its_own_rider@dbd41aed` | **Executed** 2026-09-08: the widened scan over six roots finds exactly 20 riders; a grep of the same roots finds **33** marker lines at `2f0dd02` and **36** at `677e10f`, so 13 extras and then 16, every one of them prose or a string literal. The count names the commit it was taken at because an aggregate is not a coordinate — the cell read 31 and 11 until round 2, taken at `eaa8030` and written after `923f86c` had planted the HTML opener in the case file twice (round 2, finding 12). All five cases red under mutation — dropping either comment-head requirement, stopping a block at the first line not beginning `# `, removing either closing rule, and dropping the markdown guard, which turns `## RIDER: …` into a stampless rider and the check to exit 2 | 2026-09-08 | **This list has been the defect twice.** `templates` was missing once; `.github` and `tests` were missing until now and left three riders held by nothing, one of which had never carried a stamp in any form. The line it draws is not *code* — `agents/smith.md` and `skills/implement/SKILL.md` carry real riders — it is what this repository executes or ships, versus what describes the convention. The test imports the constant rather than restating it, so the checker and the case cannot disagree about what the corpus is. **Re-read 2026-09-08 in round 2's fix pass**: the reader was looser than its own docstring on the `#` side too. The docstring gives `#` to Python, YAML and shell and gives markdown the HTML comment; the code asked only that the stripped line start with `#`, so a markdown heading naming the marker was a rider with no stamp and CI exited 2 on it. That is the mirror of the HTML opener `923f86c` closed, and it is the one loss in this design that INVENTED an alarm instead of losing one — which is why the module docstring now says the asymmetry is load-bearing (round 2, finding 11) |
| S4 · the CHECK makes no git call; only `--migrate` does, and it consults the old stamp's commit before writing rather than after. A region that hashes the same at that commit as it does today keeps its ORIGINAL date; anything else is refused and NAMED — the refusal says which of three things failed, because the commit being gone, the commit being fine and the path not being in it, and git not running at all are three different repairs | `.github/scripts/rider_check.py#migrate@ed0b4579`, `.github/scripts/rider_check.py#content_at@4830e875`, `tests/test_a_rider_reaches_its_file.py#test_the_check_asks_git_for_nothing@32d11f9b`, `tests/test_a_rider_reaches_its_file.py#test_a_refusal_names_which_of_the_three_things_failed@ef2b8a17` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. `--migrate` moved 12 of 19 with their original dates proved and refused 7 — four with no enclosing unit, two whose unit had changed since it was stamped, and one whose stamp names a commit that predates its own file. The no-git case is executed rather than read: `git` is replaced on PATH with a script that records being called, and the case reads the record | 2026-09-08 | **The one refusal was read as the ticket's defect caught in the act, and it is not that.** `.github/scripts/fold_ledger.py` was stamped `881fb0f`; git resolves that commit, it is an ancestor of HEAD, and `fold_ledger.py` was simply not in that tree — a stamp that was wrong when it was written (round 1, finding 6). This migration never observed the squash orphaning a stamp. `hooks/root-migrate.py` at `4f78074` is the same shape, found by re-enumerating rather than by a second finding, and it was refused one step earlier for having no enclosing unit. **That case verified nothing in its first form.** The tripwire returned a failing exit code and nothing read it — `subprocess.run` with `capture_output` does not raise — so a `git status` added to the check path left the exit code untouched and the case stayed green. The general shape: a tripwire whose signal the code under test may ignore is not a tripwire. The split itself is `evidence_check.py`'s, taken deliberately: a one-shot writer may consult the commit a stamp recorded, and a checker may not, because that call is the thing a squash reaches |

So a round record's SHA already survived the merge rule that orphaned a rider's, and the two were never one mechanism with different corpora. The rider check had one call, `git merge-base --is-ancestor <sha> HEAD`, and no fallback. **Corrected in round 1's fix pass**: this row and five other places named `refs/pull/<N>/head`, the REMOTE spelling, where `PULL_HEADS` is `refs/remotes/pull/`. Executed: `git for-each-ref 'refs/pull/*/head'` returns 0 refs in a working clone and the mirror returns 86, so a reader checking the ground the way it was written reads a true ground as false. The ground itself is unchanged — 116 of 117 squashed target SHAs still resolve through the fallback | 2026-09-10 | Two further grounds, and each stands alone. A `Target SHA` records a MOMENT while a rider stamp is a live pointer, and `skills/implement/SKILL.md` makes that difference the reason round records may sit beside the contract at all — re-anchoring it to content would make it assert a present state. And a reviewed tree is a whole commit across every file, so there is no anchor to write: the migration is not merely unnecessary here, it has no target. 135 records carry the row, which is why the answer is written where somebody counting them will find it **Re-read 2026-09-09 by work item 1788912166 (#296, #295, #297).** The template's `Broad gate` row gained the sentence saying the cell is read at a ready pull request, which moved the field table's anchor. `Target SHA`'s exemption and the three grounds beneath it are untouched, and the claim holds. **Re-read 2026-09-10 in work item 1789002694 (#30), phase 4.** The template's `Broad gate` row gained the sentence naming its writer, which moved the field table's anchor again. `Target SHA`'s exemption and the three grounds beneath it are untouched. |
| S6 · `--reverify` writes a rider's HASH wherever it has moved and its DATE only beside a hash that actually moved. A region hashing to exactly what its stamp records was not re-read by that run, so today's date over the recorded one asserts a reading nobody performed — the same half-truth `--migrate` refuses to manufacture, arriving from the writer instead. An `--only` that selects no rider at all is REFUSED by path and exits 1, and an `--only` with no `--reverify` to scope is refused before anything is read and exits 2, because a clean total at exit 0 reads as nothing needed doing | `.github/scripts/rider_check.py#reverify@aac8e0e2`, `.github/scripts/rider_check.py#main@ad761ced`, `tests/test_a_rider_reaches_its_file.py#test_reverify_says_so_when_only_selects_no_rider@d85daa6a`, `tests/test_a_rider_reaches_its_file.py#test_only_without_a_verb_is_refused_rather_than_ignored@f3d87a8e`, `tests/test_a_rider_reaches_its_file.py#test_reverify_does_not_move_a_date_whose_hash_did_not_move@6e5b9bd8`, `tests/test_a_rider_reaches_its_file.py#test_reverify_still_moves_the_date_of_a_rider_that_did_change@8f60d1d9` | **Executed** 2026-09-08.
