Round 1's findings 1, 2 and 3 are one defect seen at three distances: the dates in the tree, the condition that erased them, and the second checker disagreeing with the first. **Re-read 2026-09-08 in round 2's fix pass**: the same command reported success for selecting nothing. `--reverify --only hooks/wortree-guard.py` — one letter out of the path the drift message prints — gave `0 restamped · 0 refused` and exit 0 on the real tree, and exit 0 is the answer a script reads, so a typo read as *nothing needed doing* (round 2, finding 10). Executed after the fix: that path and an absolute one both refuse and exit 1, and `--reverify --only hooks/worktree-guard.py` still exits 0 on an unchanged tree. **Re-enumerating that class found three more instances in `main`**, all executed: `--only PATH` with no verb, `--only <a path that does not exist>` with no verb, and `--migrate --only PATH` each ignored the argument, read the whole tree and printed `20 ok · 0 drifted · 0 broken` or `0 migrated · 0 refused` at exit 0 — success for a run that did nothing the person asked for. All three now exit 2 with a sentence, and both cases seen red before the guard went in |

## 0.9.2 — 2026-09-08

<!-- specs/1788844127-the-reviewers-report-reaches-the-record-retyped -->
### 1788844127-the-reviewers-report-reaches-the-record-retyped

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 · `round_record.py new` reads the reviewer's report from `<item>/rounds/round-N-report.md` when `--report` is absent, derived from `--item` and `--round` — the same pair `round-N.md` itself is derived from, one function away, so the reviewer and the generator cannot spell the path differently from each other. The flag stays and it wins | `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#report_path@5d2fdf66`, `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#REPORT_NAME@14962cbc`, `tests/test_the_reviewers_report_reaches_the_record.py#test_the_default_reads_the_report_the_reviewer_left@2d61c9bf`, `tests/test_the_reviewers_report_reaches_the_record.py#test_the_flag_still_wins_over_the_conventional_path@bc11d937` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. Both cases red against `--report required=True` first, where the run exits 2 on argparse before it reaches anything they assert. Four mutations of the two added units, all killed — including `report_path` ignoring `given`, which is the one mutation the flag case exists for and the one thing no run against the old code could have shown, because the flag's behaviour is unchanged | 2026-09-08 | **The flag has two live callers and both are real**, which is why removing it was never on the table: a round whose report predates the convention, and a round that ran more than one reviewer, where the orchestrator hands each its own path rather than letting the second overwrite the first. That second case is also what keeps `skills/code-review/SKILL.md`'s *parallel writers overwrite each other* true of the report as well as of the record |
| S2 · the absence of both the flag and the file refuses, and the message names the path it looked at AND the convention that fills it | `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#report_path@5d2fdf66`, `tests/test_the_reviewers_report_reaches_the_record.py#test_the_absence_names_the_path_and_the_convention@916ad62c` | **Executed** 2026-09-08, red against the old code first (argparse exits 2 with a different message). The mutation dropping the `agents/warden.md` clause from the sentence is killed | 2026-09-08 | **`read_text`'s own refusal names the path and would have been enough for a typo.** It is not enough here, because nobody typed this path — it was derived — so a bare path reads as a mistyped argument, which is the one thing it cannot be. <!-- specs/1788844200-the-refusal-text-is-unobserved-and-an-uppercase-v-is-invisible -->
### 1788844200-the-refusal-text-is-unobserved-and-an-uppercase-v-is-invisible

<!-- specs/1788844200-the-refusal-text-is-unobserved-and-an-uppercase-v-is-invisible -->

<!-- One work item's rows. No header — `fold_ledger.py` writes the `###` at
the release and moves this file into `seal/ledger.md`.

Four rows for four tickets, and they are genuinely four claims rather than one
split up. S1 is a behaviour change to the token. S2 is what the refusal's text
is now held by. S3 and S4 are corrections to records, so each says what was
false as well as what is true — the corrections to the existing `seal/ledger.md`
rows they answer are made in those rows, because a claim corrected only in a
fragment leaves the false one standing where a reader will find it first. -->

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 · a version-shaped token is read with an uppercase or lowercase `v` prefix alike, and **both lookarounds are unchanged** — a preceding word character still refuses the token, because a word before a version renames it (`py3.13.9` is CPython's) where a suffix qualifies the same version (`0.9.0rc1` is a prerelease of it), and a preceding dot still refuses it because that makes the match the tail of a longer number. The argument for each of those two characters is written beside the constant, which is what #204 asks for before the hole is decided | `tests/test_release_hygiene.py#VERSION_TOKEN@c629af98`, `tests/test_release_hygiene.py#test_an_uppercase_v_is_a_prefix_and_not_a_preceding_word@42e3490b` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. Enumerated over all 64 loaded files with both patterns: the widening admits exactly one token and loses none, and the offender list is empty before and after. The case was seen red against the unwidened `v?` — 1 failed, 30 passed — before it was committed as a case | 2026-09-08 | **The ticket's measured fact is stale, and re-executing it is what showed that.** #204 records at `7349afc` that no instance of the invisible shape exists in the loaded set. One exists now: `docs/flow.md` writes `an uppercase V0.9.0 is invisible` in the row describing this very work item. It is still not an offender, for two independent reasons rather than the ticket's one — that file is in `RECORDS_OF_A_MOMENT`, and the version is below the running one and so is history either way. **`as_release` was deliberately left alone**: its `lstrip("v")` does not strip an uppercase `V`, and nothing hands it one — `timers_in` passes the bare `match.group(1)`, and the other-product lookup is string equality on `match.group(0)` — so widening it would be speculative and would drift a ledger anchor for a claim nobody is making |
| S2 · the text the version check prints is read WHOLE, element by element, where an element is a leaf of the expression `refusal` returns rather than a reading of it: the opening constant, the running version it interpolates, the timer paragraph together with the `"\n  "` that closes it, the joined refused lines, the blank line, and the routes | `tests/test_release_hygiene.py#refusal@2035da87`, `tests/test_release_hygiene.py#test_the_refusal_prints_every_piece_it_builds@e69e9c6b` | **Executed** 2026-09-08, and re-derived in review round 1's fix pass. `ast.parse` of the returned expression: the `+` chain flattens to four operands and the first is one `JoinedStr` of three parts — **six leaves, not seven**. Thirteen mutations, one at a time, `tests/__pycache__` cleared between and the file restored from bytes read before the first write: each of the six leaves deleted whole; the two deletions inside the timer paragraph that round 1 found surviving; the `"\n  "` closing it; the join's own separator; the check's `assert` handed a literal; and the routes emitted before the refused lines in two shapes. Eleven turned a case red — deleting the routes turns two — and the two that did not are named beside | 2026-09-08 | **Seven was a reading, and calling it a construction is what let it stand.** The old count split the third `JoinedStr` part into a paragraph and a closing separator, and promoted the `"\n  "` the join is called ON — the receiver, `func.value`, where `args` holds `offenders` alone — to an element of its own. A bare `ast.walk` does return that string, so it is a leaf of the TREE and not of the expression: the flattening rule is what puts it inside the join rather than beside it, which is why the count of six is stated after that rule and not before (review round 3). So the count reached seven while the paragraph was read at its two ends only: deleting `it goes red on the day that version ships, on the release's own `, or just `preparation commit, `, each left the module at 32 passed, and 86 characters between the two assertions were read by nothing. Every leaf is read whole now, the paragraph by two contiguous halves. **The two survivors are measured, and neither is a limit.** **A third class is outside that set by construction**: nothing pins that nothing was ADDED. A sentence inserted at the end of the timer paragraph, or a line inserted before the routes, leaves the module at 32 passed (round 2, re-measured round 3). The pin is one assertion over the concatenation of the pieces the case already reads — measured green unmutated and red on either insertion in round 3 — and it is declined because that concatenation is `refusal` rebuilt in the test, which `plan.md` rejected. The check's own `assert not offenders, refusal(running, offenders)` handed a literal leaves the module at 32 passed — and it is PINNABLE, which is the third false limit this claim has carried: swapping the module globals `tracked` and `timers_in` raises the check and its message compares equal to `refusal(running, offenders)`, measured in the round with no source read. The other is a reordering that carries every separator along with the piece it precedes; the naive swap, which leaves the separators where they are, is caught by the assertion reading `"\n  "` before the first refused line. Pinning the order fully means rebuilding `refusal` in the test, which `plan.md` rejected |
| S3 · the exemption list's order-dependence was a property of **exact-path entries alone**. A narrower `/` entry written after a wider one changes no answer in either implementation, because every `/` entry takes the same `DATED_RECORD.match(basename)` check and only `rel == entry` skips it | `tests/test_release_hygiene.py#test_the_exemption_list_does_not_depend_on_the_order_it_is_written_in@8af7db05` | **Executed** 2026-09-08, four arrangements through both implementations and in both orders. The exact-path one is the only row where they differ (`any()` True, early-return False) and the only one whose answer moves when the entries are written the other way round. The early-return implementation is RECONSTRUCTED from the docstring describing it, because the branch carrying it was reverted and its intermediate commits are not in this clone | 2026-09-08 | **No assertion was added**, and that is the claim rather than an omission: asserting an arrangement that changes no answer is the failure this correction is about, and it is the class `#51`'s observation 6 counts. The correction is made in the case docstring and in `seal/ledger.md`'s R1 note, which is where the false reason was written; `is_a_record_of_a_moment`'s own docstring says *an exact path* and was already right |
| S4 · the tracker document states what the version check refuses the way the check behaves — at or above the running one, and below it kept as history. **Two documents state that rule, not three** | `docs/issues-and-milestones.md#"## A label answers *what it is about*, and survives the move"@993881ca` | **Read** 2026-09-08, and corrected in review round 1's fix pass. `docs/release-checklist.md`'s table row states the rule correctly and was left alone. **`docs/flow.md`'s sentence about #179 does NOT state it**, and was left alone for a different reason: *"goes red on the commit that raises the version … and nowhere earlier"* describes the equality check #179 REPLACED, written as scheduling rationale for why the ticket was forced into that release, and it is correct as that. This row used to cite that sentence as a third document already stating the rule | 2026-09-08 | The half that was missing is what protects the sentence further down the same document saying which release an issue shipped in — a rule refusing every version this repository has ever shipped would have refused that sentence, which is #179's second candidate and why it was not taken. **A row that cites the wrong document as its agreement is the same class as the count above**: the claim was checkable and nobody opened it until the round did. **Nothing pins the two documents agreeing.** A case asserting it would be new mechanism in a fix scoped to prose, so it is carried as a follow-up with an answerer rather than built here |

<!-- specs/1788844300-the-guards-cases-cannot-observe-what-they-guard -->
### 1788844300-the-guards-cases-cannot-observe-what-they-guard

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 · `reader()` has FIVE arms — `spec is None`, `spec.loader is None`, and an except tuple of three — of which FOUR are reachable and each takes a different input, and each reachable one has a parameter of its own. The fifth, `spec.loader is None`, has no constructible input and is defence in depth | `hooks/review-history-guard.py#reader@20048595`, `tests/test_chain_hooks.py#test_the_guard_falls_back_to_the_raw_text_without_the_reader@504b6136` | **Executed** 2026-09-08 at `8aedf11`: 29 passed. Both new parameters seen red on their own arm — deleting `SyntaxError` from the except tuple fails `[a reader that does not parse]` alone, deleting `ImportError` fails `[a reader whose own import is missing]` alone, each leaving the other 28 green. `spec.loader is None` executed against nine inputs: a directory, a `.txt`, an extensionless file, an empty string and a missing `.txt` all return `spec is None`; a `.py`, a `.pyc`, a `.so`, a missing `.py` and a DIRECTORY named `x.py` all return a spec with a real loader. Nothing makes `spec` truthy while its loader is falsy | 2026-09-08 | **The arms were enumerated out of the function's own source, and that is what found the second one.** #209 named `SyntaxError` and tabled four arms; walking `reader`'s AST for `ExceptHandler` and `If` nodes gives five, because the except tuple has THREE members and every prose reading of it had counted two. `ImportError` is reached by a reader that parses and imports something this interpreter does not have — the same truncated copy of the plugin as `SyntaxError`, one line further in — and no case, no ledger row and no ticket had named it. Without its arm the input raises `ModuleNotFoundError` out of `reader()`, through `is_closed`, into the hook's `main()`, which is the one thing R5's own argument says must never happen. **The honest close differs by constructibility**: an arm with a reachable input gets a parameter, an arm with none gets a sentence in the docstring saying so and naming the nine inputs that proved it. Writing a case for `spec.loader is None` would mean reaching past `spec_from_file_location` to build a spec by hand, which pins the test's own construction rather than the hook's behaviour |
| T2 · what a closing word can be hidden by is **derived** from `readable`'s own source and never restated: the parametrization of the hidden-closing-word case is compared with the passes `readable` composes, so a pass added to the reader fails the case rather than arriving unguarded | `skills/verify/scripts/unverified_check.py#readable@5ef11fb3`, `tests/test_chain_hooks.py#reader_blanking_passes@35f648e0`, `tests/test_chain_hooks.py#HIDDEN_CLOSING_WORD@f9440b6e`, `tests/test_chain_hooks.py#test_a_closing_word_a_reader_blanks_is_not_a_closing_note@a28ef7df` | **Executed** 2026-09-08 at `8aedf11`. The mutation is the deliverable: a third blanking pass added to `readable` — inline code spans blanked — turns both parameters red with the tie in place, and leaves **29 passed, exit 0** with the tie assertion deleted and nothing else changed. That control is #210's finding reproduced in this tree | 2026-09-08 | This is `seal/ledger.md` F1's standard applied one module over — *the parametrized case reads its own parametrization back out of the constant, so the case list cannot fall behind it* — and F1's Notes name the failure this removes: *nothing goes red when it does, so the silence looks exactly like correctness*. The constant is keyed by the pass's own NAME rather than by prose, which is what makes the comparison possible at all; the prose the keys used to carry (*a fenced block*, *an HTML comment*) is in the comment above them. **The derivation is by name and by definition**: the passes are the calls `readable` makes to `ast.Name` targets that are functions of the reader module, so `text.splitlines()` drops out as an attribute call and a builtin like `list` drops out as having no function on the module. **Dropping attribute calls is where the derivation cannot see, which is round 1's 🟡 2**: a pass written as `_SPAN_RE.sub(...)` blanks text just as well and is an attribute call too — executed, that pass leaves the module at 30 passed, exit 0 while `is_closed` on a record whose only closing word sits in an inline span flips True to False, which is #210 reproduced with the tie in place, and it is the shape both #210 and round 3 used as their example. The derivation now REFUSES an attribute call other than `splitlines` instead of narrowing quietly, and `test_a_blanking_pass_written_as_a_sub_is_refused_rather_than_unseen` is what watches that refusal — without the assertion the fake reader in it derives one name, nothing raises, and no case says a word. **The tie goes red for two causes and the repairs differ**: a pass ADDED wants a new key, a pass RENAMED wants the existing key re-keyed, and adding one for a rename leaves three keys against two passes and the assertion still red — executed both ways. The message said only *add the pass, keyed by its name*, which is the one repair a renamer must not make; it now names both, and `test_the_ties_message_answers_a_rename_as_well_as_an_addition` keeps the second half from being taken back. **The tidy-up is deleting the tie as a tautology** — it compares a dict in the test file with something read out of another module, which reads as testing the copy. It is the only thing tying the case list to the reader, and without it the reader can grow a pass while the parametrization does not, which is exactly the state #210 was filed about. **The failure direction is safe either way**: a further blanking pass can only make `is_closed` return `False` more often, which is the reminder firing rather than staying silent, so this is case-strength and not a defect |
| T3 · `is_closed` with no records at all answers closed, so a work item whose `rounds/` directory holds no record is not read as one whose rows were never drained | `hooks/review-history-guard.py#is_closed@24b27511`, `tests/test_chain_hooks.py#test_no_records_at_all_is_not_an_unclosed_directory@99c904e6` | **Executed** 2026-09-08 at `57e9603`: 30 passed. Seen red on its own arm — mutating `if not records: return True` to `return False` fails this case alone, 29 green. The same mutation before the case existed left all 29 green | 2026-09-08 | **Neither ticket named this arm; enumerating `is_closed` from its source did.** Its four arms were mutated one at a time: `except OSError` kills four cases, `if seen is not None` kills four, `CLOSED_RE` kills two, and `if not records` killed nothing. It is unreachable from `main()`, which calls `is_closed` only behind `if records` — so it is defence in depth like T1's fifth arm. It parts from that one on constructibility: `is_closed([])` is a one-line call, and where an arm can be reached directly the honest close is a case rather than a sentence. **The direction is why it is worth pinning.** Mutated to `return False`, the pre-merge reminder fires at every merge for a work item with an empty `rounds/` directory — the state most work items are in, and the state this hook's own docstring says must stay quiet |
| T4 · `gh_segments` absorbs a `ValueError` from `shlex.split`, so a `gh` command with a pipe inside a quoted string leaves the hook silent rather than raising into the session's Bash call | `hooks/review-history-guard.py#gh_segments@70508455`, `tests/test_chain_hooks.py#test_an_unbalanced_quote_in_a_piped_gh_command_does_not_stop_the_session@fdb5a332` | **Executed** 2026-09-08: 32 passed with the arm in place. Seen red on its own arm — deleting `except ValueError` fails this case alone, 31 green. Before the case existed the same deletion left this module at 30 passed and all seven modules that reference the hook at 270 passed, exit 0, while the hook fed a real PostToolUse payload for `gh pr view 123 --json comments` piped into `jq` exited 1 with `ValueError: No closing quotation` | 2026-09-08 | **Round 1's 🟡 1, and the branch's own method applied where the branch stopped.** T1 and T3 enumerated `reader` and `is_closed` out of their own source; the same walk over the file's other two functions gives `gh_segments` five arms and `main` nineteen, and thirteen of those were watched by nothing — every one of `gh_segments`' own, measured with this branch's two `gh` cases deselected. This is one of THREE arms of `gh_segments` whose failure stops a session rather than misfiling a reminder, which is the floor `hooks/review-history-guard.py`'s own docstring sets. The other two are the index guards `i < len(toks)` in the `while` and in the `if`: `SEG_RE` splits on a newline, so any multi-line command leaves an empty segment and an empty token list, and without either guard the hook exits 1 with `IndexError` on an ordinary command. All three are covered by one parametrized case rather than three — `main()`'s single `try` covers `json.load` alone, so anything `gh_segments` raises leaves the hook. The command that does it is not exotic: `SEG_RE` splits on `|`, and the READ branch's own documented example piped into `jq` puts a `|` inside a quoted string. The remaining nine arms are deferred to #262 rather than pinned one by one — a case each closes today's list and not the class, which is the failure this whole work item is about, and the durable close is a checker that enumerates arms and mutates them. A fix pass may add the unit that pins a finding; it may not add mechanism |
| T5 · `gh_segments` has THREE arms whose failure leaves the hook and stops the session's Bash call, not one — `except ValueError` and both `i < len(toks)` index guards — and ONE parametrized case covers all three, because they share one input class: a segment `shlex` cannot reduce to a first token | `hooks/review-history-guard.py#gh_segments@70508455`, `tests/test_chain_hooks.py#test_no_segment_of_a_bash_command_raises_out_of_gh_segments@090594b2` | **Executed** 2026-09-08: 38 passed, exit 0 as shipped. Each of the three arms mutated in turn, and the parameters split by arm — deleting `except ValueError` fails the two quoted-pipe parameters, deleting either `i < len(toks)` fails the other three, each leaving 35 green — two of them leave a segment whose token list is empty, and `FOO=bar` leaves one token the env-assignment prefix arm consumes, so `i` reaches `len(toks)` either way. All six of `gh_segments`' branch decisions were then mutated one at a time against this module: four now red — both index guards, `except ValueError`, and `basename(…) == "gh"` — and two still green, the env-assignment prefix arm and the `WRAPPERS` arm | 2026-09-08 | **Round 2's 🟡 1, and it corrected a claim rather than the code.** Both index guards were in place and correct; what was false was the sentence beside the fix pass calling `except ValueError` the ONLY survivor whose failure stops a session. `SEG_RE` splits on a newline as well as on `|`, so any multi-line Bash command leaves a trailing empty segment whose token list is empty and `toks[i]` falls off it — fed a real `PostToolUse` payload with either guard deleted, the hook exits 1 with `IndexError` on `gh pr view 1 --json comments` with a trailing newline, on `echo hi;` and on `FOO=bar`. The false sentence had reached three carriers — T4's Notes, `overview.md`, and issue #262's body, which is the ticket that decides when the rest get watched — and all three are corrected. **One case rather than three is contract §12**: the three arms share one input class and one assertion, so this is not the eleven-hand-written-cases shape #262 declines. The case closed `basename(…) == "gh"` as a by-product — `echo hi;` watches it, and `FOO=bar` does not, because the prefix arm has already left `i == len(toks)` and the widened test is False either way — which leaves `gh_segments` with one unwatched arm under round 1's five-way enumeration, two under the six-way split this cell uses, and it stays with #262 |

<!-- specs/1788844400-a-body-naming-two-issues-claims-one -->
### 1788844400-a-body-naming-two-issues-claims-one

#### A body naming two issues claims one

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A pull request body's issue numbers split into what it CLAIMS and what it merely mentions, and a sentence that claims one number while naming another beside it earns a warning and nothing else | `.github/scripts/issue_claims_check.py#read@3cdf637f` | **Executed** against PR #162's live body (`gh pr view 162 --json body`): `claimed #153` · `mentioned only #150, #163, #159, #161, #160, #158` · one warning quoting the sentence · exit 0. The one measured instance, and the check's acceptance case. **Executed** by mutation for the per-number set: removing it fails `test_the_same_number_twice_in_one_sentence_is_one_warning`, and hoisting it out of the segment loop fails `test_the_same_number_in_two_sentences_earns_a_warning_each` | 2026-09-08 | The repair is NOT a wider `CLOSING`. Widening it would close issues GitHub does not, and the script and the platform would then disagree about what a body means — worse than the loss. The body is not committed as a fixture: it carries a session URL on a domain outside the fixture allowlist |
| A segment ends at `.!?;` before whitespace, at a blank line, or at a new markdown block — and NOT at a single newline, because every body here is hard-wrapped and the defect arrives split across two lines | `.github/scripts/issue_claims_check.py#segments@8e8d6239`, `.github/scripts/issue_claims_check.py#SENTENCE_END@5e875e08`, `.github/scripts/issue_claims_check.py#BLOCK_START@33aff484` | **Executed** by mutation, one rule at a time: killing rule 1 fails `test_a_new_sentence_is_a_new_segment` and `test_a_trailing_full_stop_after_a_number_still_closes_the_segment`; killing rule 2 fails `test_a_blank_line_ends_the_segment` and the fence-splice case; killing rule 3 fails the list-item and table-row cases; the naive marker class fails `test_a_hard_wrapped_sentence_is_still_one_sentence`; removing the four whole-line break alternatives fails `test_a_horizontal_rule_ends_the_segment` at all four shapes, and dropping the whole-line anchor from any one of them fails `test_a_run_of_markers_inside_a_line_is_still_prose` at that shape | 2026-09-08 | The marker class asks for the space CommonMark requires. A bare `[-*+>|#]` reads `#22` at the start of a line as a heading, and `#22` at the start of a line IS this defect hard-wrapped — the one thing the module exists to see. Found by the case, not by reading. It also took the thematic break and the setext underline out of the class, which review round 1 found as the one false-positive direction the design forbids itself; four whole-line alternatives put them back. What the choice gives up: `e.g.` ends a segment, so the check under-reports rather than inventing a warning |
| Fenced blocks and code spans are blanked CHARACTER FOR CHARACTER, so a body quoting the failure shape reports nothing at all and the warning can still quote the sentence as written | `.github/scripts/issue_claims_check.py#prose_only@8074092b` | **Executed**: a body whose fence holds `Closes #11, #22` reports `none / none / no sentence…` at exit 0; collapsing each match to one space instead fails `test_the_warning_quotes_the_sentence_as_the_author_wrote_it`, and removing the masking fails the fence and span cases | 2026-09-08 | The sibling closer collapses to one space, which is right for a `findall` and wrong here — a fence collapsing to one space would splice the line above it onto the line below, and this module segments by line structure. Same two patterns, different substitution, and the difference is written at the call site |
| What GitHub reads has one definition: the check imports `KEYWORDS`, `CLOSING`, `FENCE` and `SPAN` from the release closer rather than restating them | `.github/scripts/issue_claims_check.py#ISSUE_REF@363ba191`, `tests/test_a_body_naming_two_issues_claims_one.py#test_the_check_imports_the_closers_definition_rather_than_restating_it@1358abfa` | **Executed**: giving the module its own `KEYWORDS`/`CLOSING`/`FENCE` turns 13 of 28 cases red. The identity case is taken from `sys.modules`, where the check's own import put the sibling — loading it a second time by path builds a second module object and `KEYWORDS is KEYWORDS` is then false for a module that imported it correctly | 2026-09-08 | `CLOSING is CLOSING` passes either way, because `re.compile` caches by pattern and flags and hands both loads the same object. Only the tuple actually asks the question, and that is what made the first run of this case red for the right reason. `ISSUE_REF` is the check's own — `#(\d+)\b`, so `#L45` and `#heading` are not issue numbers |
| The check never fails a pull request; its only non-zero exit is a step handed no body at all | `.github/scripts/issue_claims_check.py#main@b4fe57d1`, `.github/scripts/issue_claims_check.py#body_from@5465c14a` | **Executed**: `Closes #11 and #22`, an empty body and a body with no numbers all exit 0; no `--body-file` and no `PR_BODY` exits 2 with *examined nothing* on stderr. Both directions mutated red | 2026-09-08 | An EMPTY `PR_BODY` is a body — a pull request opened with no description is ordinary. An ABSENT one is a misconfigured workflow, and the alternative is a step that is green having read nothing, which is what `templates/hygiene.yml`'s own header argues against |

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 · the revision `--baseline REF` compares against is `git merge-base REF HEAD`, resolved once, and all three reads of the base take the resolved commit — the file-is-gone arm, the row-count arm and the missing-path arm | `skills/verify/scripts/unverified_check.py#merge_base@549a0929`, `skills/verify/scripts/unverified_check.py#main@f7c68793` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. The ticket's repair section says the other arms *"already read only this branch's own files"*, and reading them says otherwise — that claim is what `questions.md` Q1 records, with the row-count arm's own case as the evidence |
| R2 · `resolves(root, ref)` keeps its name, signature and predicate shape because `chain_check.py` loads this module and calls it by name; it is one reader over `commit_of` rather than a second rev-parse beside it | `skills/verify/scripts/unverified_check.py#commit_of@c581f1bd`, `skills/verify/scripts/unverified_check.py#resolves@7b34da1c` | **Executed** 2026-09-08: 303 cases across the five modules that load this reader (`chain_check`, `round_record`, the floor-and-depth pin, the last-round pin, `deferral_check`) pass unchanged. | R3 · a refusal names the revision it compared against in the shortest form that is TRUE: the ref alone where the merge base is the ref's own commit, and `the merge-base of <ref> and HEAD (<short>)` where the base has moved past the fork | `skills/verify/scripts/unverified_check.py#base_label@be240603` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. ### 1788873610-every-copy-out-of-raw-meets-the-hider-question

<!-- One work item's rows. No header — `fold_ledger.py` writes the `###` at
the release and moves this file into `seal/ledger.md`.

Two rows over a branch that is four new units and their cases. What earns a
row is a judgment whose repair looks obvious from the outside, and both of
these are exactly that: F6 says the guard is asked of the ARTEFACT and not of
each copy, which reads like the wrong end of the problem until you count the
copies; F7 says one boolean separates two refusals whose difference is the
whole of whether a reviewer is sent to look for something that is there.

The row numbering continues `seal/ledger.md`'s
`### 1788668335-a-fence-under-the-probes-table-closes-after-a-later-heading`
section, whose F1–F5 are about the same guard and whose F2 this work item
struck. A reader who opens F2 is pointed here; a reader who opens F6 is
pointed back.

Every hash here was stamped by `evidence-check --reverify --ledger '<this
fragment>'`, the scoped WRITE form. The nine drifted rows in `seal/ledger.md`
were re-read one at a time before that file was re-stamped, and each carries
what moved and why its claim stands. -->

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F6 · **the hider question is asked of the RECORD, not of the copies that made it, and that is what makes the enumeration a property rather than a list.** Every copy the generator makes out of `raw` lands in one artefact, so a record read back through the shared reader before it is written answers for every copy path at once — the three the (copy × hider) grid named, the fourth it did not (`inherited_rows`), and a fifth added later. `write_record` is the one function in the module that opens a file for writing, and the argument is a case that walks the module's own AST for every writer rather than a comment that lists them | `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#write_record@5675c920`, `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#RECORD_HIDERS@1ca05983`, `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#hiders_close@3274429d`, `tests/test_the_record_is_generated.py#test_every_record_this_writes_is_read_back_before_it_is_written@6fdc64dd`, `tests/test_the_record_is_generated.py#test_a_comment_a_copied_cell_takes_half_of_writes_no_record@44983d85`, `tests/test_the_record_is_generated.py#test_a_flag_carrying_an_open_comment_writes_no_record@443ff6da`, `tests/test_the_fixes_close_the_record.py#test_close_reads_back_the_record_it_writes@331eac6c` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. Seen red at `8114937` by probe: a report whose `Grounds` cell opens a comment and closes it on the line below is accepted, `new` exits **0**, the record is written, and `## Executed probes`, `## Inherited coordinates` and `## Deferred` each resolve to **0** occurrences through the shared reader while standing in the bytes — nothing in the run says a word, and `chain_check` reads only `## Verdicts` among sections. Green at `2125094`. Four mutations, each alone and restored from kept bytes: `write_record` not asking turns three cases red, `close` writing directly turns two, `reach_back` writing directly turns one, `opens_at` returning 0 turns three | 2026-09-08 | **The tidy-up is asking the question of each SOURCE instead** — of every earlier record `inherited_rows` reads, of the `--fixes` file — which is the fourth row the grid was missing, and it reads more direct because the message can then name the file a person edits. Two things are against it. It is a whole-text question on an input read for named sections, so it refuses a file this repository already has: the `` `&lt;!--` `` inside a code span at `seal/specs/1788826000-a-stamp-names-content-not-a-commit/rounds/round-1-fixes.md` blanks that file's tail and hides nothing `fix_table` reads. And narrowed to the section it becomes `swallowed` parameterised over three constants — a refactor of the one function five rows of the section above name — for a copy the reader's own arithmetic already refuses loudly. **The second tidy-up is dropping the input questions as duplicates** now that the destination is checked. They are not duplicates and they are not guards: `write_record` refuses either way, and what the report's and the paragraph's own questions buy is a refusal naming the text a person can edit rather than the artefact they cannot. Deleting either turns no case red on the refusal and every case red on the message. **The third is `opens_at`'s prefix walk**, which is quadratic and reads like something to replace with a scan for `&lt;!--` and a fence regex. A second reading of where a marker sits is the check/copy asymmetry this module has been bitten by three times (round 1's 🔴 1, round 2's 🔴 7, and #182's own 🟡 11); a prefix walk calls the reader's own pass and cannot drift from it. A record is a few hundred lines |
| F7 · **one boolean separates a fence nobody closed from a comment crossing a fence, and it is the fence question asked of the RAW text.** A comment that opens inside a fenced block and closes outside it blanks that block's closing fence, because `strip_comments` runs first — so the fence question, asked of the comment-stripped text, finds a block with no closer while every fence in the text as written closes. Without the second reading the refusal names a fence the reviewer did close, which is §14's defect and the one round 2 of the chain above paid for once already | `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#open_hider@280da7fc`, `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#COMMENT_CROSSES_A_FENCE@2083a584`, `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#ASKED_COMMENT_CROSSES_A_FENCE@9ec1d060`, `tests/test_the_record_is_generated.py#test_a_comment_that_crosses_a_fence_names_the_comment@fe2a88e4`, `tests/test_the_record_is_generated.py#test_a_comment_that_crosses_a_fence_in_the_round_paragraph@f00e070c` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. Seen red at `8114937` by probe: exit 2 reading *a fenced block in the report is never closed*, with `blank_fences` over the report as written leaving no fence open. Green at `2125094`. Two mutations, each alone and restored from kept bytes: removing the `STRADDLE` branch turns exactly the two cases above red and no other; asking the fence question first turns exactly the two order cases red | 2026-09-08 | **The tidy-up is deleting the second `blank_fences` call as redundant** — it reads like asking the same question twice, and the two calls do read the same pass. They read two TEXTS, which is the distinction the whole module turns on and the one three findings on the chain above were about. **The second tidy-up is asking the comment question after the fence question**, which reads as the same check in a different order. It is not: an open comment blanks the closing fence of every block below it. Since `open_hider` grew a third answer this is no longer caught by the message saying `HTML comment` — the straddle sentence says that too — so both order cases gained an assertion on `never closed`, which is what separates a comment that never closes from one that closes outside a block. **The third is folding the two straddle sentences into one** and dropping the per-text wording. The message is the whole value here, and a reviewer told *the report* looks in a different file from one told *the round paragraph* |

### 1788873620-two-in-range-values-make-one-that-is-not

#### What the cost meter can read

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Every site in `session_cost.py` that converts a number to an `int` is discharged, and the class is enumerated from the module's own syntax tree rather than from a list anybody maintains. Membership is decided by execution on both halves of the owner's own wording — the operand is consumed through one of Python's six integer-conversion methods, and the callable answers a benign number with an exact `int` | `tests/test_a_derived_number_reaching_an_int_carries_a_guard.py#classify@65a70a12`, `tests/test_a_derived_number_reaching_an_int_carries_a_guard.py#consumes_an_integer@5c7bf658`, `skills/verify/scripts/session_cost.py#token_thirds@25a4cd22` | **Executed** at `1ccfbb9`: 38 cases in the new module, 74 with `tests/test_session_cost.py`, exit 0. Seen red where it must be — the shipped module with its finiteness guard removed, and the shipped module with one new unguarded `int(sum(spans))` added in a unit nobody thought to look at, each naming the site with its unit. Nineteen-plus constructed shapes classify the accepted and the refused apart, including a converter defined in the module under walk, an `import math as m` alias and a `from math import trunc` bare name — the two forms round 3 of #175 recorded as a name list's blind spots, closed here with no name list and no alias table because what is resolved is the binding and what is probed is the object | 2026-09-08 | The one member today is the `round` in `token_thirds`, so the value is in what the check refuses NEXT rather than in what it finds now. A **44-mutation battery** was run one mutation at a time against both files, and 43 are caught; the single survivor is `Converted`'s base class, which records before it raises, so no case can tell `BaseException` from `Exception` — its docstring says so rather than claiming otherwise. Five of the battery's first-pass survivors were units with no case behind them and each now has one; two were code rather than missing cases (`integer_shaped` excluded `bool`, which would have refused `round(True)`, and the cannot-raise discharge had no shape behind it) Re-read 2026-09-09 for #200 · #202 · #193, which changed four units this row anchors on; the claim is unaffected and the hash is the new content of the same units. |
| What a conversion raises is probed against the values `count` admits rather than written down, so no pair of exception classes is hardcoded: a `try` guard has to cover what the probe actually saw, and a conversion that cannot raise says so and needs no guard | `tests/test_a_derived_number_reaching_an_int_carries_a_guard.py#raised_by@6e535ba6`, `tests/test_a_derived_number_reaching_an_int_carries_a_guard.py#discharge@be09cf18` | **Executed** at `1ccfbb9`: `round` answers `{OverflowError, ValueError}` for one argument — `OverflowError` on an infinity, `ValueError` on a `NaN` — and a `try` catching only the first is refused, which is a shape in the table. A `TypeError` is excluded and the case says why: `round(3.0, inf)` raises it because `ndigits` must be an index, so a derived value in that slot reports on the probe's own filler and not on the conversion. A converter that guards itself internally reports no hazard and is discharged without a second guard | 2026-09-08 | The same filler cuts the other way, and the case states it: with a benign float in an index slot every call raises `TypeError` first, so a two-argument conversion's hazards come from the slot the probe can still reach. That is a bound on the probe, not on the class |
| Three shapes the property does not reach, each measured rather than assumed, and the first two carried in `token_thirds`' own docstring so a next editor of that file meets them | `skills/verify/scripts/session_cost.py#count@d43c2dd2`, `tests/test_a_derived_number_reaching_an_int_carries_a_guard.py#operator_converts@acfb84f1` | **Executed** at `1ccfbb9`. (1) A **subscript** bound converts through `__index__` and is excluded: telling a derived bound from `len(inputs) // 3` needs provenance, and `by_family[key][0]` indexes with a local name the walk cannot see the value of. (2) A true **division** on two derived integers raises `OverflowError` and converts nothing, so it is not a member; the wider *raises* predicate that would catch it also flags `share`'s `part / whole * 100`, unguarded today with duration operands. (3) `math.isfinite` consumes its operand through `__index__` — which is why it raises `OverflowError` on an integer with no float of its own, round 2 of #175's 🔴, still guarded by the `try` in `count` — and answers with a `bool`, so it is not a conversion TO an int. The exclusion is asserted on both halves rather than left incidental | 2026-09-08 | (1) and (2) are `questions.md` Q1 and Q2 for the repository owner; neither blocks. The operator dimension is a derived **no** rather than an assertion: every operator the module writes is unparsed from its own node and probed, no numeric operator reaches an int through an operand, and a positive control shows sequence repetition does — the one route a numeric probe cannot see, and the reason `2 * third` must not be read as one |

<!-- specs/1788873640-a-corrected-sentence-survives-elsewhere-and-nothing-looks -->
### 1788873640-a-corrected-sentence-survives-elsewhere-and-nothing-looks

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 · a survivor's score is counted in **independent runs of shared wording**, not in n-grams, and one run is worth at most 1.0 — so a floor above 1.0 is what requires two independent phrases, and no second constant says so | `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#runs@a0aaf0d2`, `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#weigh@a0428ae0`, `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#FLOOR@f3f49a4d` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. The first calibration's clearest false positive shared *be a second reader of the*: one six-word run, which at `n = 3` is four overlapping trigrams, each scored on its own for about 6 bits and clearing 26 between them. Scoring maximal runs by their rarest n-gram drops it to one piece of evidence. Both real survivors share exactly two runs that do not touch — *the join's … argument* with *never a leaf*, and *from this report once* with *has verified its findings*. Mutated: every n-gram made its own run, and a run summed instead of taken at its rarest — each turns a case red | 2026-09-08 | **`SHARED_FLOOR = 2` was a constant that could not change an answer, and a mutation sweep is what found it.** Setting it to 1 left the module green, because a one-run candidate is capped at 1.0 and the floor is 1.6. It is removed rather than pinned: it told a reader the independence requirement lived in a count, and while it existed it hid that dropping the corpus scale lets a one-run coincidence through |
| S2 · the score's unit is **one phrase that occurs nowhere else**, so the floor means the same thing whatever the corpus size, and a one-file corpus reports nothing rather than dividing by zero | `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#weights@1c14bffd`, `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#FLOOR@f3f49a4d` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. The score began as raw `log2(F / df)` bits with a floor of 15, calibrated against this repository's 633 files. A probe repository of two files scored a survivor plainly standing there at 2 bits — the most any phrase can be worth in a two-file corpus is `log2(2)` — so the constant measured THIS repository's size and every smaller repository would have been silently exempt. Dividing by `log2(F)` puts a phrase unique in the corpus at exactly 1.0 at corpus sizes 3, 633 and 100000. Mutated: the scale dropped, and `pool_size == 1` scored instead of refused — each turns a case red | 2026-09-08 | The floor is **1.6**, calibrated over 77 real ranges — every commit of five unsquashed work-item branches that carried a review chain. The curve: 80 reports at 1.0, 34 at 1.4, 26 at 1.6, 20 at 1.8, 14 at 2.0. The two known survivors score 1.89 and 1.79, so anything above 1.79 loses #267's shared-file row; 1.6 keeps 0.19 of room below the weaker at a cost of six reports over 77 ranges |
| S3 · a sentence is **corrected** where the file holds it fewer times at `b` than at `a` — counted, not tested for membership — so a claim repaired in one place and left standing in another place of the same file is still found | `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#corrected@65b199e3`, `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#score@0b214b20` | **Executed** 2026-09-08 against a probe repository driven through git from python. A claim stated twice in one file, corrected in the first copy only, is reported at the second. Mutated: removal tested by `not in` instead of counted — the case turns red | 2026-09-08 | **`score` carries no self-match guard, and the obvious one was wrong.** It skipped a candidate at the source's own path and line, but the source is read at `a` and the candidate at `b`, so equal line numbers are two revisions of one file rather than one sentence — in the probe above both landed at line 5 and the guard threw away the survivor it exists to find. None is needed, because a sentence becomes a source only where its count dropped |
| S4 · a `~~…~~` strike is read **within one line**, so a stray unpaired marker can blank only its own line | `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#STRUCK@d42c5358`, `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#blank_struck@b6b96f7e` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. `seal/ledger.md` carries **17** `~~` markers — an odd number, so one is unpaired. Read with DOTALL, every pairing after the stray one was offset and the span that followed ran across lines and blanked row R3: the check reported #267's ledger fragment and stayed silent about the shared file, which is the carrier the ticket is about. Mutated: DOTALL restored, and blanking removed altogether — each turns a case red | 2026-09-08 | Blanking preserves newlines rather than deleting the span, so every line number after a multi-line strike still reports its own. GFM strikethrough does not cross a blank line, so bounding to one line loses nothing that was ever written |
| S5 · an exemption is anchored on a **contiguous run of the standing text's own words**, so it stops holding as soon as that text changes, and neither an empty quote nor a scattered one silences anything | `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#exempted@5f2549e7`, `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#read_exemptions@a8022e7e` | **Executed** 2026-09-08 on #267's real range. Two rows quoting *never a leaf* silence both ledger carriers and are still printed with their grounds; one row quoting *never a petal* silences neither. Mutated: the quote matched on membership instead of a run, a row with no quote was accepted, and a second `--exempt` file with no rows was accepted — each turns a case red | 2026-09-08 | **A row with an empty Quote exempted its whole file**, because a zero-length run is contained in every text — the *check nothing* value this design says it does not have. It is refused at exit 2 now. The emptiness refusal also counted rows collected SO FAR, so a second `--exempt` naming an empty file passed on the strength of the first one's rows **Re-read 2026-09-09 by work item 1788912166 (#296, #295, #297).** `read_exemptions` gained the `| Range | Grounds |` row shape and a second return value for it. The quote path is untouched — `words(cells[1])` and the refusal of an empty quote both stand — and `exempted`, which is where the contiguous run is matched, was not edited and its hash did not move. The claim holds, and the whole-range row is a new claim in this work item's own fragment rather than a change to this one. **Re-read again 2026-09-09 in round 1's fix pass**, which added the source file to the RANGE tuple and left `words(cells[1])`, the empty-quote refusal and `exempted` alone. The quote path is still untouched and the claim holds. |
| S6 · a work item's `rounds/` records are outside the corpus, and what that is worth is **not that it keeps them from being reported** | `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#records_a_past_round@356dd3ce`, `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#corpus@cfdd6a91` | **Executed** 2026-09-08. With the exclusion switched off, `round-2.md` and `round-2-report.md` score **1.51** against a floor of 1.6 — the floor would have refused them anyway. What the exclusion buys is the other side of the same arithmetic: dropping two files carrying the wording raises `idf` for every phrase they held, and row R3 goes from **1.69 to 1.79**. Mutated: the exclusion returns `False`, and the case — run at a floor of 1.4, which admits the records — turns red | 2026-09-08 | **The case used to pass with the exclusion switched off**, because it ran at the shipped floor where the records sit 0.09 below. It was measuring the threshold and reading as though it measured the exclusion, which is the shape contract §15 exists for |
