### 1788912166-red-for-following-the-documents-green-for-ignoring-one

<!-- One work item's rows, three tickets. No header — `fold_ledger.py` writes
the `###` at the release and moves this file into `seal/ledger.md`.

G1 is #296, G2–G4 are #295, G5–G6 are #297. G4 is a row about where a
constant lives rather than about a behaviour, and it is a row because the
failure it prevents is silent: the writer would keep writing a row the reader
no longer finds, and the reader reads a missing row as `no run was named`. -->

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| G1 · the arm counting round records reads the draft state, so a draft pull request may lack the record the rounds have not written yet; and `unknown` — no payload, an unparseable payload, one naming no pull request, one whose `draft` is a string — is judged as READY at this arm exactly as it is at `Pass`, so the excuse cannot be reached by a harness that stops writing the flag | `skills/code-review/scripts/chain_check.py#main>"which is the state a draft pull request opens in"@29864f39`, `tests/test_chain_check_at_the_pull_request.py#test_a_draft_pull_request_has_not_had_its_rounds_yet@00b57f50`, `tests/test_chain_check_at_the_pull_request.py#test_an_unknown_state_is_not_a_draft_at_this_arm_either@c797feb5` | **Executed** 2026-09-09. Seen red before the fix: exit 1 on a draft, the failure being the very error the fix removes, printed under a header reading `judged as a draft pull request`. The six sibling cases were green before and after — they pin the direction the fix must not move. Mutated: `if not strict` forced to `if False` turns the draft case red alone | 2026-09-09 | The value was already computed for every run and reached `check_round` alone, a hundred lines down the same walk; the fix is that value reaching one more arm, with no new input and nothing threaded through anything. What made it worth a ticket is that `orchestration.md` documented the resulting red window as *the window's expected state, not a failure to chase* — a check red for obedience to the document beside it. That sentence is corrected in the same change |
| G2 · at a ready pull request the LAST round record's `Broad gate` cell is read, and FOUR states are told apart: `not yet` or no row at all is the run that never happened, a SHA the record's own `Target SHA` descends from is the run spent before the round it sealed, a cell with no SHA-shaped word in it names no run and fails above the cutoff, and a resolvable SHA on a divergent line is reported rather than failed | `skills/code-review/scripts/chain_check.py#broad_gate@48254b45`, `skills/code-review/scripts/chain_check.py#says_gate_not_yet@70eef030`, `tests/test_chain_check_at_the_pull_request.py#test_a_broad_gate_spent_before_the_round_it_was_meant_to_seal_fails@9847449e`, `tests/test_chain_check_at_the_pull_request.py#test_a_one_word_cell_is_not_a_way_past_the_arm@0c06ba74`, `tests/test_chain_check_at_the_pull_request.py#test_a_broad_gate_cell_nobody_can_parse_is_reported_below_the_cutoff@09b75946`, `tests/test_chain_check_at_the_pull_request.py#test_a_gate_sha_on_a_divergent_line_makes_no_claim_and_says_so@f0e9606f`, `tests/test_chain_check_at_the_pull_request.py#test_a_broad_gate_taken_after_the_rounds_settled_passes@9c08f5ec` | **Executed** 2026-09-09. Eleven cases seen red against the unfixed script, the first with `AttributeError: module has no attribute 'BROAD_GATE'` — the constant did not exist because nothing had ever read the cell. Eight mutations of this arm, all killed: the reason-after-separator path dropped, the draft excuse removed, an absent row read as nothing to check, ancestry without the equality guard, an unresolvable gate SHA treated as a real run, and an unparseable cell failed instead of reported. **Re-read 2026-09-09 in round 1's fix pass, which found the third state a one-word bypass and the passing condition written as its own complement.** Eight more cases seen red — seven cells at the cutoff and the divergent line — and four more mutations killed: the unparseable cell made a notice again, the at-or-after pass dropped, a divergent SHA returning silently, and `unknown` read as a draft | 2026-09-10 | **Equal is not premature**, and `merge-base --is-ancestor X X` exits 0, so an arm resting on ancestry alone fails the exactly-correct case — the round reviewed a commit and the gate ran at that commit. The resolved oids are compared first, which is also what makes an abbreviated cell and a full-length `Target SHA` comparable. **The passing condition is asked DIRECTLY**, `the gate ran at the reviewed commit or after it`, rather than as the complement of premature: written as the complement it admitted every merely-unrelated commit in silence, which was quieter than the notice an unresolvable SHA already got. Reading an ABSENT row as the same state as `not yet` is a judgment beyond the specification's letter, and the grounds first given for it — that reading it as *nothing to check* would make deleting one line the way past the arm — were false when written, because a cell holding one unparseable word was a notice and `skipped` is shorter than a deleted line. Closing that is what makes the reason true. Above the cutoff `round_record.py new` writes the row on every record and `seal` and `close --broad-gate` are the only things that change the value (#30 added the first), so an unparseable cell there is a choice; below it the tail of the same function still grandfathers **Re-read 2026-09-10 in work item 1789002694 (#30), phase 4.** Both failure messages were rewritten to name the `sealer`'s spawn and `broad-gate --record`, the second keeping `close --broad-gate` for the one pass where fixes and the gate land together. The FOUR states, the cutoff and every case are unchanged — what moved is the text a reader is handed, which two assertions in `test_a_broad_gate_that_never_ran_fails_a_ready_pull_request` and `test_a_one_word_cell_is_not_a_way_past_the_arm` now pin. **Re-read 2026-09-10 for #30 round 1**: the premature-SHA refusal's TEXT now names the sealer's spawn and `broad-gate --base <base> --record <item>` instead of telling the reader to take the run by hand. The four states this row is about are unchanged; what moved is what one of them says. |
| G3 · the arm reaches work items begun on or after `GATE_FROM` and no others, `>=` the way the seven cutoffs before it are keyed, and a work item whose id is not a unix second is grandfathered whole | `skills/code-review/scripts/chain_check.py#GATE_FROM@b20640c7`, `tests/test_chain_check_at_the_pull_request.py#test_a_work_item_one_second_below_the_cutoff_is_not_failed_for_it@f0073b98`, `tests/test_chain_check_at_the_pull_request.py#test_a_work_item_at_the_cutoff_is_held_to_it@e1926d2a` | **Executed** 2026-09-09. The two boundary cases sit one second either side of the constant, and the test module states the number as a literal rather than importing it, so a typo in the constant cannot make every case agree with it; a third case pins the script's value against that literal. Mutated both ways: dropping the cutoff turns the below case red, and `<=` in place of `<` turns the at-the-cutoff case red | 2026-09-09 | **This cutoff is not optional in the way the seven before it were, and the difference is worth the row.** Those grandfather records that could not have met a rule which did not exist. This one grandfathers records that MET the rule and had nowhere to say so: `round_record.py` has written `not yet` into every record it ever generated. The blast radius of getting it wrong is every work item in the tree and every one in flight — one was mid-review in another checkout of the same clone while this was built. `GATE_FROM` is this work item's own id, so with `>=` the first pull request the arm applies to is this one |
| G4 · `Broad gate` and its `not yet` sentinel are defined once, in the script that READS them, and the script that writes the row imports both | `skills/code-review/scripts/chain_check.py#BROAD_GATE@b37b2aa9`, `tests/test_chain_check_at_the_pull_request.py#test_the_broad_gate_label_has_one_spelling_both_scripts_read@e664be54` | **Executed** 2026-09-09. The case asserts the quoted literal appears exactly once in the reader and never in the writer, and that the writer names the reader for both values | 2026-09-09 | They lived in the writer under a comment reading *"Field labels the checker has no constant for, because it never reads them"* — true when written, false from this change on. **An `is` comparison cannot state this claim**: the two scripts load each other as separate module objects and `"Broad gate"` is not an identifier, so the literal is not interned and identity fails between two correct copies. Equal values prove nothing either — two copies agree until one is edited, which is the whole failure. So the claim is asserted against the source text, and the direction it guards is the silent one: rename the row in the writer alone and it keeps writing a row the reader no longer finds, which G2's arm reads as *no run was named* |
| G5 · `survivors.md` takes a second row shape, `\| Range \| Grounds \|`, told apart from a per-survivor row by the first cell alone; a declaration is anchored on **two** things, the range and the work item directory the file sits in, so it stops holding over a different range AND never reaches a range that touches nothing in its own work item; and a row with no grounds is not a row | `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#read_exemptions@a8022e7e`, `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#RANGE_CELL@4dc41537`, `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#OWNER_DIR@09c29ef5`, `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#whole_range@aeae8cd7`, `tests/test_a_corrected_sentence_survives_elsewhere.py#test_a_whole_range_row_does_not_reach_a_different_range@e22bce12`, `tests/test_a_corrected_sentence_survives_elsewhere.py#test_a_declaration_does_not_reach_a_work_item_that_did_not_write_it@9726f6bd`, `tests/test_a_corrected_sentence_survives_elsewhere.py#test_a_work_items_own_declaration_still_holds_in_the_ci_spelling@2a810c0c`, `tests/test_a_corrected_sentence_survives_elsewhere.py#test_a_path_row_and_a_range_row_are_told_apart@79a4be54` | **Executed** 2026-09-09 over a built repository whose range corrects one statement and leaves its twin standing. Six cases seen red first, and the two that passed had to: the undeclared-deletion floor, and a range row with empty grounds at exit 2 for the old reason that a two-cell row was not a row at all. Three mutations killed: the pattern widened to match anything reads a path row as a range, empty grounds accepted, and a declaration excusing any range. **Re-read 2026-09-09 in round 1's fix pass, which found the range alone was not an anchor at all** — `origin/<base>...HEAD` is a relation and re-resolves per checkout, so one merged row excused every later branch cut from the same base. Two more cases seen red, on a two-work-item fixture that varies the CHECKOUT rather than the spec's text, and four more mutations killed: the ownership refusal never firing, always firing, its refusal not printed, and the work item read one directory level too shallow | 2026-09-09 | A path can never match `^[^\s\|]+\.\.\.?[^\s\|]+$` because the dots need a non-space word on BOTH sides, so `../notes.md` is a path and `A..B` is a range. That is why no new file, no new flag and no header convention was needed: a two-cell range row sits inside the same three-column table, which is how it will be written. The old parser skipped two-cell rows in silence, so nothing that used to be read changes meaning. The grounds are not optional because they are the entire content of the escape — what a reviewer reads is the written sentence, and a row without one silences 153 places on the strength of nothing |
| G6 · a declared range is RESOLVED rather than compared as text, and a spec that no longer resolves silences nothing and is printed rather than refusing the run | `skills/code-review/scripts/survivor_check.py#whole_range@aeae8cd7`, `tests/test_a_corrected_sentence_survives_elsewhere.py#test_a_whole_range_row_is_resolved_rather_than_string_matched@73729189`, `tests/test_a_corrected_sentence_survives_elsewhere.py#test_a_range_row_that_does_not_resolve_silences_nothing_and_says_so@72f6af26` | **Executed** 2026-09-09: a declaration spelled `HEAD^..HEAD` silences a run given the same range as two oids, and `origin/gone..HEAD` silences nothing and is named in the report. Two mutations killed: matching on the spec's text, and raising instead of collecting the unresolvable ones | 2026-09-09 | Both halves are about the same fact — a range has two honest spellings and a `survivors.md` outlives the refs its own names. CI spells the range `origin/<base>...HEAD`, which is what a session copies into the declaration; a person types two commits. Refusing an unresolvable spec at exit 2 would be the landmine: the file lives from the work item's first row until the release that ships it, the release branch its range names gets deleted, and every later range's check would then refuse over a row that has nothing to do with it |

<!-- specs/1788926756-three-sentences-are-wrong-about-where-a-duration-is -->
### 1788926756-three-sentences-are-wrong-about-where-a-duration-is

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A window's span runs to the last call to END, so it can never be shorter than a single call the window counts | `skills/verify/scripts/session_cost.py#analyse@c8423846`, pinned at `tests/test_session_cost.py#test_a_span_covers_every_call_it_counts@f1584e66` | **Executed.** On a background command 0-1000s beside calls at 10-12s and 990-995s the span read **995s** — five seconds shorter than the single `Bash` call the window holds — and now reads 1000s, printing `span 16.7m` where it printed 16.6m. The case was seen red against the module before the change, exit 1 on `assert 995.0 == 1000`. Three mutations one at a time, each restored from bytes the probe kept: back to `calls[-1]["end"]`, `min(c["end"])` instead of `max`, and measured from `calls[-1]["start"]` — each exit 1 | 2026-09-09 | **The old rule read the end of the last element of a list `load` sorts by START**, so the arithmetic and the sentence describing it were both about the last call to BEGIN. `command_s` and `model_s` are untouched: the sum over calls and the turn walk read the same values as before, which is what keeps every other published number comparable. **The tidy-up this row exists against is taking `command_s` to the union of the call intervals** so the share cannot pass 100% — that changes what the number MEANS, from command time to wall clock with a command running, and breaks the series `analyse`'s own docstring exists to protect. `questions.md` Q3 holds it with the owner |
| No printed figure in THIS PROJECT's transcripts moves under the new span rule, on any printed surface; five figures move elsewhere on the machine — 2 row spans and 3 between-the-rows figures | `skills/verify/scripts/session_cost.py#analyse@c8423846` | **Executed**, both rules computed over every transcript under `~/.claude/projects`, on every printed surface the span feeds rather than on two of them. Run level: 169 transcripts with calls, **one span moves, by 0.006s**, printed span 10.3m either way and `command` share 0% either way. Row level, which the run-level sweep does not cover and which `reading.md` publishes: 599 spawn-cycle rows, **8 move at all** and **2 by enough to change a printed figure** (368.5m → 368.7m, 195.7m → 195.8m). **The between-the-rows figure, which is the figure this work item is about, moves on 3 transcripts** (6.7m → 6.6m, 24.4m → 24.2m, 3.3m → 3.2m). `idle`, the `model` share and the whole-run `command` share move nowhere, and the figure/refusal branch never flips. **Every move on every axis is in another project's transcript**; this project's directory has 16 transcripts and not one moving figure | 2026-09-09 | **The row-level axis is the one a run-level sweep misses, and it is the one the published readings are on.** A cycle row's span comes from the same `analyse`, so a row whose last call to begin is not its last to end moves whether or not the run does — and the two printed moves prove it happens. What makes the published readings safe is not the rule being harmless but this project's transcripts having no such row, which is a measurement and not a structural guarantee. A transcript with a genuinely long-lived call inside a cycle would move, and `skills/verify/SKILL.md` says so where a person taking a reading meets it |
| The between-the-rows refusal names the cut a row ends at rather than a spawn's result, and prints the two sums rather than their difference | `skills/verify/scripts/session_cost.py#report_spawns@15595f59`, disclosed at `skills/verify/SKILL.md#"## Measure the segment, and feed the flow log"@b1d57f7c`, pinned at `tests/test_session_cost.py#test_a_head_call_outlives_the_cut_without_outliving_a_spawns_result@6a3df726` | **Executed.** Head call 0-8s, spawn 5-9s, calls 6-9s and 9-10s: row spans 8s + 4s + 1s against a run span of 10s, and the line printed *a call outlived a spawn's result* while no call in the transcript ends after the spawn's result at 9s. `spawn_cuts` opens its cut list at the first spawn's START (`#spawn_cuts@570c32bb`), which is why the head row's cut is not a result. The difference on that shape is three seconds, so `minutes` printed *by 0.1m* beside a span column reading 0.1m/0.1m/0.0m. The shape that printed *by 0.0m* is #145's round-3 one-second overlap — head 0-9s, spawn 5-7s, call 10-12s — and `minutes` rounds any difference under three seconds away. Both cases seen red against the module before the change, exit 1. Two mutations — the result named again, the difference printed again — each exit 1 | 2026-09-09 | **Neither printed figure is the rows' overlap, and after the span rule changed that holds for a new reason.** A row's span and the run's both end at the last call to end, so every row's interval sits inside the run's, and the difference is the gaps between the rows MINUS their overlap. On the pinned fixture the head row covers the whole run, so there are no gaps and the two coincide — which is exactly why naming it the overlap would be a claim that holds there and fails in general. The old grounds, that an outliving call shortened the RUN's span too, are gone with the rule. **One consequence nothing had named: the refusal can no longer fire on ONE row.** It used to — the head row's span reached 1000s against a run of 995s — and a sum past the run now requires two rows covering the same seconds |
| An exact cover reads as the partition agreeing rather than as a refusal | `skills/verify/scripts/session_cost.py#report_spawns@15595f59`, pinned at `tests/test_session_cost.py#test_an_exact_cover_reads_as_the_partition_agreeing@ee75e690` | **Executed.** Head 0-8s, spawn 5-7s, call 10-12s: run span 12s against row spans of 8s, 2s and 2s, so `outside` is 0 and the line prints *0.0m of the run's 0.2m is BETWEEN the rows — mostly the wait*. Seen red by tightening the guard to `> 0`, exit 1, which is the mutation this case exists to kill; a second mutation reworded the sentence, also exit 1 | 2026-09-09 | **The `>= 0` choice was unpinned until now, and `spec.md` cited a case that did not exist** — the only existing boundary case covers the positive branch at `outside == 304`. #145's round 3 probed the exact cover and planted nothing. Under `> 0` a run whose rows cover it exactly would print *no between-the-rows figure*, which is the one shape where a reader can watch the arithmetic work. **`mostly` also carries the other direction**: where a small overlap is netted off against larger gaps, what prints is the gaps minus the overlap, so the figure is a floor on what is between the rows and never an overstatement |
| A negative span means no call ended after the first call began, which is strictly narrower than the last call to begin having ended before it | `skills/verify/scripts/session_cost.py#report@ae4d5c2d`, pinned at `tests/test_session_cost.py#test_a_negative_span_says_what_it_actually_saw@15c2cc26` | **Executed**, and one arm of the existing case flipped rather than being re-worded. A transcript with call `a` from 10:00 to 12:00 and call `b` from 10:05 to 09:00 read **minus sixty minutes** for a run that plainly lasted two hours, and now reads `span 120.0m` with neither non-positive sentence printed. The genuinely negative arms — every call ending before the first call begins — still print, now as *no call ended after the first call began*. One mutation on the sentence's wording, exit 1 | 2026-09-09 | **The sentence says what the arithmetic computes, and the arithmetic moved.** The sibling work item settled the old wording as *exactly the proposition* the old span stated, checked by construction; that reasoning was correct about the old rule and is why the sentence had to move with it. The old sentence is still TRUE under the new rule — every call ending before the first call began implies the last to begin did — but it is no longer equivalent, and a weaker sentence beside a stronger arithmetic is how a page stops describing its own code. **The branch is also rarer on purpose**, and the flipped arm is the shape it stopped claiming |

<!-- specs/1788936260-a-case-pins-what-it-actually-measures -->
### 1788936260-a-case-pins-what-it-actually-measures

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A case that asserts a document clause by its vocabulary pins a phrase; only asserting the clause whole pins a claim that carries a ranking or a direction | `tests/test_a_segment_feeds_the_flow_log.py#test_the_section_names_batching_as_the_way_a_share_passes_one_hundred@4ef455ff`, over `skills/verify/SKILL.md#"## Measure the segment, and feed the flow log"@b1d57f7c` | **Executed 2026-09-09.** Five edits to the paragraph, one at a time, `skills/verify/SKILL.md` restored from held bytes and sha256-compared after each (`fd3a4e7d285d` throughout). Both assertion sets were run against each mutated body in the same probe, which is what splits the five: the two causes **swapped**, the measurement **inverted**, and the old phrase re-added **sentence-initially** were all exit 0 under the shipped assertions and are exit 1 under the replacements. The two the shipped case caught — the old phrase lowercase mid-sentence, and the concurrency phrase deleted — are exit 1 under both. The case is exit 0 unmutated | 2026-09-09 | **The three that got through are rearrangements of true words, and the two that were caught are a phrase appearing or disappearing.** That is the class stated as a measurement rather than as an argument: a substring assertion sees a phrase arrive or leave and cannot see one move. `section_body()` collapses whitespace, which is what makes a whole-clause assertion affordable — the two clauses span three and four source lines, so asserting them against the raw text would pin the wrap column instead. #310's remaining `Not verified` row is inherited unanswered: whether any OTHER case in the tree pins a document clause by substring where the claim has a ranking or a direction is a sweep this work item did not run |
| A module's arms can be enumerated from its own syntax tree under #262's rule, and the per-function split is reproducible: `reader` 5, `is_closed` 4, `gh_segments` 5, `main` 17 | `skills/verify/scripts/arm_check.py#arms@b8144598`, pinned at `tests/test_arm_check.py#test_the_guards_arms_match_the_count_taken_by_hand@71238e8e` | **Executed 2026-09-09** against `hooks/review-history-guard.py`. 31 arms inside functions, matching the handoff's per-function split cell for cell, plus **one at module level** (`if __name__ == "__main__":`) that neither hand count had a row for — 32 in total. #262's table says 33, differing only in `main` (19); the file changed twice after that measurement (`341be0b`, `1dedd1e`) and the ticket is left as written | 2026-09-09 | **The counting rule that makes the two numbers comparable is TOP-LEVEL members of a boolean test, not flattened.** `gh_segments`'s `while` test is `i < len(toks) and ((...) or ...)`: two top-level members gives that function 5 and matches both hand counts, while flattening recursively gives 4 and takes the function to **7**. Either rule is defensible in the abstract and only one keeps any number this checker prints comparable with the ticket's, so it is pinned on a fixture and on the real function. Two shapes were added beyond #262's four — `match_case` and `comprehension` — which `plan.md` names as its own failure scenario; they change no count here, so the 31 is reproducible under four shapes and six alike |
| An arm shape the walk does not recognise is refused, not skipped, and the classification is total over the grammar — all 122 of this interpreter's AST constructors | `skills/verify/scripts/arm_check.py#_refuse_unknown@8bb86e28` with `ARM_SHAPES`/`NOT_ARMS`, pinned at `tests/test_arm_check.py#test_every_ast_constructor_is_classified@04c7360a` and `tests/test_arm_check.py#test_an_unclassified_node_type_is_refused_rather_than_skipped@dee74bf2` | **Executed 2026-09-09.** The totality case derives the constructor set by walking `ast.AST`'s subclass tree and asserts the two tables cover it: 122 classified, 122 in the grammar, no overlap, no duplicates. Both directions are asserted. Driven red by removing `"IfExp"` from `ARM_SHAPES` (named in neither table) and by classifying `Assign` out of both at runtime, which makes the refusal fire on the first statement of any module | 2026-09-09 | **Two constructors nearly went missing and both would have made the refusal useless.** `Constant` is dropped by a LEAVES-only walk of the subclass tree, because the deprecated aliases `Num`, `Str`, `Bytes`, `NameConstant` and `Ellipsis` are its subclasses — and it appears in nearly every parse, so the refusal would have fired on the first literal. `Assert` carries a test a mutation could flip and #262's rule does not count it; counting it would move every per-function number away from the hand count, so the exclusion is DECLARED in `NOT_ARMS` where it can be overturned rather than omitted. `For`/`AsyncFor` are excluded on the same terms: a `for`'s `orelse` is arguably an arm, and `is_closed`'s `for path in records` is not among that function's four |
| The survivor count is a property of the mutation operator: on `hooks/review-history-guard.py` against `tests/test_chain_hooks.py`, **0 of the 29** arms inversion can be asked of survive it and **12 of 32** survive removal | `skills/verify/scripts/arm_check.py#OPERATORS@da804080` and `skills/verify/scripts/arm_check.py#mutate@f6755cbd`, pinned at `tests/test_arm_check.py#test_an_arm_is_watched_when_any_operator_is_noticed@dbe50f0b` | **Executed 2026-09-09**, the full run over the real module, both operators, 61 mutations with the module restored and hash-compared after each. `invert` 29 asked · 29 killed · 0 survived; `remove` 32 asked · 20 killed · 12 survived; combined 32 measured · 31 killed · 1 watched by no case (`main:189`, `except Exception`) — the word is `measured` since round 2's finding 15, because an arm that was mutated without producing a verdict is not in that count. Ten of the twelve removal survivors are in `main`. The first run of the same command, before round 1's sole-type refusal, read `invert` 32 asked · 31 killed · 1 survived — the one survivor was `main:189`, whose inversion is the same edit as its removal, so it left `invert`'s row rather than being killed | 2026-09-09 | **#262's nine is a REMOVAL count and must not be compared with the combined total.** Every sentence in its table is about taking something out — *"removing either opt-in half makes a globally installed plugin nag unrelated repositories"* — so the comparable row is 12, and the gap to nine is the ticket's own argument: the file changed twice and four of the thirteen it started from were closed on #209 · #210's branch. Built with inversion alone the checker reported **1** survivor, and reports **0** since the sole-type refusal; either number put beside the ticket's nine would have read as a refutation by a checker measuring something else — the exact class of error this work item is about. An arm counts watched when EITHER operator is noticed, because *does any case depend on this arm* is answered by one yes; the per-operator rows sit beside that total and the report names which one the ticket compares with |
| A mutation loop's verdict can be decided by cached bytecode rather than by the source on disk, and no hash catches it | `skills/verify/scripts/arm_check.py#clear_bytecode_cache@add61f44`, pinned at `tests/test_arm_check.py#test_no_arm_runs_while_cached_bytecode_for_the_module_exists@5d9cbee6` | **Executed 2026-09-09.** A two-arm fixture whose second arm no case reaches came back `killed`, with a traceback naming an assertion the unmutated module satisfies. CPython validates a `.pyc` on the source's mtime and SIZE, and `not (host == "example.com")` and `not (flag)` both add six characters, so arm two loaded arm one's bytecode. Fixed by clearing the cache around every mutation and running the command with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE` | 2026-09-09 | **`restore`'s sha256 compare cannot see this**, which is why it is a separate row: the bytes on disk were correct at every moment and the interpreter never read them. The realistic trigger is not the loop at all — this repository's own suite imports `hooks/review-history-guard.py`, so `hooks/__pycache__/` holds bytecode for it before `arm-check` is ever run. **Reproducing it is timing-dependent** (measured both ways: poisoned when the two writes were adjacent, clean when they were not), so the case pins the MECHANISM — at the moment an arm's command runs, is there cached bytecode for the module at all — rather than the reproduction, which would pass on a fast machine and fail on a slow one |
| An operator that could not be asked of one arm falls out of that operator's denominator silently unless it is named | `skills/verify/scripts/arm_check.py#Verdict@33d032f5` (`not_applicable`) and `skills/verify/scripts/arm_check.py#_report@4bb35c59`, pinned at `tests/test_arm_check.py#test_a_partly_skipped_operator_is_reported_without_refusing_the_arm@0f01f20b` | **Executed 2026-09-09.** The first two-operator run over the real module printed `remove 31 asked` against 32 arms and named neither the arm nor the reason. The arm was `gh_segments:176`, whose `while` test wraps across lines: removing the index guard left a two-line expression with nothing bracketing it, a `SyntaxError`, and because `invert` had measured the arm it was not `refused`. Fixed twice over — the remainder is parenthesised, so the current run asks all 32, and a `Verdict` now carries the reason for any operator it could not ask | 2026-09-09 | **This is the skip-versus-refuse failure the module refuses, occurring in the code that reports the refusals.** An arm no operator can mutate was already refused and named; an arm where ONE operator has no mutation was neither. Only the report's own arithmetic gave it away. Two places where the naive removal is a `SyntaxError` and both are in the real module: an `ast.Tuple` in an except clause spans its own parentheses, so `except OSError, ValueError:` — not Python since 2 — would have refused all three arms of `reader`'s handler; and a wrapped boolean test spans its own brackets. The second was found by the run rather than by reading. **The list this row is about now holds a second outcome and is named for it.** Round 1's timeout and `OSError` fixes put pairs into it that WERE asked and answered nothing, so round 2's finding 15 renamed the section *operator/arm pairs with no verdict*; this row's own pair, `gh_segments:176`, is still one that could not be asked, and which of the two any pair is lives in the reason beside it |
| An arm's command can hang or fail to start, and either one used to cost more than the arm: the hang left the module mutated for as long as the process lived, and a failed spawn discarded every verdict already measured | `skills/verify/scripts/arm_check.py#run_arms@8995285a`, pinned at `tests/test_arm_check.py#test_a_command_that_never_returns_is_recorded_as_unmeasured@5c1a7bcb` and `tests/test_arm_check.py#test_a_spawn_failure_keeps_the_verdicts_already_measured@a307a59c` | **Executed 2026-09-09**, round 1's findings 1 and 2. A `--tests` of `python -c "import time; time.sleep(30)"` with no bound hangs for 30 seconds an arm and prints nothing, because the output is captured; `subprocess.run` made to raise `OSError` on its third of four calls raised straight out of `run_arms` and lost the first arm's two verdicts. Both are now recorded where a pair with no verdict goes, with the bound or the errno in the reason — *un-asked* was the name for that list until round 2's finding 15, which is when it stopped being true of it: a pair whose command was spawned and killed at the bound WAS asked | 2026-09-09 | **Neither is a verdict, which is why they go where the un-asked pairs go.** `killed` would read as *a case noticed* and `survived` as *none did*, and neither was measured. The spawn failure is reachable rather than constructed: `bin/test` builds a virtual environment on demand, so a command that worked at arm 1 can stop working at arm 20. What the timeout does NOT close is the working-tree loss behind it — the only copy of the module is in the process's memory and no `finally` runs for a `SIGKILL`, an OOM kill or a closed terminal — which is #312 |
| A splice's line split has to be exactly the tokenizer's, and it parts company with `ast`'s `lineno` in either direction: `str.splitlines` splits on eight characters the tokenizer does not count, and a split narrowed to `\n` alone misses two that it does | `skills/verify/scripts/arm_check.py#_lines@75083683` read by `skills/verify/scripts/arm_check.py#_splice@dc4edec3`, pinned at `tests/test_arm_check.py#test_a_separator_the_tokenizer_ignores_does_not_move_a_spliced_arm@edf39c0c` | **Executed 2026-09-09**, round 1's finding 3. Eleven fixtures, and the pin runs both ways. Nine for the separators the tokenizer ignores — each of `\x0b`, `\x0c`, `\x1c`, `\x1d`, `\x1e`, `\x85` and the two line separators U+2028 and U+2029 inside a string literal above the arm, plus a form feed on its own line — all nine `IndentationError` under `source.splitlines(keepends=True)` and all nine `OK` under `_lines`. Two more for the terminators it counts, CRLF and a lone `\r`: `_LINE_END` narrowed to `\n` alone is `IndentationError` on the lone-CR fixture and green on all ten others, so a split that keeps too FEW terminators is red as well | 2026-09-09 | **The spans were right while the splice was wrong, and the standard library is why.** `ast.get_source_segment` reads its lines through a private `_splitlines_no_ff`, docstringed *"This mimics how the Python parser splits source code"*, so `arm.source` was correct for every one of these fixtures and only the write-back moved. The loud outcome is a refusal — `IndentationError` subclasses `SyntaxError`, so every arm below the separator files as an un-asked pair. The quiet one is a mis-indexed splice that happens to parse, which is a verdict recorded against a mutation nobody asked for, and `restore`'s hash cannot see it for the same reason the bytecode cache cannot. `_splice` is the only place in the module that turns a `lineno` into a list index; the other nine readers of one display or sort it. **`_LINE_END` was an arm no case killed until the two terminator fixtures arrived**: narrowed to `\n`, every separator fixture stayed green, because none of them used a line ending the regex had dropped. Found by mutation-testing each unit this fix pass added, which is why the claim above is stated as *exactly the tokenizer's set* rather than as *not `str.splitlines`* |
| For an `except` handler with one type left, inverting the arm and removing it are the same edit, so asking both operators reports one measurement as two independent answers | `skills/verify/scripts/arm_check.py#mutate@f6755cbd`, pinned at `tests/test_arm_check.py#test_a_sole_type_handler_is_measured_by_one_operator_and_not_by_both@bc5bb92e` | **Executed 2026-09-09**, round 1's finding 6. `mutate(src, arm, "invert") == mutate(src, arm, "remove")` was `True` on a sole-type handler, and `hooks/review-history-guard.py` has three such arms — `is_closed:143`, `gh_segments:173` and `main:189`. `invert` now refuses them and the report names the pair; re-measured over the real module, `invert` 29 asked · 29 killed · 0 survived against `remove` 32 asked · 20 killed · 12 survived, combined unchanged | 2026-09-09 | **The arm the report headlines as watched by nothing was the one printing the duplicate.** `main:189` printed `invert survived · remove survived` — one measurement shown as two answers to the two questions `OPERATORS` says are different, and it was the whole of `invert`'s survivor count, so inversion alone now reports **0** survivors rather than 1. Nothing is measured less: `remove` splices the identical text, and it is the operator #262's table compares with. The class is wider than the sole type — `except (OSError,):` is a one-member tuple whose remainder is also what nothing raises, so the two operators reach different spans with the same effect, and the refusal keys on the remainder rather than on the shape |

### 1789002694-two-agents-are-forbidden-the-seal-and-nobody-is-assigned-it

<!-- One work item's rows. No header — `fold_ledger.py` writes the `###` at
the release and moves this file into `seal/ledger.md`.

Ten rows over a branch that adds an agent, two commands and a subcommand.
S10 and S11 arrived with phase 5 and the enumeration below runs to S8; the
last three are the capped run, the rule that one seal is final, and the
subcommand's own refusals.

**S8 is gone, and work item 1789034970 (#120) is what removed it.** Its claim
was that `agents/sealer.md` names its one write as §6's own exception and
states that §2 as it stands forbids its one act — and its second coordinate
was the section written to be deleted, `## §2 as it stands, and #120`. #120
deleted that section, rewrote §6 so that a named write is the rule rather than
an exception, and renamed both cases the row cited. A row whose anchor a
change removes is REMOVED rather than re-pointed, so the successor claim is a
new row in that work item's own fragment,
`seal/ledger/1789034970-the-contract-is-settled-against-the-agents-that-exist.md`.
The enumeration above still names S8 because it is a record of what the branch
judged, and that judgment happened.
What earns a row here is a judgment whose repair looks obvious from the
outside, and a gate that draws a picture has an unusual number of them: a
sampling offset that reads as an off-by-one nobody would defend (S2), a
question asked before a call rather than after it, which reads as the wrong
order (S3), a refusal where a default would be friendlier (S4), a comparison
that runs late rather than always (S5), a second subcommand where one exists
(S6), a draft judgment inside a command that is about a ready pull request
(S7), and a paragraph written to be deleted (S8).

S1 is not one of those. It is the claim R4 of `seal/ledger.md` used to carry a
coordinate for: this work item renamed the case that reads the sentence, and a
renamed unit is a REMOVED anchor rather than a drifted one, so the claim moves
here rather than being re-pointed there.

Every hash here was stamped by `evidence-check --reverify --ledger '<this
fragment>'`, the scoped WRITE form. `seal/ledger.md`'s eighteen rows that this
branch drifted were re-read one by one and re-stamped by an UNSCOPED
`--reverify`, which the scoped form cannot reach — and the measurement that
made it safe is in `phases/phase-4.md`: the base commit reads 1055 ok · 0
drifted, so there was no row on that file another session would have to judge.
The unscoped READ ran afterwards and is quoted in the same record. -->

Both halves were repaired in the same pass, and only one of them stayed in that file |

The letter twin has the same width and height as the block form at every accepted scale, and every accepted scale draws the SAME BYTES in every process — the tie between two chart colours breaks on the count and then on position in the chart, never on set iteration order (round 1's 🟡 6) | `skills/verify/scripts/seal_stamp.py#build@dd47eca8`, `skills/verify/scripts/seal_stamp.py#shrink@e8e6e102`, `skills/verify/scripts/seal_stamp.py#letter_row@626cc1bb`, `tests/test_the_seal_is_taken_once_by_the_sealer.py#test_the_disc_is_symmetric_because_it_is_computed@26ba07fc`, `tests/test_the_seal_is_taken_once_by_the_sealer.py#test_the_twin_and_the_block_form_have_equal_width_and_height@d3c72bf2` | **Executed** 2026-09-06 in phase 1: the source outside the tree sampled each cell at its corner, and the symmetry case is what found it — the top rope row measured 18 letters against the bottom row's 12, with the first line blank. | S3 · the shape is picked from the stream **before** the console is reconfigured to UTF-8, and a pipe gets letters rather than blocks | `skills/verify/scripts/seal_stamp.py#pick_shape@027a2979`, `skills/verify/scripts/seal_stamp.py#main@4b8233a8`, `tests/test_the_seal_is_taken_once_by_the_sealer.py#test_pick_shape_is_letters_off_a_utf8_terminal@dfa34011`, `tests/test_the_seal_is_taken_once_by_the_sealer.py#test_the_command_piped_prints_the_twin@66d0411b` | **Executed** 2026-09-06 in phase 1: `sys.stdout` and `sys.__stdout__` are one object, and `reconfigure(encoding="utf-8")` moves both — under `PYTHONIOENCODING=cp949` the encoding read `cp949` before the call and `utf-8` after it. Contract §12 is the rule and this is its instance: the finding named a coordinate and the fix was owed to every place the same cause produces. **Two code comments inside `chain_check.py`'s own gate arm were deliberately left**, because they explain why an unparseable cell above the cutoff is a cell somebody chose, and that reasoning survives `seal` joining `close` — both are subcommands, neither is free text. The survivor check does not reach them, and the docstring of the case that pins that arm was corrected to name both writers |
| S10 · a CAPPED run reaches its seal. `seal` asks the verdict table — the `Pass` box — and never the reviewer's `Needs a fix`, so a record whose findings all closed `deferred <home>` seals while that row still reads `yes`, and the reviewer's row is left exactly as written | `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#seal@e0ca2f8f`, `tests/test_the_seal_is_taken_once_by_the_sealer.py#capped_item@afa7f6bc`, `tests/test_the_seal_is_taken_once_by_the_sealer.py#test_seal_writes_over_a_capped_runs_needs_a_fix@9203d9a9` | **Executed** 2026-09-10 in phase 5, seen red against the old code first — the refused run printed *round-1.md's `Needs a fix` reads `yes — 🔴 1`, and the broad gate runs after the rounds settle … no cell was written*, which is phase 4's measurement reproduced by the case. Green after the removal, and `seal` ends with `chain_check`, so exit 0 is the whole chain end to end | 2026-09-10 | **The tidy-up is keeping the refusal and teaching the capped run to rewrite `Needs a fix`.** That row is the reviewer's answer from while the round ran, and nothing else in the workflow rewrites what a reviewer wrote; editing it to unblock a gate is the review certifying itself one step removed. **The second is leaving both refusals and calling the capped run an exception at the pull request**, which puts a documented way past `chain_check` in front of every branch. What the removed refusal was reaching for is *is a finding still open*, and `Pass` answers exactly that, one row down, from the table rather than from prose |
| S11 · the rule that one seal is final has ONE owner and two linking carriers, and it is held by the same table that holds the other nine — `skills/verify/SKILL.md` states *every agent seals what it verified, and the one seal over the whole project is the sealer's*, and `agents/sealer.md` and `agents/warden.md` each carry a sentence naming that section rather than restating the rule — the warden's said it verbatim before linking until round 1's correction, which is the drift the table exists to catch arriving in the row that added it | `` `skills/verify/SKILL.md#"## Every agent seals what it verified, and one of them is final"@13e77c7f` ``, `tests/test_the_rules_have_one_owner.py#test_every_link_names_the_owner@314ad3a3`, `tests/test_one_word_one_meaning.py#test_the_owner_states_that_one_seal_is_final@cdc94327` | **Executed** 2026-09-10 in phase 5: both parametrised cases red against the definitions as they stood — the owner case on the missing sentence, the link case naming `agents/sealer.md` — and green after. The sweep case that forbids an anonymous instance was red on four files at the same commit | 2026-09-10 | **The tidy-up is renaming the sealer so one agent owns the word.** The product's name is built on the noun and the whole stamp design rests on it, and the ambiguity was never that several seals exist — a smith's proof block over its own slice is a legitimate seal. What was missing is that one of them is final. **The second is writing the rule into each definition rather than linking**, which is the drift `tests/test_the_rules_have_one_owner.py` exists to catch and which its own docstring counts: one rule reached eight carriers and three consecutive rounds corrected them three at a time |
| S12 · `broad-gate` reads BOTH of `seal`'s non-zero exits as unsealed. A refusal raised before the write exits 2; what `seal` returns after the write is `chain_check.main`'s `1 if errors else 0`, and the gate's message tells the two apart by the word `sealed` in the `round-record:` line above it — **round 1 wrote this row saying the line's PRESENCE was the discriminator and round 2 measured that both endings print one**, so what the row recorded as verified was the thing that was wrong; the correction is the word, not the line | `skills/verify/scripts/broad_gate.py#gate@b7709fff`, `skills/verify/scripts/broad_gate.py#seal_record@4d582ed3`, `tests/test_the_seal_is_taken_once_by_the_sealer.py#test_a_seal_exit_that_is_not_two_leaves_the_tree_unsealed@6a8b01ee` | **Executed** 2026-09-10, round 1's fix pass: the case returned exit 0 against the branch as reviewed, with the disc printed over a failing chain check | 2026-09-10 | **The tidy-up is reading `== 2`**, which is what shipped and which is the natural reading of a subcommand documented as *exit 2 on a refusal*. The second exit is not a refusal at all — it is the chain check `seal` runs after the cell is written, and it comes back as 1 — so the seal that fell through was over a tree the gate's own check refuses. **The second is treating that 1 as a failure of the checks**: the checks passed, the cell exists, and the message has to say so or the reader hunts a check that is green |
| S13 · a run that ended at the round cap seals and a run whose fixes nobody has read does not, and the two are told apart by `Fixes checked by` rather than by `Pass`. `close` ticks the box the moment a fix table applies, which is one row earlier than the run ending; it re-derives the row to `no fixes to check` only where nothing commissioned a fix, which is the capped case. **The row holds three values and everything outside two of them is refused**, not `nobody` alone: reading `nobody` alone let a name, a word outside the vocabulary and an empty cell reach the write, and the chain check run AFTER the write then refused on that same row | `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#seal@e0ca2f8f`, `skills/code-review/scripts/round_record.py#landing_values@87e329a1`, `tests/test_the_seal_is_taken_once_by_the_sealer.py#test_seal_writes_over_a_capped_runs_needs_a_fix@9203d9a9`, `tests/test_the_seal_is_taken_once_by_the_sealer.py#test_the_capped_run_still_seals_beside_the_third_refusal@c2dc3011` | **Executed** 2026-09-10, round 1's fix pass: the new refusal red against the code as reviewed; and the plausible wrong fix — refusing anything but a named checker — turns both capped-run cases red, which is what says the two rows are not one rule | 2026-09-10 | **The tidy-up is replacing the `Pass` refusal with this one**, since this is the stricter test. They answer different questions: `Pass` says nothing in the verdict TABLE is open, and this says the fixes that closed it were read. A record can fail either without failing the other. **The second is the whole history of this cell**: three rows were tried in order — `Needs a fix`, which refused a capped run; `Pass`, which lets an unread fix pass through; and this one. Anything that reads like the obvious single answer has already been one of the three |
| S14 · the panel's `suite` row carries pytest's own counts and never a linter's, and the discriminator is the WALL CLOCK rather than a list of words. A run in which every test was skipped reports its counts instead of coming back empty, which the panel would have rendered `exit 0` | `skills/verify/scripts/broad_gate.py#suite_counts@2febe15d`, `tests/test_the_seal_is_taken_once_by_the_sealer.py#test_the_suite_row_reads_pytests_counts_and_not_a_linters@f9362575` | **Executed** 2026-09-10 over round 1's and round 2's shapes: `2 warnings emitted`, `Found 2 errors.`, `3 skipped in 0.10s`, and a decorated `768 passed in 63.21s (0:01:03)` | 2026-09-10 | **The tidy-up is a list of pytest's summary words**, which is what round 1 shipped and what round 2 reopened. It closes the finding on the instance and not the class: the list held `errors` and not `warnings`, so one linter's line still landed on the row — and ADDING the missing words is worse, because `warnings` back in the list revives round 1's original defect. The two halves fight, and a word list is the wrong axis. **The second is reading the LAST line rather than the last match**, which breaks on a runner that prints anything after its summary. **This row lost a coordinate rather than re-pointing one**: the clock pattern stood as a module constant `SUMMARY_TAIL` until `close` refused the fix table for it — a top-level name added by a fix answering a finding inside a unit `round-1.md`'s `New units` names is at depth 2, and a fix pass may not add one. The pattern is inline in `#suite_counts` now, so there is no second anchor and the claim is whole in the first |
| S15 · `quote` takes the platform as an ARGUMENT rather than reading `os.name`, so both branches can be turned red from either machine; and the Windows branch uses double quotes rather than `subprocess.list2cmdline`, because `run(..., shell=True)` there goes through `cmd.exe` and `list2cmdline` builds `CreateProcess` quoting | `skills/verify/scripts/broad_gate.py#quote@b6b2caf6`, `tests/test_the_seal_is_taken_once_by_the_sealer.py#test_a_path_is_quoted_for_the_shell_of_either_platform@e99855e3` | **Executed** 2026-09-10: `list2cmdline` with the platform as an argument returns `tests/x&y.py` unescaped, and `return path` — which was green before this case existed — fails all three parametrisations. The `cmd.exe` behaviour itself is **read**, from Python's own documentation; there is no Windows here | 2026-09-10 | **The tidy-up is reading `os.name` inside the body**, which is what shipped and which is exactly `docs/flow.md` #103's class made out of the fix for it: the branch runs on CI's `windows-latest` leg and nothing asks what it produced, so the whole unit could be replaced by `return path` with every case green on every leg. **The second is claiming the quoting is complete.** `cmd.exe` expands `%VAR%` inside double quotes, so a path holding percent signs is still mangled; the docstring names it rather than closing it, because the fix for that is not quoting |

### 1789034970-the-contract-is-settled-against-the-agents-that-exist

<!-- One work item's rows. No header — `fold_ledger.py` writes the `###` at
the release and moves this file into `seal/ledger.md`.

Three rows over a branch that rewrites three sections of one file. Most of
what this work item claims is claimed by a pin instead: the sections say what
they say, and `tests/test_the_agent_contract_holds_the_universal_rules.py`,
`tests/test_broad_gate_rule.py` and
`tests/test_the_seal_is_taken_once_by_the_sealer.py` are where that is
enforced. What earns a row here is a judgment a pin cannot carry — why §2
names an agent at all when the file's own opening forbids per-role rules (R2),

this branch inherited by removing somebody else's row (R1).

R1 replaces S8 of
`seal/ledger/1789002694-two-agents-are-forbidden-the-seal-and-nobody-is-assigned-it.md`.

a row whose anchor a change removes is REMOVED rather than re-pointed. -->

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 · `agents/sealer.md` names **both** of its permissions in its own file and apologises for neither: the one write, which §6 now says a definition names rather than excepts, and the one run, which §2 leaves each definition to assign. The window paragraph — *this file is the narrower document and the contract is the wider one* — is gone, and the case that pinned it asserts its absence rather than having been deleted with it | `` `agents/sealer.md#"## The one write, and why it is yours"@c17a938a` ``, `` `agents/sealer.md#"## The one run, and why it is yours"@1fd9352f` ``, `tests/test_the_seal_is_taken_once_by_the_sealer.py#test_the_sealer_names_the_one_write_its_definition_is_allowed@aa82091d`, `tests/test_the_seal_is_taken_once_by_the_sealer.py#test_the_window_the_sealer_shipped_under_is_closed@ed9e3fc7` | **Executed** 2026-09-10 in phase 3. `test_the_window_the_sealer_shipped_under_is_closed` was run against `agents/sealer.md` as it stood at `8204533` — the window section still in place — and failed; it passes at the working tree. `test_the_sealer_names_the_one_write_its_definition_is_allowed` passed against both, which is honest and is why it is recorded that way: only its name and its grounds changed, and its assertions are the ones S8 already carried. 350 cases green across the eight modules phase 3 touches | 2026-09-11 | **Successor to S8**, removed from work item 1789002694's fragment by this branch. **Deleting the window is not enough on its own.** §2 as rewritten says the assignment lives in the definition rather than in the contract, so a sealer definition that only stopped apologising would leave §2's pointer naming a file that does not answer — which is #30's opening restated one release later. `## The one run, and why it is yours` is what closes that, and `spawned for exactly that` is what the case holds it to.  **Re-read 2026-09-10, round 1's fix pass.** Finding 10 corrected §*The one write*'s recitation of §6's withheld acts — it dropped `post` and added `commit`, and now names the four §6 actually withholds — which moved that section's hash. The claim above is untouched. `spawned for exactly that` also became load-bearing twice over: `test_only_one_definition_assigns_the_broad_gate` counts the definitions carrying it, so §2's `One` is checked rather than asserted. **Re-read 2026-09-11 in work item 1789081272 (#84), phase 2.** The section's count of the definitions that stay silent moved from `three` to `four` when the fifth definition landed, which is the only edit to it. Both permissions are still named, the window paragraph is still gone, and `spawned for exactly that` is verbatim — the claim holds. The count is now derived rather than typed: `test_the_sealer_counts_the_silent_definitions_from_the_glob` reads it off the same glob and the same marker |
| R2 · §2 names one agent, and that is not the per-role exception this file's opening refuses. The **rule** is universal — *Whether it is yours is what your own definition says* — and the naming is a fact about where the one assignment currently lives, stated so the section is not a prohibition with nobody in the room to perform it. The guard is an ABSENCE, scoped to §2's body: the word `orchestrator` must not occur in it | `` `skills/agent-contract/SKILL.md#"## §2 Narrow and often; the broad gate is one act with one owner"@792ad5a1` ``, `tests/test_broad_gate_rule.py#test_the_prohibition_itself_has_one_home_and_it_is_the_contract@74eae03b`, `tests/test_broad_gate_rule.py#contract_section@70f2657a` | **Executed** 2026-09-10 in phase 1: the re-pointed case red against the contract as it stood at `23ca89c` (`bin/test` exit 1, five failures with §2's and §7's moved pins), green at `63d013d`. **Read**: the 15-word duplication margin re-measured after every phase — the widest run any definition shares with any section is 10 words, `agents/smith.md` against §8, unchanged from the base | 2026-09-10 | **Why an absence and not a presence.** A contract naming both owners is the state `test_the_definition_names_the_sealer_as_the_suites_owner` already refuses one file over, and a presence check cannot see it. **Why the body and not the file.** `orchestrator` is legitimate in the contract's opening paragraph, about the prompt a round arrives in; a whole-file absence check would either fail on that sentence or be dropped as over-strict, and a dropped check is worse than a scoped one. `contract_section` is cited because the scoping is the claim |
| R3 · §7's heading is **untouched** while its body widens, and the reason is an anchor rather than taste. `seal/ledger.md`'s row L7 anchors on that heading; a heading is the major level of a content anchor, so rewording it BREAKS the row where a body edit only drifts it — and this repository removes a row whose anchor a change removes rather than re-pointing it. The file form the heading states is still the rule for the common case, so nothing is lost by the widened sentence living below it | `` `skills/agent-contract/SKILL.md#"## §7 A probe is named `test_tmp_*`, one file, run once, deleted"@a677a411` `` | **Read** 2026-09-10 in phase 2: `bin/evidence-check .` reports this anchor DRIFTED — *content changed at 165-190 — re-verify* — and not BROKEN, which is the whole of the claim. **Executed**: the moved §7 pin red against the old body in phase 1's run, green at `94a2451` | 2026-09-10 | The alternative was to reword the heading so a reader skimming headings meets the widened rule. It costs row L7, whose own claim (*the probe rules and the git-from-Python rule are contract §7 and §8*) is still true and would have had to be removed and rewritten here to say the same thing. **This is worth a row because the repair looks obvious from outside**: the next session that reads §7 will see a heading narrower than its body and reach for the edit that breaks L7.  **Re-read 2026-09-10, round 1's fix pass.** Finding 7 added the ownership clause to §7's body — what the probe made for itself is what goes — so the hash moved again and the heading did not. The claim is unchanged, and the heading is now two paragraphs narrower than its body rather than one, which sharpens the hazard this row exists to name |
